# RULINGS — every divergence, absence and unproven equivalence, in plain words, for Germano

**DRAFT for Gate B (2026-09-02).** Assembled mechanically from `RULINGS-holo1-draft.md` and `RULINGS-holo2-draft.md` (the W2 and W1 workflow outputs); no entry text was rewritten. The index below orders entries by what they can change — certificates first — then by whether the row is DIVERGED/ABSENT (a real difference) or CARRIED-DIFFERENT with UNPROVEN equivalence (a difference nobody has measured). Each entry ends with a blank **Ruling:** line. Rulings recorded inline here are the record; see `README.md` for the gate log and `claude-plan.md` §7 for what each outcome (keep holo3's / port now / archive for later port / accept the loss / costed measurement) sets in motion.

Entries: holo1 65 · holo2 64 · total 129.

# holo1 entries

### `fields-discovery-continuation-operator-15` — Discovery-first receive: receiver-wide orchestration over isolated exact-row branches

**Ruling (given): claude-plan.md §12 item 3 (Germano, 2026-09-02): cold format discovery — archive for later port / re-implementation. The same route is open to any other capability the maps show worth keeping.**

Brief: `PORT-BRIEFS/cold-format-discovery.md`.

WHAT IT DOES. Lets a decode start without being told the printed format, by running one isolated branch per candidate row until exactly one certifies.
V1. The DEFAULT CLI entry when neither --coding nor --format is given and no exact state exists (src/holo/cli.py:456-473). It resolves the registry's complete row domain, optionally narrowed by --printer, computes one cross-row placement envelope so every branch searches a shared hypothesis space, and creates one isolated exact-row session per candidate (src/holo/receive/discovery.py:59-99, :147-175, :221-252). Each branch then runs the unchanged pipeline under the ordinary flag bounds; the root persists only the policy id, the requested rows, the envelope, the authenticated capture facts, the native cell vote and the branches.
HOLO3. Absent. The format identity is required at the command line and resolved once (src/holo/receiver/cli.py:50-58); the engine builds one store bound to one format row.
DIAL. v1: the default entry when no format is named. holo3: not reachable — the identity is required at the command line.
EVIDENCE. None, in either direction: there is no yield or cost number anywhere for a discovery-first entry against a --format-told one (ARTEFACTS.md G-14). 860 lines plus a 395-line state module, cited by no numbered conversion spec.
YIELD. None once the format is named: this decides whether a decode can START, not what certifies. Its cost is N branches of survey and cold placement, and no artefact has ever measured it.
OPTIONS. (a) Port now. (b) Archive for later port. (c) Accept the loss.
RECOMMENDATION. Archive for later port (b) — already ruled. The capability is real and unreproducible from anything holo3 has, but it costs nothing in certificates today: every archived volume is decoded with its identity named, and the fossil catalogue means a sheet's identity is recoverable by hand. Freeze the brief below before Gate E so the port is a build, not an archaeology.

### `refinement-18` — The refinement dial: --refine / --mopup plan-prefix bound vs always-queued rows

**Ruling (given): claude-plan.md §12 item 4 (Germano, 2026-09-02): the `--mopup` / `--refine` operator bounds — accepted as lost; they bound spend, not yield.**

WHAT IT DOES. Decides whether refinement runs at all in a given invocation.
BOTH SIDES. v1 makes the invocation a plan prefix: the default stops before the first mop-up level, --mopup before the first refinement or
supplemental level, --refine runs the whole plan (src/holo/receive/runtime.py:251-280, flags at src/holo/cli.py:950 and :954). --refine is also the
only stock activation of the plane-fields arm (docs/RECEIVE-DESIGN.md:1729-1730). holo3 has no refinement flag: refine, refine.color and revisit are
ordinary descriptor rows priced at 200 that drain when cheaper work is exhausted (src/holo/receiver/cli.py:23-41, src/holo/receiver/plugins/refine.py:663-681).
EVIDENCE. None needed. The campaign's comparison method already neutralises the difference by running v1 at full harvest against holo3's default
(claude-plan.md section 6, W2). Spec 08 section 7 records v1's form as a regression in its own right — putting refinement behind a flag cost flagless
runs their certificates.
OPTIONS. Already ruled — accept the loss (claude-plan.md section 12 item 4: the operator bounds bound spend, not yield).
RECOMMENDATION. Keep the existing ruling; do not reopen. One correction to carry into the map: the v1 flag path is src/holo/cli.py, not
src/holo/receive/cli.py, in both the row and the module list.

### `scheduling-09` — Invocation reach as a flag-bounded plan prefix (--mopup / --refine) vs unconditionally queued rows

**Ruling (given): claude-plan.md §12 item 4 (Germano, 2026-09-02): the `--mopup` / `--refine` operator bounds — accepted as lost; they bound spend, not yield.**

1. What it does: decides how much of the receiver's registered work a single invocation is allowed to attempt.
2. What v1 does: `_plan_bound` turns the operator flags into a plan-prefix length - default stops before the first mop-up entry, `--mopup` before the first refinement or supplemental entry, `--refine` runs the whole plan (src/holo/receive/runtime.py:251-280, :301-304; help text at src/holo/cli.py:951-959). A bounded run legally ends open with parked lane families and is resumable; committed prefixes are always honoured.
3. What holo3 does: every row is in DESCRIPTORS on every run (src/holo/receiver/engine.py:57-75). Revisit, refine, refine.color, the tiles rescue rows and tiles.uplift are always queued and drain by price. There is no plan-prefix bound of any kind.
4. Correction to the row as written: holo3 has THREE reach dials, not two - `--plane-fields`, `--max-pages`, and `--no-pooled-color-context` (src/holo/receiver/cli.py:35-36, store.py:280-281, plugins/color_pooled.py:167-173), the last of which stops a whole row being born. `--complete` and `--no-close` are close dials and belong to scheduling-17.
5. The evidence: v1's supplemental tail costs 0% to +31% of the archived ladder and buys 24 certificates across six volumes (0/2/6/15/1/0) - ARTEFACTS.md §2.9 -> docs/COLOR-UPLIFT-MEASUREMENTS.md:3172-3177. That is the whole of what the bound was bounding.
6. Yield: operational. The bound limits spend within one invocation; the work it defers is resumable, so no tile is lost by it.
7. Two consequences worth keeping in the map even though the row is already ruled: this row is why the campaign's comparison method is v1 `--mopup --refine --complete` against holo3 `--complete`; and with the bound gone, holo3's default run is v1's most expensive configuration, which is what the 2.7-4.4x crop wall ratios and the 39-60 GiB peaks are measured against.
8. Options: keep holo3's; port a bound; archive; accept the loss; or a costed measurement.
9. Recommendation: no change - already ruled (claude-plan.md §12 item 4, the operator bounds accepted as lost because they bound spend, not yield). Record only, with the dial list corrected.

---

## 1. K placement

### `k-placement-03` — Where the observation-window lattice is laid: per-lane pixel frame vs one shared cell frame

*Classification DIVERGED · yield decides-certificates · map: `HOLO1-MAP.md` → `k-placement-03`*

What it does: decides where each rung's observation windows sit, and therefore whether a coarse rung's accepted pose can serve the fine-rung window on the same paper.
v1: the survey runs once per ordinary lane, so each rung has its own region boxes, and the 200/40 window lattice is laid in that lane's crop pixels (src/holo/receive/placement/geometry.py:487-503, src/holo/receive/production.py:149-166). The rungs tile the page differently; cross-rung help only travels the separate lower-hint route.
holo3: the survey runs once per capture at native resolution and the cell grid comes from the region's native extent over the declared pitch, so every rung lays the identical cell lattice (src/holo/receiver/plugins/place.py:164-221, src/holo/receiver/store.py:536-560).
Consequence: in holo3 a colocated coarser-rung donor sits at distance 0.0 and is always consulted first (src/holo/receiver/plugins/place.py:344-360); v1 has to pay a separate hinted route with its own budget.
Evidence: holo2 measured the same change and found window extents identical at every footing (314/315 common) where v1's accepted page-cell origins were essentially disjoint across lanes, 1 of 36 common between 2 and 4 px/cell (holo2/docs/kparity/K-PARITY-CATALOG-draft.md:107-123).
Whole-receiver cover: e029 HP K holo1 1940 / holo3 1940, matched 1940, no misses (src/holo/receiver/docs/conversion_docs/PARITY.md:29-41); xerox K 1930 / 1924, 8 misses, 2 extras, 5 of the 8 attributed to a missing refinement lane (PARITY.md:206-237, GAPS.md:82-160).
Yield: decides-certificates. It is the structural root of rows 17, 20 and 31.
Note: holo2's own default was to build v1-faithful unless a gating measurement showed the shared lattice dominates. That measurement was never run; holo3 shipped the shared lattice and the parity numbers are the closest thing to the missing gate.
Options: keep holo3's; or accept the loss of the never-run gate explicitly.
Recommend: keep holo3's. The one measured comparison is favourable and the alternative is a rewrite of the whole placement geometry.

**Ruling:**

### `k-placement-04` — Window admission at layout time (support floor)

*Classification DIVERGED · yield decides-certificates · map: `HOLO1-MAP.md` → `k-placement-04`*

What it does: decides which observation windows are created at all, before any pixels are read.
v1: estimates each window's supported cells from one globally phased quarter-resolution mask through a summed-area table and drops the window when the estimate is under 30, or when the clipped cell shape has an axis under 8 (src/holo/receive/placement/geometry.py:513-560). Survivors are ordered richest-support-first.
holo3: counts supported cells exactly on the region's support grid and drops the window only when the count is zero; the 8-cell floor became a per-axis test on the region (src/holo/receiver/plugins/place.py:195-221, src/holo/receiver/lib/geometry.py:67-78). The 30-site floor still applies, but at fire time on the sampled mask (src/holo/receiver/plugins/place.py:706-707).
Direction: permissive. Every window v1 lays out, holo3 lays out too, plus the sparse ones, so this cannot cost a certificate directly.
What it does cost: an admitted sparse window pays a full crop, box filter, spectral fit and phase fit before it dies (src/holo/receiver/plugins/place.py:694-707), and while alive it may publish a lattice seed for its region (src/holo/receiver/plugins/pose.py:500-503). Under best-supported-wins a low-support seed rarely wins and is superseded with a wake, so the second-order risk is small but not nil.
Evidence: no artefact. The number the map cites (193 and 131 cells) is a misreading of holo2/src/holo2/solve/DESIGN.md:333-340, which is about clipped window SIZES against a 70-cell floor, not about the 30-support drop; and holo2's clipped lattice is not v1's flush one anyway. ARTEFACTS.md 2.1 carries the same misreading.
Gap G-13 stands: place_survey has zero measured yield anywhere.
Yield: decides-certificates in kind (permissive direction only).
Options: keep holo3's; or a costed measurement — lay out one archived capture's regions under both rules and count the windows and their exact support. Pure geometry, no decode, minutes.
Recommend: keep holo3's, correct the cited evidence, and drop the 'dies at the first sample' cost claim from the map.

**Ruling:**

### `k-placement-06` — Which pitch the region aperture and cell grid are built from

*Classification DIVERGED · yield decides-certificates · map: `HOLO1-MAP.md` → `k-placement-06`*

What it does: chooses the pitch the region aperture and the cell grid are built from, which fixes the pixel geometry of every window crop and every cell read.
v1: uses the survey's measured platen-wide median pitch, falling back to the declared raster only when the survey measured none (src/holo/receive/place.py:911-913, src/holo/receive/placement/geometry.py:487-494).
holo3: always the declared printed pitch in exact rational arithmetic, Fraction(dpi) * row.pitch_in (src/holo/receiver/plugins/place.py:164-192, src/holo/receiver/store.py:547-549). The literal 600 has left the receive path.
The two agree to the scanner's scale error (Canon +0.3-0.6%), which the per-window affine absorbs; they diverge when the survey's vote is wrong or absent (row 07).
v1 argues holo3's side against itself: its own lattice.py note calls the measurement's affordability a workplan residue and says placement rides the declared-pitch fallback (src/holo/receive/lattice.py:159-163). The house rule is the same: page-derived pitch estimators fit channel wander as scale, 5.92 measured against 6.00 true.
Correction to the map: the measured pitch is not unused in holo3. It still refines region components and scales the support-hole fill (src/holo/receiver/plugins/survey.py:654-676), and that mask decides which windows exist and how much support each has.
Evidence: no artefact isolates the aperture-pitch source on either side. The whole-receiver K parity of row 03 is the only cover.
Yield: decides-certificates.
Options: keep holo3's; or a costed measurement (recompute one archived capture's region frames under both pitch sources and diff the cell grids — pure geometry, no decode).
Recommend: keep holo3's; it is the house rule and v1's own comment agrees.

**Ruling:**

### `k-placement-07` — The declared-vs-voted pitch refusal, and what happens when no pitch is voted

*Classification DIVERGED · yield decides-certificates · map: `HOLO1-MAP.md` → `k-placement-07`*

What it does: the survey votes on the printed cell pitch; that vote decides whether the capture is accepted as this format and whether it produces any windows.
v1: snaps each component's measured pitch to an integer cell_dots in 1..12 at 4% tolerance and takes the plurality; a tie returns no vote. A vote that contradicts the declared row raises FormatHypothesisRejected, which the discovery receiver catches as one row's refusal. With NO vote, placement proceeds on the declared fallback pitch (src/holo/receive/survey.py:330-380, src/holo/receive/place.py:906-913).
holo3: snaps to four printed pitches (1/600, 1/300, 1/200, 1/150 in) plus the declared one (src/holo/receiver/plugins/survey.py:38-39, 283-298). A contradicting vote raises ValueError from inside survey_capture and aborts the whole run (survey.py:700-710; nothing catches it). No vote or a tie gives pitch_px None (survey.py:398-409), and the store then builds no frames for that capture at all (src/holo/receiver/store.py:540-541) - zero windows.
The yield-bearing half is the no-vote branch, and it guards nothing: holo3's frame geometry does not use the measured pitch (row 06), and the only other consumers of CaptureSurvey.pitch_px in the receive path are this guard and the diagnostic report.
Where it bites: a confetti or fragment capture with few components, or a sheet at an off-catalogue pitch. Both give a tie or no snap, and both now cost an entire capture.
Evidence: no artefact - gap G-08 records that the declared-vs-voted pitch row has no measured number on any of the three sides.
Yield: decides-certificates, and at the coarsest possible grain (a whole capture).
Options: keep holo3's; accept the loss; or port now - build frames on the declared pitch, keep the contradiction refusal, and downgrade the no-vote case to a warning.
Cost of the port: small and self-contained (one branch in store._fold_survey plus a warning event), qualified by a holo3 sim-lane test at a capture with no votable component. No decode, no hardware.
Recommend: port now. This is the only mechanism in the family that can silently lose a whole capture, and it protects nothing holo3 uses.

**Ruling:**

### `k-placement-13` — Word-route admission: first-hit-wins vs the whole-word cross-page rival bar

*Classification DIVERGED · yield decides-certificates · map: `HOLO1-MAP.md` → `k-placement-13`*

What it does: decides whether a decoded, gate-passing address word is accepted as this window's placement.
v1: first hit wins. Candidates are walked in error order and the first one whose page is in the domain and which passes the coupled parity gates is returned at once; there is no rival test on this route (src/holo/receive/place.py:394-449). Its own comment states the doctrine - a word is one-way evidence and never vetoes the independent correlation lane.
holo3: the first passing candidate becomes the winner and then must clear a whole-word rival bar; rivals are the other decoded candidates (deduped, and same-page near-neighbours within 3 cells excluded) plus the winner's own origin and rotation on every possible page. The bar is rival mismatch above 0.20 with a margin of at least 0.08 (src/holo/receiver/plugins/place.py:447-489, src/holo/receiver/plugins/words.py:299-327).
Failing the bar refuses the whole word pass, not just that candidate (src/holo/receiver/plugins/place.py:485-487).
Why the bar exists: a full-domain cross-page constant-XOR alias concentrates its difference codeword into 8 of 45 search-class bits, giving a weighted rival mismatch of 0.1415 - certain rather than unlucky at a 312-page domain, and fixed by whole-word scoring rather than by raising bars (src/holo/receiver/docs/conversion_docs/01-bch.md:363-365; the same argument is v1's own note at src/holo/receive/placement/search.py:38-46).
Evidence: aggregate only. e029 HP K 1940/1940 with no misses (src/holo/receiver/docs/conversion_docs/PARITY.md:29-41); xerox K 1930/1924, 8 misses and 2 extras, later 1927 with 5 misses (PARITY.md:206-237, :520-546). Nothing attributes a single miss to this bar. v1, which has no bar, audited 69,066,304 cells with zero wrong certificates.
Yield: decides-certificates. A bar that refuses what v1 admits is exactly the class that needs a ruling.
Cost: 133 extra whole-word scores per word admission on the default domain.
Options: keep holo3's; accept the loss of v1's admit-everything word route; or a cheap measurement - count rival-bar refusals as a diagnostic event on an existing K decode (an events counter, no new receiver run).
Recommend: keep holo3's. It closes a hole v1 left open on a route that searches the same complete page domain, and the alias it guards is argued-certain at full domain. Buy the refusal counter if isolation is wanted.

**Ruling:**

### `k-placement-15` — Cold page domain and the support floor it prices

*Classification DIVERGED · yield decides-certificates · map: `HOLO1-MAP.md` → `k-placement-15`*

What it does: decides which pages a cold correlation sweeps and at what support floor. The floor is a pre-FFT eligibility mask, so it decides which origins can be found at all, not merely how fast.
v1: one cold domain frozen for the run - every page of the row unless --page narrows it - and one floor priced on that whole domain, about 54-55 sites per parity on a full A4 domain. One sweep per window covers every page (src/holo/receive/production.py:621-626, src/holo/receive/placement/policy.py:220-226, src/holo/receive/place.py:1450-1512).
holo3: two cold flavours partitioning the possible set - live pages (those already holding a committed pose) and the rest - each pricing its floor from its own partition and de-duplicating its own swept pages (src/holo/receiver/plugins/place.py:117-125, 748-768, src/holo/receiver/plugins/place_sched.py:95-101).
Floors are an address-space constant: 43 sites at one page, 48 at ten, 54 at 133 on a 53x37 matrix (src/holo/receiver/docs/PAGE-DOMAIN.md:114-139, pinned at src/holo/receiver/tests/test_h3_plugins_placement.py:598-625).
So the live route admits origins v1's single floor excludes. That is yield, not only cost. The accepted counterpart: a thin window never rest-places into a page nothing has opened yet; it waits for a word to make the page live and is then re-armed by the live-pages wake.
Evidence: on e029, the same tree at the default 133-page domain read 1,356 certificates in 1,639.8 cpu-seconds against the single-page null's 1,360 in 2,557.4 - one certificate for 35% of the CPU, all three arms VERIFIED e5021f8d, 465,580 B (scratch/page-domain-qual/*.log, ARTEFACTS 2.12).
v1's own boundary number: a cold at a single-page domain costs 1-3 s per patch, and widening pays decisively only at large domains, 30-48 s per avoided cold (docs/PLACEMENT-IMPROVEMENTS.md:325-331).
Yield: decides-certificates.
Options: keep holo3's; or accept the loss of v1's single uniform floor and record that the live floor is the permissive half.
Recommend: keep holo3's. The qualification arm is measured, verified byte-exact, and the cheaper floor is the yield-positive direction.

**Ruling:**

### `k-placement-16` — The page domain itself: static declared set vs dynamic live set over a possible set

*Classification DIVERGED · yield decides-certificates · map: `HOLO1-MAP.md` → `k-placement-16`*

What it does: defines the page address space - which pages a word may claim, which pages exist to be swept, and how the search is priced.
v1: the domain is every legal page by default, or exactly the pages named with --page, frozen for the invocation and identical for every route; every legal page is equally live from the first instant (src/holo/receive/production.py:621-626, src/holo/cli.py:930-934).
holo3: two classes. The possible set is range(row.max_pages), narrowable with --max-pages, which the CLI refuses to raise above the row (src/holo/receiver/store.py:77-79, src/holo/receiver/cli.py:87-93). The live set seeds at page 0 and grows whenever a placement folds on a new page, waking every place worker (src/holo/receiver/store.py:97, 672-676).
Word admission accepts any possible page, so the word route is the page-opener; the cold splits live from rest (row 15); per-page state is built on first touch.
Evidence: e029 at the default domain is 35% cheaper in CPU and 36% fewer jobs for one fewer certificate, VERIFIED byte-exact (scratch/page-domain-qual/*.log). Sheet J, the only real fragment material on record, read 357 certificates at the single-page default and 657 on a wider-domain resume, close OPEN both times (scratch/J-run.52093, scratch/J-run.54028) - evidence that fragment yield lives in a multi-page domain, but a resume, so it mixes more pages with more work.
v1 side: a scrap-corpus page domain is {0} and a scrap-only session cannot reach K, so neither arm closes (docs/PAYLOAD-MEASUREMENTS.md:21-26).
Yield: decides-certificates.
The honest cost: because floors read the live set at fire time, certificate SETS may now differ across worker counts. src/holo/receiver/docs/PAGE-DOMAIN.md:175-182 keeps zero-wrong and payload byte-identity and explicitly drops certificate-set identity across worker counts. v1 had that identity by construction.
Options: keep holo3's; accept the loss of cross-worker certificate-set determinism explicitly; or a costed measurement - a fragment sheet decoded from cold at both domains without a resume, to isolate the pages term (a real receiver run, minutes on holo3).
Recommend: keep holo3's, and record the determinism narrowing as a named, accepted consequence rather than leaving it in a design doc.

**Ruling:**

### `k-placement-18` — The two-loser ledger (runner-up hints)

*Classification ABSENT · yield decides-certificates · map: `HOLO1-MAP.md` → `k-placement-18` · brief: `PORT-BRIEFS/two-loser-ledger.md`*

What it does: keeps a refusing window's best near-miss as continuation geometry, so a later, finer or mopped-up window can start from it.
v1: a window that refuses records its best candidate as a runner-up hint; the region's ledger is ranked by held-out mismatch, then search mismatch, then support, and capped at two per region. Retained losers join the accepted hints as continuation geometry for later lanes and for the mop-up level, with two of the eight portfolio slots reserved for them (src/holo/receive/place.py:92-93, 306-343, 147-182, 1101-1132).
holo3: absent. A refused window writes nothing durable at all; only its lattice-seed proposal survives (src/holo/receiver/plugins/pose.py:112-118, src/holo/receiver/plugins/place.py:437-439). src/holo/receiver/docs/PAGE-DOMAIN.md section 10 records the drop, calling runner-ups computed and dropped.
Evidence, and it is against the mechanism on dense sheets: v1's loser ledger holds 2 losers on e029, 5 on xerox and 73 on the synthetic demo9, and every real-sheet loser anchors ground that was already certified (holo2/docs/kparity/K-PARITY-STATE.md:69-75).
holo2 reached the same place: its catalogue row 1.8 calls the effect unmeasured and made building it conditional on a non-zero inherited-hint count on the two archived states - a gate that was never met.
Yield: decides-certificates in kind, on material the record barely covers. The 73 on the synthetic sheet say this is a sparse-sheet and fragment tool.
Options: keep holo3's (accept the loss); port now; or archive for later port with a brief.
Recommend: archive for later port. It earns nothing measurable on any archived dense volume, so it must not hold up retirement; but sheet J and the confetti class are the one place it could pay, and holo3's shape makes the re-implementation small.

**Ruling:**

### `k-placement-22` — What happens to a window whose straddle audit refuses

*Classification DIVERGED · yield decides-certificates · map: `HOLO1-MAP.md` → `k-placement-22`*

What it does: decides what becomes of a window that found an address it could not audit.
v1: straight to subdivision, then dead. The refusal is recorded, the four corner children are pushed, and the window itself is gone - it had already been removed from the pending and deferred sets, so it never returns to any route (src/holo/receive/place.py:1155-1183, 1053-1061).
holo3: both. The refusal is in the direct-subdivision set, so the children are born immediately, and the parent is also re-pushed onto the frontier so it can climb to any unspent stage - an un-decoded word stage, the wide tier, or its cold pair. Only when no stage is left does it retire (src/holo/receiver/store.py:610-612, src/holo/receiver/engine.py:564-576).
So holo3 is a strict superset of both predecessors here: it quarters like v1 and re-arms like holo2.
Direction: permissive. A refused window can only gain attempts, so this cannot cost a certificate; it can cost passes on a window whose geometry is genuinely wrong.
Evidence: no artefact. Gap G-08 records that this refusal path has no measured number on any of the three sides.
Yield: decides-certificates in kind (permissive direction only).
Worth naming: the plan's own seed row believed holo3 followed v1's rule here. It does not, and the map should say so plainly.
Options: keep holo3's; or a costed measurement - count, from an existing decode's events, how many windows fired again after an audit refusal and how many of those ever placed (a counter, no new run).
Recommend: keep holo3's. The direction is safe and the cost is bounded by the ladder.

**Ruling:**

### `k-placement-29` — Pilot-observation harvest, the known-position word firewall, and placement quarantine

*Classification ABSENT · yield integrity · map: `HOLO1-MAP.md` → `k-placement-29`*

What it does: pools every accepted placement's read of each printed address word, decodes the pooled read with erasures, and marks a contradiction when it disagrees with the expected counter. Contributing placements are quarantined - their hints are dropped and the session refuses payload evidence that came from them.
v1: builds one pilot observation per audited word site on every accepted, non-redundant placement, pools the strongest read per site once per level, and quarantines contributors on a contradiction (src/holo/receive/placement/search.py:1063-1099, src/holo/receive/words.py:329-390, src/holo/receive/_bch_quarantine.py, src/holo/receive/session.py:265-279, 437-466).
holo3: absent as a gate. The audited core's word-site coverage and the winning word are emitted as events and read only by the diagnostic model (src/holo/receiver/plugins/place.py:639-669, 834-847). No pooling, no cross-placement contradiction test, no quarantine, no payload exclusion. The contract records the drop (00-contract 07-6).
Evidence, and it is unusually good for accepting the loss: the mechanism has never fired on any corpus, on either receiver.
  - v1's zero-contradiction invariant: 70 of 70 regions, all 925 pilot tiles, ZERO contradictory reads across about 1,780 attempted known-position decodes (docs/RECEIVE-DESIGN-EVIDENCE.md:492).
  - holo2 measured it inert on its whole corpus: 0 contradictions and 0 quarantined poses or pieces across 21,079 and 21,508 evidence records (holo2/docs/kparity/K-PARITY-CATALOG-draft.md:286-303).
  - v1's campaign audit: 0 wrong certificates in 69,066,304 cells. holo3's crop batteries: zero wrong on both sides across 285 target addresses.
Yield: integrity, not certificates. It has never removed or added a tile.
What is actually lost: the ability to notice a contradiction, not protection against one. A certificate in holo3 carries its own CRC and syndrome, so cells from a contradicted placement cannot mint a wrong tile - they can only fail to mint.
Options: accept the loss; archive for later port; or keep a diagnostic-only version (pool the word reads, emit a contradiction event, quarantine nothing).
Recommend: accept the loss, and record the argument above rather than just the verdict. If Germano wants the alarm back, the diagnostic-only version is the cheap form and needs no gate.

**Ruling:**

### `k-placement-30` — Redundant-placement suppression (observed-sites dedup) and who may be an anchor

*Classification ABSENT · yield decides-certificates · map: `HOLO1-MAP.md` → `k-placement-30`*

What it does: marks a second placement that covers no new sites as redundant - it harvests no word observations, does not count toward the region, and, the load-bearing part, is never added to the anchor list, so it can never seed a neighbour's chain prediction.
v1: keeps an observed-sites set per region and applies exactly that rule (src/holo/receive/place.py:466-530, 1210-1215).
holo3: absent. Every committed placement of the region is an anchor at every level, with no redundancy or component concept (src/holo/receiver/plugins/place_sched.py:62-75). Per-cell ownership is resolved downstream instead - one owner per capture, page and cell - rather than by suppressing the placement.
Direction: mostly permissive (more anchors, more seeds).
The one way it could cost, which the map does not name: holo3 keeps only the 8 nearest anchors (src/holo/receiver/plugins/place.py:344-360), so near-duplicate placements over the same paper can crowd a farther but geometrically distinct donor out of the consult.
Evidence: holo2 measured the 8-slot cap never binding - the truncation bit set on 0 of 60 passes, per-pass maximum 5, median 2 (holo2/docs/MEASUREMENTS.md:1648-1651). That is holo2, one hop from holo3, but it is the only number that bears on the crowding risk. holo2's own catalogue row 1.13 expected no yield loss in either direction and gated the change on a multiplicity check that was never run.
The mechanism's other consumer, the word firewall's input population, is itself gone (row 29).
Yield: decides-certificates in kind; permissive in direction, with a bounded crowding risk that the one available measurement says never materialised.
Options: accept the loss; or a costed measurement - count, on an existing decode, the per-pass consult truncations and how many kept seeds were near-duplicates (a counter, no new run).
Recommend: accept the loss, and record the crowding risk with holo2's truncation number beside it so the acceptance is on the record rather than by omission. The payload reader should confirm nothing downstream needs v1's component field.

**Ruling:**

### `k-placement-33` — The small-domain cold-first price inversion (cold_first_max_pages)

*Classification ABSENT · yield decides-order-only · map: `HOLO1-MAP.md` → `k-placement-33`*

What it does: on a small page domain, decides whether the remaining-slot word sweep or the cold correlation runs first.
v1: a switch. At or below one declared page, a cold sweep prices below a remaining-slot word sweep, so the cold runs first and a refused window still rest-decodes before giving up; above it, rest runs first and cold is the fallback. Either way both routes run before a window gives up (src/holo/receive/placement/model.py:308-314, src/holo/receive/place.py:1630-1653, 1489-1497).
holo3: no inversion. Remaining-slot word is priced at 80 and the cold pair at 250 and 260, unconditionally (src/holo/receiver/plugins/place.py:41-48). The contract records the drop and says the small-domain economy is now expressed as the live-pages prior (00-contract 07-7).
That replacement is real: on a word-failed sheet the first cold sees a one-page live partition and its cheaper floor of 43 sites rather than a full 133-page sweep at 54 (row 15).
Evidence: v1's own boundary number - about 75 word-dead windows each paying about 2.8 s of eight-slot decode ahead of a cold costing 1-3 s at single-page domain, with widening paying decisively only at large domains, 30-48 s per avoided cold (docs/PLACEMENT-IMPROVEMENTS.md:325-341). On e029 at the default 133-page domain, holo3 read 1,356 certificates in 1,639.8 cpu-seconds, VERIFIED byte-exact (scratch/page-domain-qual/*.log).
Yield: decides-order-only. Both orders still run both routes, so this can only cost CPU.
Exposure: on a genuine single-page decode holo3 spends about 2.8 s per word-dead window of rest decode it might have skipped, against a cold that now runs at the live floor.
The archived default domain is 133 pages, where v1 itself takes the rest-first branch - so on the corpus the two receivers already agree.
Options: accept the loss; or re-introduce the inversion as a price rule conditional on the live-page count.
Recommend: accept the loss. It buys CPU on a case the corpus does not contain, and the live-pages prior already carries the economy it existed for.

**Ruling:**

---

## 2. Payload decode, union, retry, intake

### `payload-union-retry-intake-04` — Never sum the same physical capture twice (the v0.27 fix and the duplicate-source refusals)

*Classification CARRIED-DIFFERENT · yield integrity · map: `HOLO1-MAP.md` → `payload-union-retry-intake-04`*

What it does: stops one physical scan being counted twice in a tile's decoder input.
v1: SCAN gets only this capture's blocks (src/holo/receive/production_payload.py:424-430), backed by two explicit refusals — a duplicate (source hash, lane) component in blocks_by_address (src/holo/receive/evidence_blocks.py:660-691) and a duplicate-source raise in the attempt view (src/holo/receive/evidence_blocks.py:719-721).
holo3: no assertion anywhere. assemble_union takes at most one level per capture (src/holo/receiver/plugins/tiles.py:291-301 with store.level_of_ppc, src/holo/receiver/store.py:700-704) and _combine sums those components (src/holo/receiver/plugins/tiles.py:332-341); intake dedups by file hash (src/holo/receiver/plugins/intake.py:223-233). The property holds by construction, not by a check.
Evidence: none either way — no artefact shows double counting on either receiver. The nearest thing is v1's multi-scan battery, where a duplicate re-ingest preserved state byte-for-byte (ARTEFACTS.md §2.6 -> docs/RECEIVE-DESIGN-EVIDENCE.md:499).
Yield: integrity, and not certificate-bearing on the archived corpus. Double counting could not mint a wrong certificate (the checksum gate stands) and no live path can produce it.
Options: keep holo3's; add a one-line assertion; port v1's two raises; accept the loss.
Recommendation: keep holo3's, plus the one-line assertion in _combine that the component capture ids are distinct. The first refuter checked every live caller of attempt_tile — local_ctx.py:488, plane_k.py:525, swim.py:433, uplift.py:248, tiles.py:628 — and all build components from cover_lanes, assemble_union or a single cut, so the assertion holds today on every path and would not break a rescue tier.
It converts a code-reading into a checked invariant at zero runtime cost, which is the cheapest settlement available and needs no measurement.

**Ruling:**

### `payload-union-retry-intake-05` — The multiview combination arithmetic (equal-weight per-capture LLR sum)

*Classification CARRIED-DIFFERENT · yield decides-certificates · map: `HOLO1-MAP.md` → `payload-union-retry-intake-05`*

What it does: the arithmetic that turns several scans of one tile into the numbers the error-correcting decoder sees — the multi-scan combining law the project has relied on since experiment 014.
v1: components sorted by (source hash, block id), each materialised as a 32-bit tensor (src/holo/receive/evidence_blocks.py:285-296) and accumulated into a 64-bit zero array (src/holo/receive/evidence_blocks.py:800-802). Equal weights, no normalisation, no clamp.
holo3: src/holo/receiver/plugins/tiles.py:332-341 zeroes a 64-bit (cells, arity) array plus an observed mask, walks components sorted by capture id, and scatter-adds each cut's 32-bit values at its observed indices. Same weights, same dtypes, same effective order (one component per capture).
Evidence: only the single-component case is pinned (src/holo/receiver/tests/test_h3_drift_receiver.py:928-986 passes one block on every call). The union route is holo3's largest paper yield — 1,818 of 4,237 certificates on sheet H (ARTEFACTS.md §2.6 -> sheetH-maxstack-report.json rows[3]) and 1,508 of 4,307 across captures (src/holo/receiver/docs/conversion_docs/MEASUREMENTS-2026-08-31.md:18-19).
Yield: decides-certificates. Every multi-capture tile on e029, xerox, sheet H and sheet I is decided by this sum.
Options: keep holo3's on the code-reading; run the costed measurement; port v1's dense form.
Recommendation: run the costed measurement, and run it before src/holo goes. Extend test_k_tile_attempts_match_the_v1_attempt_records to a two-block view built through v1's tile_attempt_view and assert the combined arrays agree element for element.
Add one assertion in the same test that no cut repeats a cell: holo3's in-place fancy-index add (src/holo/receiver/plugins/tiles.py:338) silently drops duplicates, and is correct only because the owner freeze guarantees one owner per cell.
Cost: a unit test in an existing lane, seconds, no capture and no receiver run — but it is only possible while v1 is still in the tree.

**Ruling:**

### `payload-union-retry-intake-06` — The 018.7 bounded-LLR invariant and the finiteness boundary

*Classification CARRIED-DIFFERENT · yield integrity · map: `HOLO1-MAP.md` → `payload-union-retry-intake-06`*

What it does: two numerical rules — never re-saturate a summed multi-scan value (the experiment-018.7 bounded-value invariant), and refuse a non-finite detector output rather than decoding it.
v1: the invariant is stated at src/holo/receive/tiles.py:28-33 and nowhere else (the design doc does not restate it); the finiteness refusal sits at the shared attempt boundary (src/holo/receive/evidence_blocks.py:727) and so covers black-and-white and both colour codecs.
holo3: the no-clamp half is carried exactly — nothing after src/holo/receiver/plugins/tiles.py:332-341 clamps. The finiteness refusal narrowed to black-and-white: src/holo/receiver/plugins/tiles.py:369-373 raises with v1's own wording, and the two colour bodies have no check at all.
Evidence: none — no artefact records a non-finite detector output on either receiver. The black-and-white paths are pinned against v1 on finite inputs (src/holo/receiver/tests/test_h3_drift_receiver.py:928, :990).
Yield: integrity. A colour detector emitting a non-number would reach the demapper silently; it cannot mint a wrong certificate, but it turns a diagnosable refusal into an unexplained failure, and colour is where the volumes that failed on paper sat.
Options: keep holo3's; port now; accept the loss.
Recommendation: port now. Move the finiteness check from the black-and-white body into _combine so all three families are covered by one refusal at the point where evidence becomes decoder input.
Carry the invariant's two sentences as a comment there at the same time: it is the most load-bearing numerical rule in the receiver and its only written home is a docstring that is about to be deleted.
Cost: a few lines and no measurement — the change only adds a refusal on inputs no artefact has ever produced.

**Ruling:**

### `payload-union-retry-intake-08` — Per-child support floors for the colour codecs (CMY7 pack-complete scatter, CMY8 per-plane active bits)

*Classification CARRIED-DIFFERENT · yield decides-certificates · map: `HOLO1-MAP.md` → `payload-union-retry-intake-08`*

What it does: decides whether a colour tile is handed to the expensive demapper at all — each error-correcting child must independently carry at least its own information bits of support.
v1: src/holo/receive/color_attempts.py:200-215 scatters pack-complete support through the real inverse interleave and re-applies the floor to the demapped values; :240-263 does the per-plane count. The floors are read off the stream profile's child objects, and the children are asserted to cover the whole bank (:212-213).
holo3: src/holo/receiver/plugins/tiles.py:128-145 recomputes the same floors from the format row's (rate, shorten) pairs, and src/holo/receiver/plugins/tiles.py:418-446 / :449-467 apply them in the same two places. The bank-coverage assertion is dropped.
Evidence: holo3 is at or above v1 on both colour volumes that closed — xerox c3-cmy7 720 -> 786 baseline / 815 post, xerox c2-cmy8 5,370 -> 5,422 (ARTEFACTS.md §1.3, §1.4 -> src/holo/receiver/docs/conversion_docs/PARITY.md:42-107, :108-205). No test on either side exercises a below-floor colour tile.
Yield: decides-certificates. A floor one bit too high loses a certificate outright; one bit too low costs a demap the checksum then discards.
Options: keep holo3's on the parity numbers; run the costed measurement; port v1's profile-driven form.
Recommendation: run the costed measurement before src/holo goes — add a below-floor colour case to the drift lane on the pattern of the black-and-white one at test_h3_drift_receiver.py:990, asserting holo3's refusal against v1's on the same starved input.
The parity numbers say holo3 loses nothing on the two volumes that closed; they do not say the two floors agree, and an unmeasured floor on the colour path is exactly the class of thing the e030 and hp c2-cmy7 failures would hide.
Cost: unit scale, in an existing lane, while v1 is still importable.

**Ruling:**

### `payload-union-retry-intake-12` — Attempt identity and the spend-once / no-op rule

*Classification CARRIED-DIFFERENT · yield decides-certificates · map: `HOLO1-MAP.md` → `payload-union-retry-intake-12`*

What it does: decides when two evaluations of a tile count as the same attempt — and therefore whether re-reading a tile is another chance at the checksum.
v1: the attempt identity is a hash over the address, stage, decoder profile and the exact component block ids, which are themselves content hashes of the frozen evidence (src/holo/receive/tiles.py:110-149, src/holo/receive/records.py:99-144); new evidence necessarily mints a new identity (docs/RECEIVE-DESIGN.md:1159-1162).
holo3: the key is a plain tuple (page, row, column, stream, stage, iteration budget, and a signature of (capture id, level index) pairs) at src/holo/receiver/plugins/tiles.py:304-319; the store keeps the last outcome per key (src/holo/receiver/store.py:803-811) and attempt facts are deliberately not snapshotted (src/holo/receiver/snapshot.py:16-17). Re-firing is bounded by the worker's version floor (src/holo/receiver/engine.py:452-455), not by identity.
Evidence: retry attempts rose 4.7x / 0->2,694 / 3.7x / 23x / 1.9x across the five post-change volumes, minting 50 / 0 / 21 / 13 / 99 certificates (ARTEFACTS.md §2.6 -> src/holo/receiver/docs/conversion_docs/PARITY.md:449-455). What re-fired step 3's extra scans is recorded as UNKNOWN (ARTEFACTS.md G-11).
Yield: decides-certificates. The key decides which failures arm a 150-iteration retry and which re-reads count as a fresh chance, and the retry route minted certificates on every volume.
Options: keep holo3's and record it; put an evidence digest into the key; run a costed measurement first.
Recommendation: rule this with rows 14 and 18 — one seam, that holo3 has no content-addressed evidence identity. Keep holo3's keying and record it, but run the single sim-lane measurement named in row 14 first.
Putting a digest into the key would change de-duplication across the whole receiver, so it is a measured change, never an assumed improvement.
Worth recording alongside: the real re-fire rule is the pair (declared read-set, version floor), not the key alone — the retry row reads the same keys the scan row does, so a pure read change re-fires both.

**Ruling:**

### `payload-union-retry-intake-14` — Retry never tunnels past its baseline evidence

*Classification DIVERGED · yield decides-certificates · map: `HOLO1-MAP.md` → `payload-union-retry-intake-14`*

What it does: guarantees the second, longer decoding pass sees exactly the evidence that failed the first one.
v1: the retry view is rebuilt from the parent's own component block ids out of the retained frontier, with two refusals — a missing retained block, and a check that the retry's components and dependencies equal the parent's (src/holo/receive/payload_engine.py:283-302, raising at :287-288 and :301-302). Block ids are content hashes, so this is byte-exact.
holo3: src/holo/receiver/plugins/tiles.py:683-707 re-assembles from the live store at fire time and accepts if a same-keyed attempt is still recorded as failed. The key names (capture, level) pairs, not content, so a window re-fitted in between passes the check while feeding the retry different numbers; and the retry's read-set equals the scan's (src/holo/receiver/plugins/tiles.py:795-799), so it re-fires on every evidence change.
Evidence: no direct artefact. The visible surface is the retry multipliers and step 3's unattributed extra scans (src/holo/receiver/docs/conversion_docs/PARITY.md:449-455, ARTEFACTS.md G-11). holo3 also contradicts its own spec paragraph at src/holo/receiver/docs/conversion_docs/02-ldpc.md:256-257.
Yield: decides-certificates. The direction is not one-sided — a re-derived read can be better as easily as worse — but it changes which tiles get a 150-iteration pass over which numbers.
Options: accept the re-derivation as intended holo3 semantics and fix the spec; put an evidence digest into the attempt key; run a costed measurement first.
Recommendation: measure, then accept. A sim-lane case that re-fits a window between an ordinary attempt and its retry, asserting what holo3 actually does, is unit scale and needs no capture.
My reading is that the re-derivation is the better semantics for a receiver that keeps no evidence — the retry is a fresh attempt over the best current read, which is why holo3's retry counts rose while its certificate counts held at or above v1's on five of six parity volumes.
The fix then belongs in src/holo/receiver/docs/conversion_docs/02-ldpc.md, not in the code, and the ruling should say which document survives.

**Ruling:**

### `payload-union-retry-intake-17` — Durable compact evidence blocks and the cross-invocation same-lane frontier

*Classification ABSENT · yield decides-certificates · map: `HOLO1-MAP.md` → `payload-union-retry-intake-17`*

What it does: lets a later run combine a new scan with evidence it never re-reads, by storing compact per-(source, lane, tile) evidence in the session state.
v1: one block per (source hash, lane, page, tile) carrying an observed bitmap, exact 32-bit values, an origin table and dependency ids, content-addressed and persisted (src/holo/receive/evidence_blocks.py:169-345), handed back to the next run as prior blocks with certified addresses dropped (src/holo/receive/production_payload.py:327-360).
holo3: absent by design. Evidence is excluded from the snapshot (src/holo/receiver/snapshot.py:5-17, :49-57) because it is a pure function of the rasters plus a pose and the operator re-declares every capture; a resume re-ingests and re-samples.
Evidence: v1's states ran 2 MB to 15 GB, 28 of them, costing about seven times the file size in memory (931 MB peak for a 131 MB colour state) — ARTEFACTS.md §1.14, src/holo/receiver/docs/conversion_docs/PARITY.md:22. holo3's sheet-I resume chain went 5,543 -> 5,866 -> 5,866 -> 5,880 with every capture re-declared, verified byte-exact three times (experiments/034-sheet-i-full-readout.md:28-44), and holo3's walls are 2.7-4.4 times under v1's on identical crops (ARTEFACTS.md §2.9).
Yield: decides-certificates, conditionally — a union needs two captures live in the store (src/holo/receiver/plugins/tiles.py:754-762), so a resume that declared only the new scan would form none.
Options: keep holo3's; port a durable evidence store; accept the loss.
Recommendation: keep holo3's and record the operator rule, noting that it is already enforced: dropping a capture from the declaration crashes on resume, ruled knowingly with no guard (src/holo/receiver/snapshot.py:27-32).
The trade is deliberate and the record supports it — the walls holo3 saves are exactly the blocks it does not carry.
Record separately that this is the mechanism behind the loss of the 28 archived states when the tree goes (ARTEFACTS.md G-01); only the address freeze survives.

**Ruling:**

### `payload-union-retry-intake-18` — The mop-up supersession contract (extension-only, byte-exact, never revise a committed cell)

*Classification ABSENT · yield integrity · map: `HOLO1-MAP.md` → `payload-union-retry-intake-18`*

What it does: governs what a second look at already-committed territory may do to the evidence underneath it — a replacement must be a strict superset agreeing value-for-value on every cell already seen, with a non-gating count of the disagreements.
v1: src/holo/receive/evidence_blocks.py:473-506 validates the superset, :508-556 tallies dropped observations and winner flips, :557-645 materialises the replacement, applied at the mop-up barrier (src/holo/receive/production_payload.py:339-348).
holo3: absent. There is no second evidence namespace: the mop-up row is a subscription (src/holo/receiver/plugins/revisit.py, src/holo/receiver/docs/SCHEDULE.md:126-128) re-firing the same window's work, and store._fold_evidence replaces the standing evidence wholesale whenever any field changed (src/holo/receiver/store.py:702-746).
Evidence: v1's own pruning counter never fired on any volume (ARTEFACTS.md §2.4 -> docs/DONE-GEOMETRY.md:284-287). holo3's revisit row fired 593 times across five volumes for zero certificates, recorded as the one genuinely zero row (ARTEFACTS.md §2.3 -> src/holo/receiver/docs/conversion_docs/PARITY.md:443-448).
Yield: integrity, not certificates. The checksum gate and the certificate merge mean this cannot produce a wrong answer; what it can produce is an attempt history that is not monotone.
Options: accept the loss; port the algebra; port only the winner-flip canary.
Recommendation: accept the loss for the algebra — there is no second namespace in holo3 for it to guard, and v1's own counters show it never bound on any archived volume.
Rule it in the same entry as row 14: the property this contract really bought is the one holo3's retry baseline gives up, and it should be recorded once, there, rather than twice.
If the winner-flip canary is ever wanted back, it belongs with the divergence canary of row 19, not with a supersession rule holo3 has no place to apply.

**Ruling:**

### `payload-union-retry-intake-19` — The certificate divergence audit (non-gating, per observed cell)

*Classification ABSENT · yield integrity · map: `HOLO1-MAP.md` → `payload-union-retry-intake-19` · brief: `PORT-BRIEFS/certificate-divergence-canary.md`*

What it does: the in-run check that observed evidence agrees with certified truth, counted per observed cell and gating nothing.
v1: one pairing loop for every codec with a per-codec comparison kernel (src/holo/receive/records.py:375-425, colour kernel in src/holo/receive/color_divergence.py), run once per lane over all certified truth against that capture's observations (src/holo/receive/production_payload.py:515-517) into durable records.
holo3: absent from production, stated so in src/holo/receiver/docs/API.md:769. The nearest equivalent is the offline crop-parity harness's wrong-certificate audit.
Evidence: this mechanism produced the project's headline integrity number — zero wrong certificates in 69,066,304 audited cells (docs/COLOR-UPLIFT-MEASUREMENTS.md:3029) — and the print-day run of xerox c2-cmy8 recorded 5,370 divergences alongside 5,370 certificates (experiments/032-xerox-altalink-payload-volumes.md:86-88). holo3's offline batteries cover 285 target addresses, zero wrong, zero out of bounds (ARTEFACTS.md §1.12).
Yield: integrity. It changes no certificate; it is the evidence that the certificates were sound.
Options: keep holo3's offline-only position; port now; archive for later port; accept the loss.
Recommendation: archive for later port, with the brief below. Nothing is at risk without it — the checksum gate stands — so it does not belong in the pre-retirement gate.
But every future paper claim of the 'zero wrong' kind would otherwise rest on a harness nobody is obliged to run, on crops rather than on whole sheets.
The brief should require a measured processor-second share before adoption, because v1 paid this over retained blocks and holo3 would pay it over live evidence.

**Ruling:**

### `payload-union-retry-intake-22` — The attempt-record validation contract and the per-child outcome ledger

*Classification ABSENT · yield integrity · map: `HOLO1-MAP.md` → `payload-union-retry-intake-22`*

What it does: keeps an attempt record honest — the per-child work must add up to the recorded totals, a scan names one component and a union at least two, and a colour attempt that stopped at a child's syndrome stays distinguishable from one that reached the checksum.
v1: src/holo/receive/evidence.py:102-188 validates all of it at construction, including the colour clause at :162-183 that a null checksum result with a non-empty child ledger means the pass stopped early; the recorded iteration count is the measured total, never the budget (docs/RECEIVE-DESIGN.md:1171-1183).
holo3: src/holo/receiver/data.py:296-308 is a frozen dataclass with no validation, and src/holo/receiver/plugins/tiles.py:408-416 sums the children's iterations, ands their syndromes and discards the per-child ledger.
Evidence: none — no artefact on either side turns on a malformed attempt record, and holo3's cost accounting (processor-seconds per certificate, ARTEFACTS.md §2.8) does not use the child ledger.
Yield: integrity, shading into diagnosis. Nothing about which tiles certify: an inconsistent record is a record, not a decision.
Options: accept the loss; port the validation clauses; port only the child ledger.
Recommendation: accept the loss for the validation clauses — they guard a durable record holo3 does not keep (attempt facts are not snapshotted).
Record the child-ledger loss explicitly as a diagnostic debt: it is the only evidence that separates a colour attempt that reached the checksum from one that stopped at a child's syndrome.
That is exactly the discriminating diagnostic the per-plane hypothesis for the failed c2-cmy7 volume is missing (ARTEFACTS.md G-21), so if anyone reopens that question, this ledger is the cheapest instrument and should be rebuilt then rather than now.

**Ruling:**

### `payload-union-retry-intake-23` — Native raster ingest: acquisition identity and the ingest refusals

*Classification CARRIED-DIFFERENT · yield integrity · map: `HOLO1-MAP.md` → `payload-union-retry-intake-23`*

What it does: says what may enter as an independent physical acquisition, and refuses files that cannot be read unambiguously before any decoding starts.
v1: src/holo/receive/scan.py:27-62 binds hash, operator resolution, dtype, shape and channels into the acquisition identity and states the doctrine that only original scanner acquisitions may enter. Refusals: four-channel rasters and bad shapes/dtypes (:79-102), multi-page files (:285-288), non-finite pixels (:130-131), ambiguous image modes; the raster is frozen read-only and borrowed arrays detached (:105-121).
holo3: src/holo/receiver/plugins/intake.py:35-63 hashes the file and reads page 0 with no page-count check; :174 derives channels as one if two-dimensional else three, and _copy_plane (:140-149) then takes channels 0-2. Every refusal is gone.
Evidence: no archived capture triggers any of them — the corpus is Canon grey or colour TIFF at 1200/2400/4800 dpi (ARTEFACTS.md §1.0-§1.10) — and holo3's own survey rests on that premise (src/holo/receiver/docs/conversion_docs/06-intake-survey.md:381-385).
Yield: integrity. Nothing on the archived corpus moves; each dropped refusal turns an operator mistake into a silent wrong read on the next capture.
Options: keep holo3's; port the two cheap refusals; port all of them; accept the loss.
Recommendation: port now, cheaply — a page-count check and a channel-count check in load_native, two statements between them.
The sharp case is not a transparency channel but a four-channel print-shop or CMYK TIFF, which holo3 would read as if its first three channels were red, green and blue; a multi-page TIFF is the same class of silent wrong answer.
The remaining refusals (non-finite pixels, image-mode allowlist, read-only freeze) can be accepted as lost; the dtype one is ruled separately in row 27.

**Ruling:**

### `payload-union-retry-intake-24` — Per-barrier source re-authentication

*Classification ABSENT · yield integrity · map: `HOLO1-MAP.md` → `payload-union-retry-intake-24` · brief: `PORT-BRIEFS/source-re-authentication.md`*

What it does: checks, just before anything durable is written, that the scan file the run is about to attest to is still the file it hashed at ingest.
v1: src/holo/receive/scan.py:212-255 re-stats the source at every durable barrier; an exact (device, inode, size, modification time) match is a memo hit, and any difference escalates to a full re-hash and refuses on mismatch. The threat model on record is accidental mutation, not an adversary.
holo3: absent. The file is hashed once at ingest (src/holo/receiver/plugins/intake.py:35, :172, :225) and never re-checked; nothing re-authenticates before the snapshot is written.
Evidence: the memo is why this was affordable — v1's whole 169-second phased-schedule residual was eight extra re-authentications, one hash pass over a 6.8 GB scan costing about 19.1 seconds (ARTEFACTS.md §2.9 -> docs/PHASED-SCHEDULE.md:535-548).
Yield: integrity. It cannot change which tiles certify; it decides whether a run's provenance claim survives a file changing underneath it.
Options: keep holo3's; port now; archive for later port; accept the loss.
Recommendation: archive for later port, with the brief below.
holo3 re-reads every raster on every run, so an unchanged-source guarantee buys much less than it did for v1's retained evidence — but a four-run resume chain still writes snapshots attesting to hashes taken hours earlier, and a stat memo before the snapshot write costs a stat.
The brief must carry the measured cost of the naive form so nobody rebuilds it without the memo.

**Ruling:**

### `payload-union-retry-intake-27` — Non-uint8 rasters: dtype-preserving views vs uint8-only materialisation

*Classification DIVERGED · yield integrity · map: `HOLO1-MAP.md` → `payload-union-retry-intake-27`*

What it does: decides what a scan that is not 8-bit decodes to.
v1: the 8-bit path keeps the frozen operation order byte-for-byte, while other real dtypes keep their own dtype and numerical range — 16-bit views are never clipped — with separate exact integer, floating-point and arbitrary-precision kernels so no scale limit is imposed (src/holo/receive/scale_space.py:13-19, :391-437, :461-500); crops come back in the native dtype.
holo3: every plane is materialised into an 8-bit memory map (src/holo/receiver/plugins/intake.py:134-157) with no dtype check anywhere. A 16-bit source is mangled two different ways — the native level wraps modulo 256 (src/holo/receiver/plugins/intake.py:140-149) while the decimated levels round and clip (src/holo/receiver/lib/decimate.py:115-119, :163-171) — so the rung ladder would not be self-consistent.
Evidence: none — every archived capture is 8-bit and holo3's own survey says so as its premise (src/holo/receiver/docs/conversion_docs/06-intake-survey.md:381-385). For 8-bit sources the two scale spaces are bit-identical.
Yield: integrity. Nothing on the archived corpus moves; this is a trap for the next scanner, not a present loss.
Options: keep holo3's; refuse non-8-bit input with a named error; restore the general kernels.
Recommendation: port now, cheaply — one dtype check in load_native that refuses anything but 8-bit, naming the dtype.
Restoring the general kernels is not worth it: no archived capture needs them, and supporting a 16-bit scanner is a measured decision for the day one arrives, not a port.
What must not survive is silently decoding a 16-bit scan into noise, and doing so inconsistently across rungs so that the symptom points at the wrong module.

**Ruling:**

### `payload-union-retry-intake-28` — Platen backing — per-lane vs per-capture

*Classification CARRIED-DIFFERENT · yield decides-certificates · map: `HOLO1-MAP.md` → `payload-union-retry-intake-28`*

What it does: sets the value the sampler uses where there is no printed page — the fill outside the support and the pad past a raster edge.
v1: the median of four 32-pixel border strips computed on the lane raster, so each rung fills with its own border median (src/holo/receive/placement/geometry.py:109-119, used at src/holo/receive/place.py:833 and :919). At a coarse rung those 32 pixels are a wider physical band.
holo3: the identical four-strip expression computed once on the native raster at ingest and stored on the capture (src/holo/receiver/plugins/intake.py:66-75, :176, :197-206); every rung then uses that one number.
Evidence: one number exists and it is holo2's — 0 to 2 luma levels out of 255 apart on the two black-and-white volumes (e029 native 227.0 against rung medians 227-228; xerox 229 against 230-231), with the effect called nil because no payload cell reads a filled pixel (holo2/docs/kparity/K-PARITY-CATALOG-draft.md §2.14, filed as inert with a watch item). ARTEFACTS.md carries no entry for it.
Yield: decides-certificates as a class of thing — it is a sampled value — but measured inert on the two volumes checked, and unmeasured on colour and on sheet I.
Options: keep holo3's; recompute per lane; measure on a capture with interior support holes.
Recommendation: keep holo3's. The one measurement says the two numbers differ by at most two levels and that no payload cell reads either of them.
Carry the watch item forward verbatim rather than acting on it: a capture with support holes inside the printed field is what would separate the two, and none of the archived captures has one.
Also record that the number is holo2-side, so nobody later cites it as a holo3-versus-v1 measurement.

**Ruling:**

### `payload-union-retry-intake-29` — Duplicate re-ingest and the one-DPI-per-source refusal

*Classification CARRIED-DIFFERENT · yield integrity · map: `HOLO1-MAP.md` → `payload-union-retry-intake-29`*

What it does: says what happens when the operator declares the same file twice, or the same file at two different resolutions.
v1: identity is the source hash, so a re-declared source is the same acquisition and merges at the same key (src/holo/receive/scan.py:29-37). A re-declaration at a different resolution is refused at every durable barrier: src/holo/receive/session.py:302 calls _bind_facts (:577-581), which raises when the stored capture's immutable facts differ.
holo3: src/holo/receiver/plugins/intake.py:213-245 dedups by file hash within a run, emitting a duplicate marker for the second declaration, and refuses outright when one source is declared at two resolutions, with a message naming the reason (a corrected resolution renames every lane the evidence cites).
Evidence: v1's duplicate re-ingest preserved state byte-for-byte (ARTEFACTS.md §2.6 -> docs/RECEIVE-DESIGN-EVIDENCE.md:499), and the misdeclared-resolution incident on the rung-experiment sheets ended in a clean refusal (experiments/033).
Yield: integrity. Neither behaviour changes which tiles certify on the archived corpus.
Options: keep holo3's; port a cross-run form; accept the scope difference.
Recommendation: keep holo3's, and record the scope difference rather than a loss.
v1 refuses for longer (its check is against durable state, so it catches a corrected resolution on a later run); holo3 refuses earlier and more legibly (at ingest, before any work, with the reason in the message).
Each is the right scope for its architecture: holo3 retains no evidence a corrected resolution could invalidate, and a capture dropped from a resume declaration already fails loudly (src/holo/receiver/snapshot.py:27-32).
This row also retires the seed's question about intake.py:229 — that line is this refusal, not an integer-scale clause.

**Ruling:**

---

## 3. Calibration, context, ownership

### `calibration-context-ownership-15` — the standing region-context cell: a quality-ordered decision cell, and the standing fit applied to late-arriving windows

*Classification DIVERGED · yield decides-certificates · map: `HOLO1-MAP.md` → `calibration-context-ownership-15`*

The standing region-context cell: which of several context fits a region keeps, and whether a window that joins the region after the best fit ever gets corrected.
v1: nothing like it. A region is fitted once per pass over that pass's complete window population and that fit is what the pass uses (src/holo/receive/_payload_context.py:262-296, src/holo/receive/payload.py:189-213). There is no cross-pass comparison of fits and no notion of a better standing fit.
holo3: the region's context is a DECISION cell ordered by (adopted, -validation log-loss) (src/holo/receiver/store.py:750-755, src/holo/receiver/data.py:156-161) - a re-fit that scores worse does not replace the standing one. Adoption and derived-cell maintenance are decoupled: on a refusal the fold still pushes the STANDING adopted offsets onto any input window carrying no correction yet (src/holo/receiver/store.py:769-778), and on a tie the re-fit's own outputs are applied (the held branch, :756-759, gated at :761). The elif at :769 is safe from a missing standing fit only because a None standing always adopts (src/holo/receiver/store.py:44-46).
Evidence - all of it in commit bodies, none of it in ARTEFACTS.md: the first landing (9a15a51) cost 274 certificates on real paper - an e029 re-harvest read 1,664 against the sheet's 1,938, with calibrate firing 38,547 jobs at 31.4 fires per window - because a worse-scoring re-fit dropped the entire rewrite batch (bc730a0). After the fix all four sims re-decode identical on payload sha, certificate count, signature, bits and addresses, and the K job-count golden returns to 18 retries / 88 scans; those four lost retries and four lost scans were exactly the starved windows' tile decodes.
Still open, in 9a15a51's own words: context quality is measurably non-monotone over growing evidence (0.0201-0.0216 oscillation, zero certificate effect on sims), so an evidence-base-major ordinal versus quality-only is a yield question. The reason is structural - the ordinal compares held-out losses fitted on DIFFERENT populations, and a fit over more windows can score worse without being worse.
Yield: decides-certificates. Today the consequence is bounded - the fix guarantees every window carries some adopted correction, and no bit moved on any sim. What is unmeasured is whether a real sheet's late windows would do better under the newest fit than under the best-scoring older one.
Options: keep holo3's; or make the ordinal evidence-base-major with quality as the tie-break; or measure - the e029 K harvest already proposed for the context-apply row, re-run once with the alternative ordinal, is about 25-35 minutes for the pair on prior wall times, one decode at a time.
Recommendation: keep holo3's for now, but put the ordinal to Germano with the 274-certificate incident attached - it is the family's one genuine ruling. Whatever he decides, lift the three numbers from the commit bodies into ARTEFACTS.md section 2.2 before those bodies are their only home.

**Ruling:**

---

## 4. Refinement

### `refinement-02` — The composed RF1 fit for one window (refine_segment vs refine_window) end to end

*Classification CARRIED-DIFFERENT · yield decides-certificates · map: `HOLO1-MAP.md` → `refinement-02`*

WHAT IT DOES. The whole per-window refinement fit end to end: take the standing pose, sample a baseline, derive the score reference, walk the candidate
grid, score the held-out sites, and either return a revised pose or a refusal.
BOTH SIDES. v1 refine_segment at src/holo/receive/certified_refine.py:735-931 works from a region-wide prepared image and a frozen generation-zero evidence
record, and verifies the baseline sample byte-for-byte against the retained raw means. holo3 refine_window at src/holo/receiver/plugins/refine.py:412-509 works from a
per-window crop, re-derives the window-local pose from the durable native affine (local_segment, :370-387), and takes the fresh sample as the baseline.
EVIDENCE. No oracle on either side compares the two composed fits; holo3's only tests are behavioural
(src/holo/receiver/tests/test_h3_plugins_calibrate_refine.py:945-976). Four composition differences, all sub-float32 on inspection: the byte-replay assertion (v1
only), the midpoint/spread source, float32 vs float64 sampling, and holo3's extra unchanged_affine outcome. The one difference that looked like a defect is
not: v1's in-bounds refusal fires only on the baseline (certified_refine.py:746 is the sole require_in_bounds call site), and holo3 enforces the same thing
upstream at calibrate.py:168-175/:365-367 plus refine.py:640-641.
OPTIONS. Keep holo3's; or a costed measurement — a sim-lane cross-tree oracle inside holo3's own suite that drives v1's refine_segment and holo3's
refine_window from one synthetic window and compares the chosen (dtheta, du, dv) and the adopt/refuse bit. That is a test, not a receiver run (claude-
plan.md 2, principle 3), and costs an afternoon; it must be written before Gate E, because after Phase 4 there is no v1 arm to drive.
RECOMMENDATION. Costed measurement, written as one drift-suite test in Phase 2 alongside the literal-vector freeze of refinement-01. If it is not written
then, rule 'keep holo3's' and record the composition as permanently unproven.

**Ruling:**

### `refinement-03` — The detector the adoption gate scores: context-corrected logits vs marginal logits

*Classification DIVERGED · yield decides-certificates · map: `HOLO1-MAP.md` → `refinement-03` · brief: `PORT-BRIEFS/context-corrected-adoption-gate.md`*

WHAT IT DOES. Decides which detector the adoption gate measures the held-out improvement on: the marginal PAVA log-likelihood, or that plus the region's adopted
neighbour-count context offsets.
BOTH SIDES. v1 _fixed_detector_logits at src/holo/receive/certified_refine.py:702-715 recomputes the neighbour counts over the candidate pose's own cells and applies
the region's adopted offsets, and both base_loss (:800) and candidate_loss (:902) ride that path; the request constructor refuses a ctx-profiled generation zero
without its adopted decision (:284-291). holo3 scores marginal logits only (src/holo/receiver/plugins/refine.py:439-441), on a documented holo2 ruling carried into spec 08
section 5 (src/holo/receiver/docs/conversion_docs/08-pose-refine.md:384-385).
EVIDENCE. The prior is holo1's: context calibration is adopted on all 70 eligible region fits on both footings, and the context correction alone moved 742 to 749
certificates while cutting emitted-cell BER from 1.9179% to 1.3509% (docs/RECEIVE-DESIGN-EVIDENCE.md:611-612). The counter-prior is holo3's own high adoption rate,
2,471 adoptions on 4,616 fires (src/holo/receiver/docs/conversion_docs/POST-FORENSICS.md:20-58). No artefact measures the two gates against each other, and the catalogue's proposal
(recompute both ways off stored revisions) is not runnable: holo3 stores nothing for a refused fit and the archived holo1 states hold no refinement inputs.
OPTIONS. Keep holo3's; port now (add the arm behind a dial, then A/B the two sim decodes and the per-arm existence stimuli — minutes, inside the test lanes, not a
receiver run); archive for later port; accept the loss.
RECOMMENDATION. Archive for later port, with the sim A/B offered as an optional cheap check first. Porting now means editing the one kernel whose whole value is that
it is bit-pinned, days before its reference tree is deleted, and the result would be uninterpretable until refinement-16 gives the refusals a voice. Archiving costs
nothing and keeps the option: the arm is fourteen lines of v1 and it must be git-moved into docs/archive/holo1 before Gate E or it is gone.

**Ruling:**

### `refinement-06` — Which certificates may label a proposal: prior-barrier snapshot vs fire-time live set

*Classification CARRIED-DIFFERENT · yield decides-certificates · map: `HOLO1-MAP.md` → `refinement-06`*

WHAT IT DOES. Fixes which certificates may label a refinement fit — the training population the pose is fitted from.
BOTH SIDES. v1 builds a CertificateFrontier once per refinement level from the prior barriers' certificates
(src/holo/receive/certified_cascade.py:185), validated, sorted, conflict-checked and identified by a SHA-256
(src/holo/receive/certified_refine.py:103-172); every window in the level is fitted against that identical snapshot. holo3 reads whatever certificates
the store holds in the window's reach at fire time and re-encodes their printed fields there (src/holo/receiver/plugins/refine.py:576-586, :117-127); there is no
snapshot object, no frontier identity, and no conflict check inside refinement — the store's fold carries that terminally (src/holo/receiver/store.py:899-915).
EVIDENCE. No artefact compares the two label populations. What is measured is the consequence: which store state a job fires against is not
deterministic under wait(FIRST_COMPLETED) (src/holo/receiver/docs/conversion_docs/POST-FORENSICS.md:380-406). On labels alone holo3's rule is monotonically better —
the fire-time set is a superset of any earlier snapshot.
OPTIONS. Keep holo3's; or reintroduce a per-level snapshot, which would mean reintroducing levels.
RECOMMENDATION. Keep holo3's. It cannot lose labels, and the reproducibility cost it introduces is a workers question, already open as POST-FORENSICS
section E.1 and already absent at --workers 1. Record the dropped frontier identity as a forensic loss and let it be answered by refinement-07's
lineage port, not by rebuilding the snapshot.

**Ruling:**

### `refinement-07` — Certificate/pose dependency lineage on refined poses and blocks

*Classification ABSENT · yield integrity · map: `HOLO1-MAP.md` → `refinement-07` · brief: `PORT-BRIEFS/refinement-lineage.md`*

WHAT IT DOES. Records which certificates a refined pose was fitted from, so that later readers can attribute a certificate to the refinement that enabled
it.
BOTH SIDES. v1 carries dependency_certificate_ids on every SegmentLabels (src/holo/receive/certified_refine.py:334-337), a frontier_id on the snapshot, and
unions the adopted frontier's ids into the minted pose's lineage and into every block minted in the delta (src/holo/receive/production_refine.py:141-143,
:310-318). holo3 keeps a Revision.basis of certificate ADDRESSES, stamped on the revised placement as refine_basis (src/holo/receiver/plugins/refine.py:508-514) and
durable through snapshots (src/holo/receiver/snapshot.py:49-58) — but it exists to gate the re-arm, and no certificate, block or event points back at it.
EVIDENCE. Measured, and the numbers are the campaign's: 18 of holo1's 1,930 xerox K certificates were won by refinement and all 18 sit in column 0
(src/holo/receiver/docs/conversion_docs/GAPS.md:95-107); 387 of 720, 808 of 1,559 and 1,774 of 5,370 certificates on the three colour volumes carry lineage, and 4 of 4, 20
of 22 and 33 of 36 of holo3's misses are lineage-carrying tiles (GAPS.md:177-181, :226-227, :302-305). Against that: src/holo/receiver/docs/conversion_docs/POST-
FORENSICS.md:489-499, "no event links a certificate to the fact that woke it".
OPTIONS. Accept the loss; port now (one field on the fact that follows the re-read, or one event per adoption naming its basis); archive for later port.
RECOMMENDATION. Archive for later port, and fold the cheap half into refinement-16's event package now — the adoption event should name the basis it used,
which costs nothing extra and gives half the trail immediately. The full reverse edge (certificate to enabling adoption) is a data-model change and should
not be rushed before Gate E.

**Ruling:**

### `refinement-08` — The refinement domain: which windows are offered a fit, and when

*Classification CARRIED-DIFFERENT · yield decides-certificates · map: `HOLO1-MAP.md` → `refinement-08`*

WHAT IT DOES. Decides which windows are offered a refinement fit and when — where refinement spend goes.
BOTH SIDES. v1 appends one refinement level per ordinary ladder rung, re-derives that lane's committed poses, applies a pixel-free bounding-box pre-gate and
then the exact unresolved-region need map, and refines every surviving window once against one frontier (src/holo/receive/cascade.py:327-336,
production_refine.py:97-109, :208-222). holo3 runs a per-window subscription: eligible when the window's evidence reach holds at least one certified and one
uncertified tile and the certified set strictly outgrows the adopted basis, retired when the whole reach is certified (src/holo/receiver/plugins/refine.py:537-548,
:636-656).
EVIDENCE. The family's sharpest measured gap and it is already half closed. holo1 won 18 xerox K certificates by refinement, all in column 0; holo3 got 13 of
them with no refinement at all, and the 5 it missed are 5 of its 8 misses (src/holo/receiver/docs/conversion_docs/GAPS.md:95-107). On that sheet holo3 ran 144 refinement jobs,
120 on the high-column edge and only 24 on the column-0 edge, and for five page rows no job covered the tile at all (GAPS.md:138-160). After the post batch
widened the re-arm, xerox K went 8 misses to 5, recovering two of the rescue-column tiles (src/holo/receiver/docs/conversion_docs/PARITY.md:442). Why the skew exists is
inference, not measurement (GAPS.md:377-381, GAP G-10). I checked the one mechanical hypothesis available: the refine row subscribes on window_reach and gates
on evidence_reach (src/holo/receiver/plugins/refine.py:628-633 vs :642-646), but the two maps cannot diverge after an adoption, so this does not explain the skew.
OPTIONS. Keep holo3's; or widen further blind; or measure first via refinement-16.
RECOMMENDATION. Keep holo3's, and treat refinement-16 as this row's prerequisite rather than a separate line. Fold in one line of hygiene while the row is open:
subscribe on the same reach the gate tests.

**Ruling:**

### `refinement-09` — Refining a revision: never (v1) vs the certified-reach lift (holo3)
