*Classification DIVERGED · yield decides-certificates · map: `HOLO1-MAP.md` → `refinement-09`*

WHAT IT DOES. Decides whether a window that has already adopted a revised pose may be refined again.
BOTH SIDES. v1 never refines a revision: each refinement level refines the poses committed by its own base lane, so a window is refined at most once
per rung (src/holo/receive/cascade.py:319-336). holo3 stamps the adopted basis on the placement and re-fires when the certified reach strictly
outgrows it, so chains are unbounded in principle (src/holo/receiver/plugins/refine.py:537-542, :509-514); a refusing fit publishes no basis, so eligibility
persists (src/holo/receiver/tests/test_h3_plugins_calibrate_refine.py:960-976 pins exactly that).
EVIDENCE. Against iterating: the E024 knee — after a full scale-space cascade, broad affine feedback beat control by two to five tiles of 757-772,
"expect one or two productive passes, never iterate to convergence" (src/holo/receiver/docs/conversion_docs/08-pose-refine.md:283-287); and holo2's port of the one-
shot rule measured zero yield three ways at up to +90% wall (ARTEFACTS.md section 2.3). For iterating: sheet I ran 2,950 colour refinement fires for
1,631 adoptions, and 124 tiles first certified after the first adoption, 96 of them new against baseline (src/holo/receiver/docs/conversion_docs/POST-FORENSICS.md:56,
:447-462). The cost question is the fire count, not the yield.
OPTIONS. Keep holo3's lift; bound the chain (a generation cap); revert to one fit per window.
RECOMMENDATION. Keep holo3's. The measured counter-evidence is holo3's own and it is the cleanest causal chain in the batch; the e024 knee is a v1-era
measurement of a different cascade. Two pieces of follow-through: mark spec 08 section 2.9 clause (c) superseded in Phase 2, and hand the fire count
to the scheduling family's pricing row (refinement-19), since 2,950 fires at a flat price is a cost question the yield answer does not settle.

**Ruling:**

### `refinement-11` — The R-level's own evidence namespace: only certificates cross lanes (monotonicity of a refinement)

*Classification DIVERGED · yield decides-certificates · map: `HOLO1-MAP.md` → `refinement-11`*

WHAT IT DOES. Decides whether an adopted revision that turns out to be worse can destroy a read the base lane already had.
BOTH SIDES. v1 gives each refinement lane its own evidence namespace, so the base lane's evidence stands untouched and only certificates — terminal
and conflict-checked — cross (src/holo/receive/types.py:322-330, production_refine.py:249-250). A bad revision costs the fit and nothing else. holo3
has one store, one standing placement and one standing evidence per window: the revised placement outranks any non-refine one and the re-read replaces
the standing evidence unconditionally (src/holo/receiver/data.py:117-120, store.py:702-746).
EVIDENCE. The predicted failure mode was looked for and the artefacts refuse it: 54 lost tiles across three post volumes and "not one of them is a
scheduling defect", with refinement explicitly ruled out on step 6, on 12 of 17 step-6 gains and on the step-2 loss by event ordering
(src/holo/receiver/docs/conversion_docs/POST-FORENSICS.md:265-345); on sheet I, 13 losses, about half attempted before the first adoption, 11 of 13 in the Y stream
(:339-352); every completed volume closed byte-exact (src/holo/receiver/docs/conversion_docs/PARITY.md:441).
OPTIONS. Keep holo3's; or give evidence an ordinal so a worse re-read cannot supersede a better one.
RECOMMENDATION. Keep holo3's. The adoption gate — a held-out gain plus two non-regressions, the same gate as v1's — buys in practice what v1 got
structurally, and no measured loss is attributable to it. Record two residuals for the record: evidence supersession is unordered, and placement
supersession is one-way once a revision exists. Neither is worth work now; if evidence ordering is ever wanted, it is the scheduling family's fold
rule, not a refinement change.

**Ruling:**

### `refinement-14` — Colour certified pose refinement (per-plane presence labels, frozen per-plane calibration, furniture non-regression, diagonal-stripe holdout)

*Classification CARRIED-DIFFERENT · yield decides-certificates · map: `HOLO1-MAP.md` → `refinement-14`*

WHAT IT DOES. Re-fits a colour window's pose from certified neighbours, using exact per-plane presence labels, a frozen per-plane calibration, a diagonal-
stripe holdout and a furniture non-regression clause.
BOTH SIDES. v1 drives the whole committed inventory in one address-level pass and mints a replacement address (src/holo/receive/color_refine.py:253-341,
:416-639, production_color.py:508-600). holo3 is a per-window descriptor row at price 200 (src/holo/receiver/plugins/color_refine.py:83-155, :228-348, :468-481),
importing the K kernel functions and reusing the colour head's own row builder for the label join; it emits a revision whose adoption re-fires the whole
colour lane through ordinary declared reads.
EVIDENCE. Numerics pinned (src/holo/receiver/tests/test_h3_drift_receiver.py:846-861). Adoption rates 75.2% / 66.9% / 55.3% across three post volumes
(src/holo/receiver/docs/conversion_docs/POST-FORENSICS.md:56). holo1 misses where the lane fired went 130 to 60 (POST-FORENSICS.md:230-241) — but honestly re-adjudicated as
partially supported, not closed: step 6 went 22 to 26 worse while adopting 527 poses, and on two of three volumes most gains predate any adoption. On sheet
I, 124 tiles first certified after the first adoption, 96 new, gains overwhelmingly Y (:456-462). Cost prior from v1: about 550 s for zero yield on the
scrap corpus, 94% of the payload bucket (docs/PAYLOAD-MEASUREMENTS.md:213-219).
OPTIONS. Keep holo3's; or build a cross-tree colour fit oracle. The latter is more expensive than it looks: v1's only colour refinement fixture asserts a
refusal, so an adopting fixture would have to be written from scratch.
RECOMMENDATION. Keep holo3's. This is the strongest mechanism in the family and its composition difference is the same per-window/per-pass difference
already ruled acceptable on K. If a cross-tree oracle is built at all, build the K one (refinement-02) and let the shared kernel carry the colour claim.

**Ruling:**

### `refinement-16` — Refinement observability: per-window progress events and refusal reasons

*Classification ABSENT · yield operational · map: `HOLO1-MAP.md` → `refinement-16`*

WHAT IT DOES. Nothing at run time. It is what makes every other row in this family measurable rather than inferable: per-window refinement progress, the terminal
outcome and the refusal reason.
BOTH SIDES. v1 emits a per-window progress event built from a seventeen-field metrics record — eligible, adopted, reason, candidate count, the three deltas, both
loglosses, both pilot mismatches and seven support counts (src/holo/receive/production_refine.py:270-281, certified_refine.py:340-365), with the colour analogue at
production_color.py:566. holo3 emits nothing: both refinement jobs return an empty events tuple (src/holo/receiver/plugins/refine.py:610-611, color_refine.py:409-410), and the
store drops the whole fact for a refusal (src/holo/receiver/store.py:791-792). Revision carries reason, both loglosses and the candidate count, and nothing reads them.
EVIDENCE. Measured, and it has already cost the campaign a published claim: PARITY.md's "the refinement rows adopted nothing, on any volume" was structurally pinned
at zero because a refine-route pose never reaches the analyser's counter; the true figure is 2,471 adoptions on 4,616 fires (src/holo/receiver/docs/conversion_docs/POST-
FORENSICS.md:20-58; the retracted line at PARITY.md:448). The refusal histogram is "unbuildable from any run dir in this batch, not missed, structurally absent"
(:194-204); how the 45% splits across three terminal outcomes is "the one instrumentation gap worth closing" (:166-168); and GAP G-10 is open for the same reason
(GAPS.md:377-381).
OPTIONS. Port now; archive; accept the loss.
RECOMMENDATION. Port now — the cheapest and highest-leverage item in the family. Scope: one event dataclass emitted by both plugins carrying window id, adopted,
reason, candidate count, both loglosses, the basis and the reach the fit saw, plus a counter for prepare-stage refusals. Pattern already exists at
src/holo/receiver/plugins/calibrate.py:618-624. Do it before Gate E so the GAPS comparison can be re-run against a holo1 tree that still exists.

**Ruling:**

---

## 5. Close

### `close-06` — Disposition of a TERMINAL non-verified close (integrity_failure / unsupported)

*Classification DIVERGED · yield operational · map: `HOLO1-MAP.md` → `close-06`*

What it does. Decides what happens when the certified evidence is fatally inconsistent, or names a payload interpretation this receiver does not implement: whether the run stops, what it keeps, and what the operator is told.
What v1 does. close_record_from_result maps integrity_failure and unsupported to durable CloseRecord statuses carrying the reason and the certificate-frontier digest (src/holo/receive/fountain_close.py:829-863); SessionState.status mirrors it, so every driver loop breaks the moment the session leaves OPEN (src/holo/receive/runtime.py:555, :765, :784), and the verdict is written into the state file and is immutable thereafter (session.py:642-659). v1 stops AND keeps.
What holo3 does. integrity_failure raises data.IntegrityError out of _close_decide (src/holo/receiver/engine.py:731-733, was :608-611). The exception escapes run() through the executor-shutdown finally (:178-210), so _finish never runs - and _write_snapshot has exactly one call site, inside _finish (:653).
[Corrected 2026-09-05 after the rebase: on the tip `_write_snapshot` has TWO call sites — the `--snapshot-every` cadence (default 30 min, also SIGUSR1; src/holo/receiver/engine.py:310-320) and `_finish` (:787) — and since main's `31d786f` each write keeps its predecessor as `facts.npz.bak`; the loss on an abort is bounded by the cadence, not the run. The adjudication of close-06 stays with Germano.]
No facts.npz, no RunDone, no accounting. The CLI prints one line and exits 1 (src/holo/receiver/cli.py:184-186). Separately, 'unsupported' is not terminal at all: the loop breaks only on 'verified' (:200-208), so the run drains to exhaustion and is finally reported as 'open'.
Wider than the row says. The same fatal exception is raised in three other places that run in EVERY mode: the store's merge gate on conflicting certificates and opposite metadata bits (src/holo/receiver/store.py:900-915), and CloseProgress.absorb's four raises at src/holo/receiver/engine.py:716 -> src/holo/receiver/format/close.py:226-228, :242-244, :256-263, :317-320 (re-anchored 2026-09-04 after the column-group merge (`2d56e5a`) rewrote close.py; the four raises themselves are unchanged). So holo3 has no mode in which a wrong certificate leaves the run's certificates on disk. holo3 stops and LOSES.
Evidence. No artefact exercises either path: the frozen holo1 certificate JSONs record only 'verified' and 'open' (src/holo/receiver/docs/retirement_docs-2026-09/holo1-certificates/*.json), and no holo3 run in the record has produced an integrity_failure or an unsupported close (ARTEFACTS.md 2.5). This is a code read on both sides, verified line by line above.
Yield. It cannot change which tiles certify on the archived corpus - no archived run reaches these paths. What it can do is destroy an entire run's earned certificates: the sheet-I chain's runs cost thousands of CPU-seconds each (arms-wave run on machine 2 spent 5,211 CPU-s in the field arm alone, MEASUREMENTS-2026-08-31.md:22-35) and the next run resumes from facts.npz.
Options. (1) Keep holo3's. (2) Port now, at the RUN BOUNDARY, not inside the close: write the snapshot before any data.IntegrityError propagates, and make 'unsupported' break the frontier loop and surface in RunDone's status. (3) Convert integrity_failure into a terminal RunResult status instead of an exception. (4) Accept the loss.
Recommendation. Port now, option 2 - a small, format-free change with no yield at stake, but it must be scoped at run() so it covers the store's merge gate and absorb as well as the close result; a fix inside _close_decide alone would miss the paths that fire in --complete and --no-close.
Confirm with a sim inside holo3's own test lane (feed two contradictory certificates through the engine, seconds), not a receiver run. Note in the ruling that holo3's own snapshot doctrine argues for this: a snapshot is the operator's own run product (src/holo/receiver/snapshot.py:20-32), which is a reason to write it on the one path that most needs it.

**Ruling:**

### `close-10` — The e028 verified-close trap — a retained VERIFIED close must re-ask the fountain

*Classification ABSENT · yield operational · map: `HOLO1-MAP.md` → `close-10`*

What it does. Guards the one way a resume can fabricate a verified close: v1's durable close record stores only the payload SHA and length, never the bytes, so a replay over an already-VERIFIED state must re-ask the fountain rather than construct a verified result with no payload.
What v1 does. The gate's first clause returns None when the retained close is VERIFIED, forcing a real close (src/holo/receive/production_close.py:68-74), and _replay_deferred_close raises if a verified replay comes back without payload bytes (src/holo/receive/runtime.py:365-407, :388-394).
The comment names the incident: the e028 ink-page rerun, 2026-08-04.
What holo3 does. Nothing, because there is nothing to guard. The snapshot persists facts only - src/holo/receiver/snapshot.py:49-58 lists eight fact families and no close record - so every run, resume included, recomputes the close from the certificates it currently holds. The trap is structurally unreachable.
Evidence. The sheet-I four-run resume chain: 5,543 -> 5,866 -> 5,866 -> 5,880 certificates with three VERIFIED closes over the same payload, including one after the run that had already closed - 'VERIFIED 2445b95db5b49bf3, 1,798,761 B, three times' (ARTEFACTS.md:235; the log itself is experiments/034-sheet-i-full-readout.md:28-33).
Yield. None. No certificate changes; the capability the guard protected is delivered by holo3's recomputation.
Options. Accept the loss (recorded) / reintroduce a durable close and its guard - which nothing in the record asks for.
Recommendation. Accept the loss. The mechanism exists only to service a durable close record holo3 deliberately does not keep, and the e034 chain demonstrated the replacement three times byte-exact.
Carry the comment's GENERAL sentence into holo3 in the comment pass - 'a close is a pure derivation from certificates; never fabricate a verified result without asking the fountain' - even though the specific trap cannot recur.

**Ruling:**

### `close-12` — Rank reporting for unsolved blocks — exact GE rank vs the have-floor

*Classification CARRIED-DIFFERENT · yield operational · map: `HOLO1-MAP.md` → `close-12`*

What it does. Produces the 'at least N additional symbols required' number the operator reads off an unsolved close, from the per-block GF(2) rank.
What v1 does. rank_status runs one coefficient-only elimination per changed block, so every unsolved block reports its EXACT rank and the summed shortfall is exact (src/holo/core/fountain.py:763-781, was :929-948 before core/fountain.py was split on 2026-09-04; src/holo/receive/fountain_close.py:539-554, unchanged).
What holo3 does. Re-derived 2026-09-04 after the column-group merge (`2d56e5a`): `_rank_of` is deleted. Rank is now per (block, COLUMN GROUP) and is written ONLY where an elimination ran and failed; it is forgotten when the group solves and forgotten again the moment the group gains a row (src/holo/receiver/format/fountain.py:478-506 and :399-405). `block_status` names the block's scarcest OPEN group and reports that group's rows and rank - `None` whenever no failed elimination stands for it (:508-525). The floor moved into `BlockStatus.short` = needed - (rank if rank is not None else min(rows, needed)) (:142-148), and `_open_result` is what prints it (src/holo/receiver/format/close.py:471-488). Since rank <= rows always, and can be strictly less, holo3's reported shortfall is still a LOWER BOUND. Two changes to the ruling's substance. On a UNIFORM row - every identity ever printed - the number is bit-for-bit what it was, because one certificate contributes exactly one row to the block's one group. And the understatement got SHARPER: because a new row deletes the recorded rank, a counting consult taken between a failed elimination and the next attempt reports rank None and short 0 on a block that cannot solve. Measured on the rebased tip: a single-block K code at K=20 with a forced rank-17 elimination reports rank=17, short=3; one symbol later, before the next try_solve, the same block reports rank=None, short=0.
Evidence. The difference is provable from the code and needs no run. No artefact compares the two shortfall numbers on the same certificate set; src/holo/receiver/tests/test_h3_format_close.py:394-407 does take the floor branch (50 certificates against k=68) but cannot discriminate, because the fixture hands out systematic ESIs first so the true rank equals the floor.
The discriminating case this entry asked for now EXISTS in holo3's own lane, added by the column-group merge: src/holo/receiver/tests/test_h3_format_column_groups.py:425-453 drives a group to K_b with DEPENDENT rows, records a rank strictly below K_b, and pins that one new row in the group clears it; :374-421 pins the floor branch itself (rows=56, rank=None, short=k-56).
[Corrected 2026-09-05: `test_h3_format_column_groups.py:425-453` does NOT record a rank — its rank assertions iterate an empty `decoder._rank` and pass vacuously; the pin that really records one is the count-gate case at :471-506 (a monkeypatched elimination returning k_b−1). The discriminating case — 129 rows per group, rank 128 standing until one new row in the group clears it — was reproduced by hand on the tip and is NOT yet a test.]
Yield. None, and I re-checked against the rewritten code rather than assumed: nothing schedules on rank. close_is_new consults `_snapshot()` - the metadata-terminal flag and one (decoder key, block, have) triple per unsolved block (src/holo/receiver/format/close.py:407-419); `record_attempt` is DELETED and `close()` takes the snapshot itself (:421-424), reading solved and have; and the count gate reads `min_rows`, a row count per (block, group) (:390-394 into src/holo/receiver/format/fountain.py:508-525). The readers of rank are all report-only and there are now five, all reached through `BlockStatus.short`: the open-result reason string (src/holo/receiver/format/close.py:471-488), the diag close row's "blocks ready N/M" (src/holo/receiver/diag/readout.py:175), the shortfall line and its worst-block pick (:337-360), the JSON status row (:500-505) and the viewer shell (src/holo/receiver/diag/server.py:287-290).
Options. Keep holo3's / restore the exact rank (pays an elimination per unsolved open group per consult) / keep the floor and label it honestly in the reason string.
Recommendation. Keep holo3's, and say the sentence out loud in the ruling and in the operator string: holo3's 'needs N more symbols' is a lower bound; v1's was exact - and since the column-group merge that lower bound can read 0 on a block that cannot solve, which is the case the operator string most needs to stop implying a count.
Record the coupling to close-08 - v1's provable-skip argument needed the exact rank, holo3's more eager consult predicate does not, so if holo3 ever tightens close_is_new toward v1's baseline it must restore the exact rank first.
If the honest label is wanted now, the discriminating unit test already exists (test_h3_format_column_groups.py:425-453); only the label is owed, and it costs no run.
[Corrected 2026-09-05: `test_h3_format_column_groups.py:425-453` does NOT record a rank — its rank assertions iterate an empty `decoder._rank` and pass vacuously; the pin that really records one is the count-gate case at :471-506 (a monkeypatched elimination returning k_b−1). The discriminating case — 129 rows per group, rank 128 standing until one new row in the group clears it — was reproduced by hand on the tip and is NOT yet a test.]

**Ruling:**

### `close-13` — D16 post-solve surplus audit — certified symbols of SOLVED blocks that disagree with the reconstruction

*Classification ABSENT · yield integrity · map: `HOLO1-MAP.md` → `close-13` · brief: `PORT-BRIEFS/d16-surplus-audit.md`*

What it does. After a solve, re-encodes every certified symbol of every SOLVED block from the solved sources and names the certificates that disagree. It changes no outcome; it is the only mechanism that localizes a wrong certificate to a tile without ground truth.
What v1 does. _surplus_audit runs on every solve and attaches CloseSurplusInconsistency rows to the CloseResult (src/holo/receive/fountain_close.py:557-594); it is wrapped in a bare except that returns () on any failure (:581-593), is never consulted by status, payload or the durable record, and is printed by the CLI epilogue on both the verified and the non-verified path (src/holo/cli.py:733, :743).
Its kernel is Decoder.audit_solved_symbols (src/holo/core/fountain.py:803; was :969 before core/fountain.py was split on 2026-09-04 — the method itself is unchanged).
What holo3 does. Absent. No audit surface in src/holo/receiver/format/close.py, no event carries one, and the word appears in holo3's production tree nowhere.
Evidence. Zero wrong certificates is the campaign's currency - 69,066,304 audited cells on the v1 side (docs/COLOR-UPLIFT-MEASUREMENTS.md:3029) and zero wrong across holo3's five completed crop batteries (ARTEFACTS.md 1.12) and the yask battery (ARTEFACTS.md 2.8). Every one of those audits was run by an external instrument against ground truth, not by the receiver.
Yield. None to date, and none possible on the archived corpus: the audit only speaks after a block solves, and every solve in the record verified. Its value is insurance - on a real sheet with no ground truth it is the only wrong-certificate detector holo3 would have, since the store's gates catch only a same-tile conflict or a metadata-bit conflict, and the envelope SHA fails the whole volume without naming anything.
Options. Keep the loss / port now / archive for later port as an optional diag pass, off by default (the conversion spec's own recommendation, 05-fountain.md 8 Q1).
Recommendation. Archive for later port, with a brief. It changes no outcome, so it must not delay the retirement; but it is the only localizer, and it should be cashed AFTER close-06's fix, because the path it exists to serve currently kills the run before the audit could be printed.

**Ruling:**

### `close-17` — Close detach — reopening a terminal state so accumulation can continue

*Classification ABSENT · yield operational · map: `HOLO1-MAP.md` → `close-17`*

What it does. Reopens an already-VERIFIED archived session so it can keep taking captures and certificates - the e033/e034 sheet-I workflow: close once, keep reading, close again.
What v1 does. detach_close is the deliberate inverse of apply_close: it removes the terminal record so the session is OPEN again, reachable only through --no-close (src/holo/receive/session.py:662-674, reached from src/holo/receive/runtime.py:464-472, announced at src/holo/cli.py:512-516).
Its justification is stated in the code: a close is a pure derivation from the certificate set, so a later closing run recomputes it and re-verifies byte-exactly.
What holo3 does. Nothing, and it needs nothing. The snapshot holds facts including Certificates but no close record (src/holo/receiver/snapshot.py:49-58), so a resumed run is always open and re-derives the close. The reopening v1 has to perform explicitly is holo3's default state.
Evidence. The sheet-I four-run resume chain: 5,543 -> 5,866 -> 5,866 -> 5,880 certificates across three VERIFIED closes over the same payload, one of them on a run that followed a run that had already closed (ARTEFACTS.md:235; experiments/034-sheet-i-full-readout.md:28-33).
Yield. None. The capability is preserved; only the explicit gesture disappears.
Options. Accept the loss (recorded) / build a detach for a record holo3 does not keep - which is not a coherent option.
Recommendation. Accept the loss. Record the reasoning so the doctrine is not re-derived later, and carry the load-bearing sentence - 'a close is a pure derivation from the certificate set' - as a comment against holo3's close consult in the comment pass.
It is the reason holo3 needs neither a durable close nor a detach, and it is the sentence that makes close-10, close-17 and close-18 all follow at once.

**Ruling:**

### `close-18` — The durable close record and its binding to the certified frontier

*Classification ABSENT · yield integrity · map: `HOLO1-MAP.md` → `close-18`*

What it does. Makes a state file's recorded close trustworthy. v1 stamps every terminal close with a canonical digest over the certificate identities it was derived from, refuses to apply or load a record that does not reproduce from those certificates, and refuses any transition out of a terminal close.
What v1 does, part 1. close_record_from_result stamps every terminal record with certificate_frontier_sha256, a canonical digest over the sorted certificate identities (src/holo/receive/fountain_close.py:829-863).
What v1 does, part 2. apply_close refuses a record whose frontier disagrees with the state, and refuses any transition out of a terminal close (src/holo/receive/session.py:642-659, :677-690).
What v1 does, part 3. validate_session RECOMPUTES close_volume from the frontier on every ordinary load and raises if the record does not reproduce (session.py:693-735); SessionIndex refuses a terminal close whose digest disagrees (:284-288); _replay_deferred_close raises when a retained record does not reproduce (runtime.py:384-394).
What holo3 does. All of it is absent by ruling. holo3 persists facts only and recomputes the close from scratch on every run, so there is no record to bind, nothing to reproduce on load, and no immutability to enforce - the recomputation IS the check (src/holo/receiver/snapshot.py:20-32, :49-58).
Evidence, v1 side. The frozen holo1 certificate JSONs still carry close{certificate_frontier_sha256, payload_sha256, payload_length, status} for the archived states (src/holo/receiver/docs/retirement_docs-2026-09/holo1-certificates/*.json; ARTEFACTS.md 1.1-1.2).
Evidence, holo3 side. Every close in the campaign record is byte-exact where it ran - five of five completed volumes recovered their payload sha exactly (src/holo/receiver/docs/conversion_docs/PARITY.md:446, not :441).
Yield. None. No certificate changes. What genuinely leaves with the tree is the ability to AUDIT the 28 archived .state files' close records - which is ARTEFACTS G-01, already answered by the Phase-0.3 certificate freeze.
Options. Accept the loss / reintroduce a durable close record and its binding - which nothing in the record asks for / carry a cheap frontier version integer, as the catalogue suggested for holo2.
Recommendation. Accept the loss, recorded rather than ported. The cheap idiom the catalogue proposed does not transfer: it presupposed a durable CloseRecord, and holo3 keeps none, so the choice is recompute or reintroduce. Recompute is what holo3 does, and it is the same computation the frontier digest was checking - run more often, not less.

**Ruling:**

### `close-20` — Certificate→close-input conversion and its mint-once memo

*Classification CARRIED-DIFFERENT · yield operational · map: `HOLO1-MAP.md` → `close-20`*

What it does. Governs how much work each close consult spends turning stored certificates into the close's input.
What v1 does. Durable TileCertificateRecords are reconstructed into CertifiedStreamSymbol by certificate_for_close, which re-runs the record's re-encode integrity gate and derives the tile counter (src/holo/receive/records.py:182-201).
Because that is expensive and records are content-addressed and immutable, ProductionClose memoises by certificate_id, so the conversion runs once per certificate rather than once per consult (src/holo/receive/production_close.py:37-50).
What holo3 does. The store already holds close-ready data.Certificate rows - user_bits[0] is the metadata bit, [1:] the symbol - so there is no conversion and no memo. What remains is a sort: tuple(self._certificates[key] for key in sorted(self._certificates)) (src/holo/receiver/engine.py:602-604).
Two corrections to the map. The sort is inside the `if solve:` branch, so it is paid per solve ATTEMPT, not per consult - the count gate and close_is_new make solves much rarer than consults. And the store already has the memo: certificate_keys() at src/holo/receiver/store.py:368-372 caches the sorted key tuple keyed on the certificate count, and has a live caller at src/holo/receiver/plugins/fields.py:749. The close consult just does not call it.
Evidence. UNPROVEN in the sense that no artefact reports holo3's consult CPU on a real sheet - but EngineStat.consult_cpu_s is instrumented in every run (src/holo/receiver/engine.py:173, :578-584), so the number is in every existing report.json and needs no new run. The nearest comparable is holo2's 1,358 consults = 34.1 s = 6.0% of wall, linear at about 30 microseconds per certificate (ARTEFACTS.md 2.5).
Yield. None on either side. Neither the memo nor the sort touches which certificates exist or which tiles are attempted.
Options. Keep holo3's / point the consult at certificate_keys() (one line) / measure first by reading consult_cpu_s out of an existing report.json (zero cost).
Recommendation. Keep holo3's - a one-line ruling. If the consult ever shows up in a run's accounting, the fix is one line pointing at the memo the store already carries; read consult_cpu_s from any existing report.json before touching it.
Keep the cross-family pointer in the notes: v1 re-ran the certificate's re-encode gate at close time and holo3 does not, because in holo3 that gate lives at mint - whether it is equally strong there is the tiles family's question, not this one.

**Ruling:**

### `close-21` — Certificate↔counter binding check at the close boundary

*Classification ABSENT · yield integrity · map: `HOLO1-MAP.md` → `close-21`*

What it does. Refuses, at the close boundary, a certificate that does not belong: one whose stored counter addresses a different tile, one naming a stream the printed row does not declare, and one whose symbol shape does not match the stream profile.
What v1 does. _validate_close_certificate re-derives spec.tile_counter(page, ty, tx) and refuses a disagreeing stored counter, refuses an undeclared stream via spec.stream_profile, and checks the symbol shape; _build_decoder additionally refuses a foreign stream on a one-stream close (src/holo/receive/fountain_close.py:106-124, :116, :342, :343-344). Every certificate is validated exactly once per close.
What holo3 does. The counter check has nothing to check: _tile_counter derives the counter from (page, ty, tx) at use (src/holo/receiver/format/close.py:157-159, was :141-143), so the two can never disagree. That half is unrepresentability, which is the stronger form.
But two refusals moved rather than vanished. close_volume's multi-stream branch silently DROPS a foreign-stream certificate from every decoder while still counting its metadata equation (src/holo/receiver/format/close.py:595-603 against :183-193), and its single-stream branch loads every certificate regardless of stream_id (:578-581).
On a pooled row an undeclared stream raises a bare KeyError from idx_of (:184-185, :557-558) rather than a named refusal. The named guard now lives one object away, in CloseProgress._ingest (:404-409).
The symbol-shape check is gone; a wrong-width array fails later inside the decoder's packing.
RE-DERIVED 2026-09-04 after the column-group merge (`2d56e5a`): the three paragraphs above describe deleted code, and the recommendation they drove is ALREADY SATISFIED. `close_volume` now routes its certificates through `CloseProgress.absorb` (src/holo/receiver/format/close.py:548-556), so `_ingest`'s named undeclared-stream refusal (:317-320) fires at the function boundary and the wrapper returns `CloseResult('integrity_failure', reason="...certified stream 'ghost' is not declared by the printed row (('main',))")` - measured on the rebased tip, pinned at src/holo/receiver/tests/test_h3_close_state.py:186-192. The two branches cited (:595-603, :578-581) were merged into one per-stream comprehension and no longer exist. The SYMBOL-SHAPE check is BACK, not gone: `Decoder.add_symbol` refuses a symbol whose bit count is not its stream's declared width, with a named error (src/holo/receiver/format/fountain.py:382-395), because a column-group pool's streams have different widths and the width can no longer be inferred from the code. What SURVIVES of this entry: the counter half is still unrepresentable (`_tile_counter`, src/holo/receiver/format/close.py:157-159), the ABSENT classification stands, and `metadata_from_certificates` still indexes `index_of[cert.stream_id]` unguarded (:506-509), so calling THAT function directly with a foreign stream still raises a bare KeyError - it is a test-only entry point.
[Corrected 2026-09-05: `metadata_from_certificates` is not test-only — `analyzer/inputs.py:256` and `analyzer/close.py:391` call it in production; the bare KeyError is unreachable from there because the analyzer refuses non-cmy8 rows first (`analyzer/inputs.py:298`), so the disposition stands but the consumer list did not.]
Evidence. Behaviour only - the conversion doc records the drop (05-fountain.md 2.5 item 16, 5 DROP) and no artefact exercises any of it. Production is equal: certificates are minted with the row's own stream ids (src/holo/receiver/plugins/tiles.py:351) and _ingest refuses an undeclared stream before close_volume ever sees it.
Yield. None on the archived corpus. Nothing can produce a certificate carrying a stream the row does not declare, so no certificate changes either way.
Options. Accept the loss / add a named refusal in close_volume for an undeclared stream (a few lines, no run) / accept and record the relocation precisely.
Recommendation. Accept the loss, but record it as the refuter states it rather than as 'unrepresentability replaced the check': one of the three refusals became unrepresentable, one moved to CloseProgress, and one (symbol width) is now carried by the tile codec at mint and by array shapes at load.
RE-DERIVED 2026-09-04: the second option - 'give close_volume a named refusal for an undeclared stream' - was DONE by the column-group merge, not as a response to this entry but as a consequence of routing close_volume through CloseProgress; and the symbol-width refusal came back as a named error in the decoder. The ruling is now a one-liner: record the relocation precisely, accept the counter-binding loss, and note that both residuals closed themselves.

**Ruling:**

---

## 6. Scheduling

### `scheduling-06` — Refinement levels own their OWN evidence namespace (only certificates cross lanes) vs in-place evidence supersession

*Classification CARRIED-DIFFERENT · yield decides-certificates · map: `HOLO1-MAP.md` → `scheduling-06` · brief: `PORT-BRIEFS/refinement-evidence-namespacing.md`*

1. What it does: decides whether a re-read of a window is allowed to destroy the read it was supposed to improve on, and whether the refined and unrefined reads of one window can both be used.
2. What v1 does: a refinement level binds the base level's transform and committed poses but keeps its OWN evidence namespace (src/holo/receive/cascade.py:319-336, src/holo/receive/types.py:259-267). Only certificates cross lanes, never soft evidence. The base read survives whatever the refinement decides, and everything it could still buy survives with it.
3. What holo3 does: an adopted revision folds as a refine-route Placement (src/holo/receiver/plugins/refine.py:512-514) through src/holo/receiver/store.py:787-801 and :632ff into src/holo/receiver/store.py:702-748, which replaces the standing WindowEvidence with no quality test at all. The pose is unrecoverable too - src/holo/receiver/data.py:118-120 ranks any refine-route pose above the base pose on its first term, quality untested. What does survive is certificates already minted: src/holo/receiver/store.py:893-919 is append-only and raises on a conflicting bit.
4. The evidence: the overwrite has never fired on paper. Across the five completed post-change volumes `refine` fired 717 times, `refine.color` 3,899 and `revisit` 593, producing zero route=refine poses (src/holo/receiver/docs/conversion_docs/PARITY.md:445, ARTEFACTS.md §2.3). The one overwrite path that IS live - revisit - is harmless by construction, since it only re-calibrates a window whose evidence was refused (src/holo/receiver/plugins/revisit.py:50-52).
5. Yield: decides-certificates, but latent. Nothing on the archived corpus turns on it today. It becomes live the first time refinement adopts, which the debt rows at src/holo/receiver/docs/SCHEDULE.md:105-124 exist precisely to make happen, and at that moment v1's protection is simply gone with the tree.
6. Options: keep holo3's; port the namespace now; archive for later port; accept the loss; or a costed measurement.
7. Recommendation: ARCHIVE FOR LATER PORT (brief below). Porting now buys nothing measurable - zero adoptions everywhere - so it does not earn a pre-retirement work package; but this is the one row in the family where holo3 can silently LOSE something v1 kept, and the capability should not leave with the tree unwritten.
8. Buy one tripwire now, at test-lane cost: the colour sim refinement lane already adopts (src/holo/receiver/docs/SCHEDULE.md:426-436, 14 fires / 2 adoptions), so assert there that the pre-adoption certificate set is retained across an adoption. No receiver run.

**Ruling:**

### `scheduling-07` — Tail class rank M > R > U vs the 200 / 210-220 / 230-240 price bands

*Classification CARRIED-DIFFERENT · yield decides-order-only · map: `HOLO1-MAP.md` → `scheduling-07`*

1. What it does: guarantees that each class of second-chance work runs after everything that could still shrink what it reads - mop-up before refinement, refinement before the supplemental colour arms.
2. What v1 does: one class rank in the tail loop, `lane_class = -1 uplift, 0 refine, 1 mopup` taken descending (src/holo/receive/runtime.py:742-761), so all mop-up levels of all captures run, then all refinement levels, then all supplemental lanes.
3. What holo3 does: the same order emerges from price literals - revisit / refine / refine.color all flat 200, tiles.uplift 210 and 220, tiles.local 220, tiles.planek 230, tiles.swim 240 (src/holo/receiver/docs/SCHEDULE.md:70-80), with subscriptions re-arming a row as the frontier grows instead of running it once at the right moment.
4. Correction to the row as written: only six rows re-price by an ordinal (colour refit, color.pooled_ctx, fields, tiles.uplift, tiles.planek, tiles.swim). `tiles.local` is flat (src/holo/receiver/plugins/local_ctx.py:597-599) and so are refine and revisit.
5. Two order inversions are real and stand: revisit and refine now interleave at 200 where v1 ran all mop-up first; and refinement (200) now always precedes cold (250/260) where v1 ran cold inside each level's address stage.
6. The evidence: the sharpest measured comparison in the record is v1 winning 18 of its 1,930 xerox-K certificates by refinement, all in column 0; holo3 certified 13 of those with no refinement at all, and the 5 it missed are 5 of its 8 misses (src/holo/receiver/docs/conversion_docs/GAPS.md:82-107). GAPS.md:377-381 records the attribution as inference, not measurement.
7. Yield: order-only, and the five tiles are not this row's doing. holo3's refine subscribes to every tile key in its own reach (src/holo/receiver/plugins/refine.py:622-634) and retires only when exhausted (:648-656), so it re-fires on a growing frontier and does see the maximal one; and both refinement rows fired heavily (717 / 3,899 fires, PARITY.md:445). What did not happen is adoption, which is the refinement family's row, not this one.
8. Options: keep holo3's; port the class rank; archive; accept the loss; or a costed measurement.
9. Recommendation: keep holo3's, and hand the five-tile gap to the refinement family rather than paying for it here. If someone wants ordering exonerated on the record, the instrument is a fire/refusal event on the refinement rows plus a re-run of the existing xerox-K crop battery - 5 cases, 7 targets, holo3 wall 145.4 s, peak RSS under 2.22 GiB (ARTEFACTS.md §1.12). No sheet decode.

**Ruling:**

### `scheduling-11` — Supplemental (uplift) work bound to the FINEST ordinary rung vs one uplift subject per contributing lane

*Classification CARRIED-DIFFERENT · yield decides-certificates · map: `HOLO1-MAP.md` → `scheduling-11`*

1. What it does: decides through which resolution the residue - the page's hardest tiles - is re-read by the supplemental colour arms.
2. What v1 does: the colour tail appends exactly one lane per applicable arm, all bound to the finest ordinary rung of that capture (src/holo/receive/cascade.py:337-365; docs/RECEIVE-DESIGN.md:951-963). The stated reasons: the residue deserves the best evidence the capture has, and the finest rung is the only capture-independent choice, so the supplemental form keeps no tuning surface.
3. What holo3 does: `_uplift_subjects` walks the failed attempt's own components and mints a subject for EACH contributing lane (src/holo/receiver/plugins/uplift.py:290-301); the rung enters only as a price premium (:325-331). Reach is strictly WIDER - a residue tile attempted from coarse-lane evidence arms an uplift job at that coarse lane too - and v1's finer-is-better law survives as a price ordering rather than a restriction.
4. The evidence, v1 side: the supplemental tail buys 24 certificates across six volumes (0/2/6/15/1/0) for 0% to +31% of the archived ladder (ARTEFACTS.md §2.9 -> docs/COLOR-UPLIFT-MEASUREMENTS.md:3172-3177). But certificates carry no lane marker, so even v1's own 15 on xerox c2-cmy7 is an upper bound, not an attribution (ARTEFACTS.md G-22; GAPS.md:255-269).
5. The evidence, holo3 side: two different zeros. On both sims the row fires ZERO times because its price puts it last and the page is finished first (ARTEFACTS.md §2.8; src/holo/receiver/docs/SCHEDULE.md:146-149). On paper it appears in no certs-by-plugin breakdown of the four-run sheet-I chain (ARTEFACTS.md §1.8).
6. Yield: decides-certificates. This is the one row in the family where v1 has a measured yield and holo3 has an observed zero, and the parity runs predate the row so no head-to-head exists.
7. Options: keep holo3's; port the finest-rung binding; archive; accept the loss; or a costed measurement.
8. Recommendation: COSTED MEASUREMENT, in two steps, the first of which is free. Step one: read `tiles.uplift` job counts out of the five completed volumes' existing report.json plugin_rows (ARTEFACTS.md §2.9 says every report carries them) and settle whether the row fires on paper at all - zero artefact cost, no run. Step two, only if it does not fire: instrument the row with a fire/refusal event and re-run the existing xerox c2-cmy7 crop battery, the volume where v1's tail bought its 15 - 20 cases, 57 targets, holo3 wall 3,773.6 s, batch 20,412.8 s, peak RSS under 2.22 GiB (ARTEFACTS.md §1.12). No full-sheet decode either way.
9. If step one shows the row never fires, the finding is a PRICING ruling inside holo3 (210/220 plus rung puts it behind everything and a preempting close cancels it), not a port of v1's plan machinery.

**Ruling:**

### `scheduling-13` — Supplemental arm membership and plan order: off, a3, a4b, field

*Classification CARRIED-DIFFERENT · yield decides-certificates · map: `HOLO1-MAP.md` → `scheduling-13`*

1. What it does: decides which supplemental corrections are attempted on the residue and in what order - specifically whether the pre-campaign baseline gets its own residue pass, and whether the plane-field read is an arm at all.
2. What v1 does: `UPLIFT_ARMS = ('off','a3','a4b','field')` is a frozen plan order and the module states twice that the order is load-bearing (src/holo/receive/lane_uplift.py:33-74, cascade.py:355-365, color_field.py:119): `off` runs FIRST so a ladder-configuration change cannot strand a tile the old path would have certified, and `field` runs LAST because it consumes what the others mint - its strongest evidence tier is the certified-tile probe.
3. What holo3 does: `UPLIFT_ARMS = ('a3','a4b')` priced 210 and 220, so v1's relative order survives (src/holo/receiver/plugins/color_arms.py:70-72, plugins/uplift.py:91-95). There is no `off` arm, because the arms are override-only and the standard rows are the unarmed path. There is no `field` arm either: the plane field is its own row at 180 x fire ordinal, dial-gated, read by the colour lane, refine.color, tiles.planek and tiles.swim through the standing cell (src/holo/receiver/plugins/fields.py:60, :804-806).
4. Yield, the `off` half: order-only and structurally unnecessary. holo3 has no ladder-configuration change to strand a tile across, and its unarmed rows re-fire by subscription. Nothing to insure, nothing lost.
5. Yield, the `field` half: certificate-bearing, and the numbers point opposite ways. v1's field arm on sheet I took 5,297 -> 5,549 certificates (+252, Y 1,444 -> 1,629) and closed byte-exact, though its pre-registered dead-band bar FAILED at 24.0/53 against 5/53 (ARTEFACTS.md §2.8 -> docs/PLANE-FIELDS.md:915-921). holo3's fields row contributed nothing on the best-ever sheet-I run: 54 fits, 5,211 CPU-s, ZERO PlaneField facts in the snapshot (src/holo/receiver/docs/conversion_docs/MEASUREMENTS-2026-08-31.md:28-35).
6. But that comparison is not this family's to adjudicate. Whether the field pays is the plane-fields line's question; what THIS row owns is only that the field stopped being an arm with a guaranteed ordering and became a priced row with a certificate subscription.
7. Options: keep holo3's; port the `off` arm; port the field's run-last guarantee; archive; accept the loss; or a costed measurement.
8. Recommendation: keep holo3's for arm membership and order, and cross-reference the field half to the fields/plane-fields family with the two numbers above attached, so nobody re-derives them. One free check belongs with that hand-off: read the `fields` row's fire counts and PlaneField fact counts out of the existing report.json files rather than assuming the sheet-I zero generalises.

**Ruling:**

### `scheduling-18` — Determinism of the certificate set under scheduling order and worker count

*Classification DIVERGED · yield integrity · map: `HOLO1-MAP.md` → `scheduling-18`*

1. What it does: decides whether two runs of the same captures on the same tree certify the same tiles.
2. What v1 does: `--workers` is declared execution-only - "results are bit-identical for any value" (src/holo/cli.py:960-965). Parallelism lives inside stage kernels and never in the schedule, and the worker count never enters a policy id (src/holo/receive/_parallel.py:1-16, cascade.py:623-625). The schedule is a deterministic function of the plan and the retained prefix, so the certificate SET is reproducible.
3. What holo3 does: workers ARE the schedule - N threads pop the frontier and every job consults the store at fire time (src/holo/receiver/engine.py:184-211, :433-475). src/holo/receiver/docs/PAGE-DOMAIN.md:175-182 states the consequence plainly: fire-time floors on a growing live set make intermediate outcomes schedule-dependent, so certificate sets may differ across worker counts. What is kept instead is zero-wrong (certificates are proof-carrying), C-12's letter (payload bytes identical across `--workers N`) and convergence.
4. The doors that make the set order-dependent: (a) the race-mode close breaks the run and cancels in-flight work (scheduling-17); (b) fire-time eligibility floors read a live page set that grows during the run - `cold_flavour_pages` partitions the domain by `live_pages` at fire time (src/holo/receiver/plugins/place.py:121-125) and the support floors at :62-79 are consulted the same way, re-armed by the live-pages wake (PAGE-DOMAIN.md:145-152); (c) retire is terminal for most rows; and (d) the version floor at src/holo/receiver/engine.py:446-449 and the price re-push at :456-463, both fire-time refusals with no v1 analogue - this last one is unowned by any row in the family and should be adopted here.
5. The evidence: on sims the certificate signature is identical at workers 1 and 8, twice independently (ARTEFACTS.md §1.11, d6-baseline k/color w1/w8 JSON; and again after the endgame-feeder compaction, src/holo/receiver/docs/SCHEDULE.md:601-642). On real sheets NOTHING has been measured - holo3's `--workers 8` multiple on a real sheet is nowhere recorded and the acceptance bar of at least 3x has never been measured (ARTEFACTS.md G-07).
6. Yield: integrity. It does not lose or win tiles by itself; it decides what a certificate count MEANS. Every holo3 certificate count in the record is "a" certificate set, not "the" one, and that sentence belongs in the map.
7. Options: keep holo3's; port the determinism guarantee; archive; accept the loss; or a costed measurement.
8. Recommendation: keep holo3's - the narrowing is ruled, documented and deliberate, and the replacement invariants (payload-byte identity plus zero-wrong) are the ones that actually protect the archive. Record explicitly that v1's stronger guarantee leaves with the tree, and that G-07 means no real-sheet evidence exists on either the identity or the speed side.

**Ruling:**

---

## 7. The colour stack

### `colour-stack-08` — A5's deterministic 40,000-row stride subsample of the fit and gate populations

*Classification DIVERGED · yield decides-certificates · map: `HOLO1-MAP.md` → `colour-stack-08`*

What it does: caps how many labelled cells each context curve is fitted and gated on. v1 takes a deterministic every-k-th stride above 40,000 rows; holo3 uses the whole population.
What v1 does: src/holo/receive/color_context.py:132 sets the cap, :551 strides the fit side, :566-567 strides the gate side, and :552-555 can still refuse a curve whose classes were thinned by the stride. Its own reason is bounded cost — 'a late lane's millions of labels would make one region's fit the run's CPU peak' (:126-132).
What holo3 does: no cap and no stride anywhere in the module — ContextConfig has no such field (src/holo/receiver/plugins/color_region_ctx.py:49-56) and _fit_one_curve indexes the whole row set (:359-401).
Note holo3 KEPT the equivalent cap in the conditioned-marginal arm (src/holo/receiver/plugins/color_conditioning.py:98, :402-403), so this is a deliberate, arm-specific removal.
Why it decides certificates: on a real page every curve is fitted and gated on a different, much larger population than v1 used, so every adopted offset differs.
Page scale is measured: 8,050,972 support cells per capture region (scratch/sheetI-20260831/report-first.json, capture_facts[].regions[].support_cells) — the regime v1's comment describes.
Evidence on cost, already on disk: sheet I's first run spent 17,135.7 CPU-s over 422 colour-lane fires (40.6 s per fire) out of 175,681 CPU-s total; the run's real peak is the local-refit row at 129,017 CPU-s (73%).
Colour fitting plus the pooled context row is 14.7% of the run (scratch/sheetI-20260831/report-first.json, scheduler.plugins and scheduler.churn). The uncapped fit did not become the CPU peak on the biggest colour sheet in the record.
Evidence on yield: none, and none is obtainable — v1's cap makes its own numbers depend on population size, so no bit-exact comparison exists in either direction.
The mechanism argument is mildly favourable: v1 could refuse a curve purely because the stride thinned a rare state (:552-555), a refusal holo3 cannot suffer, and every holo3 adoption still has to pass the same held-out gate on more data.
Options: keep holo3's uncapped fit; or port the cap; or run a measurement.
Recommendation: keep holo3's, and record the sheet-I cost numbers next to v1's cap comment so the fear is retired with a number rather than an opinion. No run, no measurement package.

**Ruling:**

### `colour-stack-11` — How the supplemental arms are reached and scheduled: v1's u-lane in the scale plan versus holo3's priced `tiles.uplift` row

*Classification CARRIED-DIFFERENT · yield decides-certificates · map: `HOLO1-MAP.md` → `colour-stack-11`*

What it does: schedules the two supplemental colour arms over the uncertified residue — v1 as extra lanes appended to the scale plan, holo3 as one priced, subscribed row.
What v1 does: one lane per arm, bound to the finest ordinary rung and ranked last so each sees the maximal frontier (src/holo/receive/cascade.py:355-367; src/holo/receive/runtime.py:742-761), reached only under --refine.
What holo3 does: one subject per (capture, level, page, arm), born from any failed attempt, eligible while the page has residue, priced at 210/220 times its own fire ordinal plus a rung premium (src/holo/receiver/plugins/uplift.py:289-300, :325-331).
More reach in principle — every rung, not just the finest — and it re-fires as the frontier grows.
Evidence, v1: the supplemental tail cost between 0% and 31% of the ladder and bought 24 certificates across six volumes (ARTEFACTS.md:717).
Evidence, holo3, and this is the finding: the row has never fired on paper.
Sheet I's four runs (all --complete, 302,422 CPU-s, three plus one captures) show no such row in scheduler.plugins in any of report-{first,resume,resume-2,resume-3}.json and no matching string in any of the four event logs; sheetH-maxstack-report.json and the overnight c2-cmy8 / c3-cmy7 event logs are the same.
The row was registered on that tree and its code is unchanged since.
Why it never fired is NOT recoverable from the record: a lane with no target tiles returns None from prepare (src/holo/receiver/plugins/uplift.py:258-266) and a None prepare writes no event, so 'priced out' and 'refused at prepare' look identical from outside.
Options: keep holo3's; or reprice the row; or accept that the supplemental arms are paper-dead in holo3 (which would make rows 12, 14 and 28 moot); or spend the cheap instrument that would tell us which.
Recommendation: keep holo3's shape and land colour-stack-25's verdict publication, which costs nothing at run time and turns this from unanswerable into answerable on the next colour run anyone makes.
Do not reprice on a guess: the delta at stake is at most the 24 certificates v1 measured corpus-wide, and it is realised at zero today.

**Ruling:**

### `colour-stack-12` — The supplemental BASELINE lane — the `off` arm, which re-reads the residue on the pre-campaign A0/A1 skeleton

*Classification ABSENT · yield decides-certificates · map: `HOLO1-MAP.md` → `colour-stack-12` · brief: `PORT-BRIEFS/supplemental-baseline-arm.md`*

What it does: a third supplemental arm that re-reads the residue through the pre-campaign detector path — no head, no context, no maps, no conditioning — so a tile the OLD configuration would have certified can still be recovered after a ladder change re-times the cascade and strands it.
What v1 does: src/holo/receive/color_stack.py:200 registers the arm and :602 collapses the ladder to the baseline skeleton for that lane; it is scheduled first among the arms (src/holo/receive/lane_uplift.py:74) and is monotone under the same per-address retirement law as the others.
What holo3 does: absent. arms_for_family returns at most two arms (src/holo/receiver/plugins/color_arms.py:94-99), and no other row offers the unarmed skeleton — the retry row re-runs belief propagation on the same evidence, and the local and swim rows re-fit the whole head including context.
Evidence: the arm's own record (docs/COLOR-UPLIFT-MEASUREMENTS.md:2352-2400) — 0 certificates for 108 s CPU on one capture, 0 for 686 s on another, 3 of which ONE was new for 392 s on the third; of six stranded addresses it recovered one; the pre-registered acceptance was NOT met (-2/-1/-2 against +255 gained).
Of the five misses, two were lost to footing (the lane binds one rung) and three to history (by the time it runs the frontier has grown, so the 'pre-campaign' model is fitted on more labels than the pre-campaign run had).
What holo3 changes about that: the footing mechanism is fixed for free — holo3 arms one subject per capture-level, which is v1's own deferred candidate fix; the history mechanism is worse, because holo3 never barriers the frontier at all.
Options: port now (about 30 lines: a third arm plus a skeleton flag that skips head and context in finish_region_stack); archive for later port; accept the loss.
Recommendation: archive for later port, bundled with colour-stack-11.
Ported on its own it is worthless — it would live in a row that has never fired on paper.
If Germano prefers a hard close, 'accept the loss' costs at most the one-to-three addresses above, and that is a defensible ruling.

**Ruling:**

### `colour-stack-14` — What the supplemental arm's corrected evidence is: v1's durable lane-scoped evidence blocks through the full attempt ladder, versus holo3's transient in-job cut

*Classification CARRIED-DIFFERENT · yield decides-certificates · map: `HOLO1-MAP.md` → `colour-stack-14`*

What it does: decides whether an arm-corrected read of a tile is durable and poolable across captures, or transient to one job.
What v1 does: a supplemental lane emits ordinary durable evidence blocks in its own namespace, and its lane id is capture-independent, so two captures at the same footing land in the same lane and the attempt schedule runs a 60-iteration scan, then a union where two or more components exist, then a selective 150-iteration retry (src/holo/receive/production_color.py:327-392; src/holo/receive/payload_engine.py:210-302).
What holo3 does: the correction is built in the job, cut per tile from ONE capture-level, handed to the decoder as a single component and discarded (src/holo/receiver/plugins/uplift.py:149-255).
No evidence row is written, so no other row can ever union an arm-corrected read; the attempt goes straight to the heavy 150-iteration budget (src/holo/receiver/plugins/tiles.py:61-64, :114-116).
The two effects point opposite ways: holo3 spends fewer attempts per tile at a heavier budget, but can never sum two arm-corrected views of the same tile on a multi-capture footing — and unioning captures is where holo3's largest measured colour yield comes from elsewhere (sheet H: the union row minted 1,818 of 4,237 certificates, 43% — ARTEFACTS.md:621).
Evidence: no artefact separates a v1 supplemental UNION certificate from a supplemental SCAN one, so the lost fraction of v1's 24 corpus-wide arm certificates is unmeasured; and on holo3's side the row has never fired on paper at all (colour-stack-11), so the realised loss on the archived corpus is zero.
Options: keep holo3's; or make arm evidence durable; or record the limit and revisit if the arms ever earn a firing.
Recommendation: keep holo3's and record the lost cross-capture pooling as a named limit of the arm rows.
Transience is what makes the row cheap, provably monotone and safe to run at every level; durable arm evidence would reintroduce standing state for a mechanism that has not yet produced one certificate on paper.
Revisit only after colour-stack-11.

**Ruling:**

### `colour-stack-16` — `--force-null` / HOLO_COLOR_STACK_FORCE_NULL — the contract that makes the receiver run exactly its pre-campaign colour path

*Classification ABSENT · yield integrity · map: `HOLO1-MAP.md` → `colour-stack-16`*

What it does: one switch that makes the receiver run exactly its pre-campaign colour path, so a colour change can be proved to be a null by running the same tree with every campaign arm removed.
What v1 does: one environment read, per call, never cached (src/holo/receive/color_stack.py:91-101).
When set, the ladder collapses to the baseline skeleton (:207, :258-260, :602), the context variant is forced off, the seven-state head reports force_null, the demap budget drops to one (src/holo/receive/color_attempts.py:60-72) and the supplemental lanes are skipped entirely (src/holo/receive/production_color.py:361-368).
It can only REMOVE behaviour.
What holo3 does: absent, and deliberately (src/holo/receiver/docs/conversion_docs/11-colour-planes-ctx.md:181, :373; 10-colour-heads.md:418-421).
There is no environment read anywhere in holo3's production tree.
The nearest equivalents are per-arm: remove a row from the descriptor list, or pass --no-plane-fields / --no-pooled-color-context.
Evidence: v1's contract was exercised and clean — xerox c3-cmy7 720 of 720 certificate identities identical, zero wrong in 8,588,160 cells (ARTEFACTS.md:654), and the supplemental tail cost 0.0 s and emitted nothing under it (ARTEFACTS.md:189). holo3's replacement is per-row and equally hard: identical payload digest, identical certificate sets (49/49 and 279/279) and identical per-plugin job counts (ARTEFACTS.md:507-512).
What is genuinely lost: a WHOLE-STACK baseline. A future colour change can only be null-proved arm by arm; there is no single switch that returns the ladder to its pre-campaign shape.
Options: accept the loss; or add a flag (not an environment variable) that disables the whole colour ladder.
Recommendation: accept the loss. Deleting a descriptor row cannot be half-off, which is a stronger null proof than an environment hatch, and holo3's existence tests already run it.
Record in the map that whole-stack null proofs are no longer available, and that the comparison harness still pops the old variable.

**Ruling:**

### `colour-stack-24` — The cmy7 head's population-disjointness assertions: site keys unique, fit/validation disjoint, and neither overlapping the QDA's own training population

*Classification ABSENT · yield integrity · map: `HOLO1-MAP.md` → `colour-stack-24`*

What it does: four assertions that catch an upstream deduplication failure which would let the seven-state head be gated on the very cells it was fitted on — site keys unique, fit and validation disjoint, and neither overlapping the underlying model's own training population.
What v1 does: src/holo/receive/color_calibration.py:327-380 makes the model's training keys a REQUIRED keyword argument specifically so the disjointness is checked rather than assumed, mirroring the black-and-white path's contract, and raises on a violation because these are bugs, not channel facts.
What holo3 does: absent. fit_cmy7_head takes scores, states and site keys only (src/holo/receiver/plugins/color_model.py:548-584) and makes none of the four checks.
Why that is safe here, checked leg by leg: the owner selection emits one entry per (page, y, x) from a dictionary and returns the sorted keys (src/holo/receiver/plugins/color_region_head.py:168-183), so repeated keys are unrepresentable; the sub-split is a pure function of the key (src/holo/receiver/plugins/color_model.py:544-546), so the two sides cannot intersect; and the head's population and the model's training population are complementary slices of one mask (src/holo/receiver/plugins/color_region_head.py:205, :218-220, :249).
Evidence: no artefact records the check ever firing in v1 either — which is what the construction predicts, in both trees.
Options: port now (three assertions, or one uniqueness assertion where the deduplication happens); accept the loss and record the invariant; do nothing.
Recommendation: accept the loss, and record the invariant instead — one comment at src/holo/receiver/plugins/color_region_head.py:249 saying the head's population and the model's training population are complementary masks, carried in the comment pass rather than as a port package.
Porting the assertions would add code that asserts its own construction; the honest safeguard is the sentence that tells a future editor why the split is safe.

**Ruling:**

### `colour-stack-25` — Publication of the arm ladder's own verdicts: the A4 head reason and saturation, A3's per-window map reasons, A4b's per-stratum deltas, A5's curve records and the three region legs

*Classification ABSENT · yield operational · map: `HOLO1-MAP.md` → `colour-stack-25`*

What it does: publishes the ladder's own verdicts — why the seven-state head was refused or adopted and at what saturation, how many context curves survived and why the rest did not, and what each supplemental arm did per stratum.
It changes nothing in a decode; it is the record by which any future colour ruling could be made.
What v1 does: every arm returns a summary and the region loop emits it under named diagnostic keys (src/holo/receive/color_stack.py:317-371; src/holo/receive/color_conditioning.py:408-495; src/holo/receive/color_context.py:167-260), explicitly because a region refusal is spent by particular strata and one region number cannot name them.
What holo3 does: the records are still COMPUTED — the head reason, the saturation, the context verdict and a twelve-field arm verdict including per-stratum deltas — and then dropped.
The colour region job emits one event carrying only adopted plus a reason string (src/holo/receiver/plugins/color_region.py:316-318); the arm job binds its verdict and never reads it (src/holo/receiver/plugins/uplift.py:232-233).
Outside their defining modules those four names have one hit in the entire tree, a test fixture.
Evidence of the cost: certificates carry no lane marker, so no artefact can say which of a volume's misses an arm won (ARTEFACTS.md:829, gap G-22); and no artefact records the supplemental row firing on any real sheet — which, as colour-stack-11 shows, is because a lane with no targets returns None from prepare and writes no event at all.
Options: port now; archive for later port; accept the loss.
Recommendation: port now, in its cheapest form: put the head reason and saturation and the context verdict counters on the existing colour event (src/holo/receiver/data.py:399-404 is a plain event dataclass), and emit the arm verdict from the arm job.
Events, not facts — the fact snapshot and the resume schema stay untouched.
This is dead work being computed and discarded in the dearest colour row, and it is the instrument that makes four other rows in this family answerable on the next run anybody makes.

**Ruling:**

---

## 8. Plane fields, cold discovery, archive continuation, operator surface

### `fields-discovery-continuation-operator-01` — Per-plane registration-delta field estimator (the W1 core)

*Classification CARRIED-DIFFERENT · yield decides-certificates · map: `HOLO1-MAP.md` → `fields-discovery-continuation-operator-01`*

WHAT IT DOES. Estimates each colour plane's registration delta across the page from scattered measurements, so the colour head samples where that plane's ink actually landed.
V1. src/holo/receive/plane_fields.py:338/406/468 accumulates already-measured (dx,dy) deltas at tile-space positions, Gaussian-smooths them at bandwidth 2.0 tiles and returns a normal-normal posterior per plane about zero, with an uncertainty a caller can gate on. No cross-plane pooling (the law changed 2026-08-22, plane_fields.py:44-58).
HOLO3. src/holo/receiver/lib/plane_field.py:118/157/228 solves, rather than pools: each evidence cell is a rank-1 normal equation, binned per tile, smoothed at the same 2.0-tile bandwidth and solved against a ridge to zero, iterated four outer passes (src/holo/receiver/plugins/fields.py:97, :500-521) with photometry re-measured each pass. Tiers add exactly, so leave-one-out is a subtraction. Quality is a relative residual (lib/plane_field.py:260); there is no per-lookup uncertainty.
EVIDENCE, V1 SIDE. W6 sheet I: 5,297 -> 5,549 certificates (+252, Y 1444 -> 1629), close byte-exact, but the pre-registered dead-band bar FAILED at median 24.0/53 against <=5/53 (docs/PLANE-FIELDS.md:915-921), and the baseline is a different (August) tree, so part of +252 is unattributed drift (:931-943, ARTEFACTS.md G-18).
EVIDENCE, HOLO3 SIDE — NEW, and the number this ruling turns on. On the deepest real read on record (e034, three 2400 dpi captures, --plane-fields on), holo3 fitted 39 fields and published C on every one of them and M and Y on none: scratch/sheetI-20260831/report-resume.json (plane_fields[*].planes) and events-resume.jsonl.xz FieldFitted. Both refusals are the 0.25-dot publish floor, missed by 0.01 dot on M and 0.12 on Y.
So the honest claim is not 'holo3's field has never published on paper'. It is: it published on one plane of three, and no artefact separates its effect from the rest of the run, because there is no dial-off control on the same tree.
YIELD. With the flag off, inert. With it on, this decides where every colour cell of a published plane is sampled, and a publication wakes four rows that read it — so it can change which tiles certify on the archived corpus, and on sheet I it was live for C on every lane.
OPTIONS. (a) Keep holo3's estimator. (b) Port v1's posterior form. (c) Archive v1's form for later port. (d) Accept the loss of the per-lookup uncertainty. (e) Measure: a same-tree sheet-I pair, --plane-fields on and off, from the run-2 facts.npz — which is also the control G-18 says the v1 line itself owes. Run 3 of the e034 chain (the +0 control, same footing) cost 1,039 jobs and 4,193 cpu-s in ~8.5 minutes, so the pair is under an hour on holo3 and needs no new scan.
RECOMMENDATION. Keep holo3's (b and c buy nothing: v1's own paper result is confounded and its primary bar failed). Record the C-published / M,Y-refused fact in the map as the field line's real paper status, and schedule (e) as a holo3 run, not a v1 one — it costs minutes and it is the only thing that turns 'the field is on' into 'the field paid'.

**Ruling:**

### `fields-discovery-continuation-operator-05` — Handling of the plane-COMMON component of the measured field

*Classification DIVERGED · yield decides-certificates · map: `HOLO1-MAP.md` → `fields-discovery-continuation-operator-05`*

WHAT IT DOES. Decides whether the part of the measured displacement that all three planes share is carried into each plane's correction or removed from it.
V1. Keeps it. Each plane is estimated independently with a ridge to zero and no cross-plane pooling, so the shared part stays inside every plane's published delta (src/holo/receive/plane_fields.py:44-58). The shared part is measured separately per window and reported, never applied (src/holo/receive/color_field.py:229-262, :671).
HOLO3. Removes it. settle() subtracts the MEDIAN across the three planes and only then clamps (src/holo/receiver/plugins/fields.py:449-489). The median is chosen over the mean-of-others because both cancel a common bias exactly while the mean redistributes one plane's real displacement onto its neighbours — measured at (0,0,D) -> (-D/2,-D/2,D), 0.45-0.56 dots of spurious sibling publish (src/holo/receiver/docs/conversion_docs/ARMS-2026-08-31.md:46-52).
EVIDENCE AGAINST. docs/INDEPENDENT-PLANES.md:884-889 measured a DIFFERENTIAL field on paper — three archived 2400 dpi sheet-I captures, the W6 state, 334 open slots — and it lost: over the dead band, the plain field kept 1 certificate at 13.06% cell BER and the differential kept 0 at 13.70%. The doc's summary: the differential arms never reach the plain field (:944-949). This table is not in ARTEFACTS.md and should be added.
WHY IT DOES NOT SETTLE THE QUESTION. That arm subtracts the MEAN of the three planes (measure/plane_fields/_pose_apply.py:88-91). On a page where only Y is displaced — which is precisely sheet I's dead band, the measured Y x-registration sinusoid — the mean form returns two thirds of Y's correction and paints a third of it, with the wrong sign, onto C and M. holo3 subtracts the median, which returns (0,0,D) unchanged. The measurement refutes the form holo3 also rejects.
EVIDENCE FOR. v1's own pose writer, the mechanism that would USE the common part, measured negative on the same state: all 334 open slots unmoved, -5 certificates and +2.1 pp cell BER on the refit footing (docs/INDEPENDENT-PLANES.md:833-838, :906-910).
YIELD. On a page with a real plane-common bias the two receivers sample at different absolute offsets for every colour cell, so this changes which tiles certify whenever a field publishes. It also changes what the publish floor is measured on, which is how it reached sheet I: C published, M and Y did not.
OPTIONS. (a) Keep holo3's median subtraction. (b) Port v1's keep-the-common-part behaviour. (c) Make it a dial. (d) Measure: add one 'field-diff-median' configuration to the existing instrument (a one-line change at measure/plane_fields/_pose_apply.py:88-91) and re-run it over the same frozen W6 state — the whole six-arm sweep cost 3,400 s wall and 13.96 GB peak (docs/INDEPENDENT-PLANES.md:849-851), so a two-arm re-run is well under an hour. It runs on the retiring v1 tree, so it is a before-Gate-E item or nothing.
RECOMMENDATION. Keep holo3's median (a), and put the §2.11 table in the entry so the ruling is made with the contrary evidence in view. Do NOT let this row be recorded as 'no paper artefact contradicts it'. If Germano wants the question closed rather than reasoned, (d) is under an hour and is the last chance to ask the v1 instrument anything. Either way, fix the stale comment at src/holo/receiver/plugins/fields.py:74-93 during the comment pass — it describes a tree that no longer exists and calls a ruling open that the code has already made.

**Ruling:**

### `fields-discovery-continuation-operator-20` — Additive schema migration for a stored state

*Classification ABSENT · yield integrity · map: `HOLO1-MAP.md` → `fields-discovery-continuation-operator-20`*

WHAT IT DOES. Decides whether a state written by an earlier receiver can still be loaded, and whether the reader can tell when it should not be.
V1. A declared schema version with one additive migration table: a step supplies keys a later field set added, at their declared defaults, and never rewrites a stored value (src/holo/receive/session.py:67-79, src/holo/receive/state_codec.py:360-378). The stated reason is that the archive is why the policy gate was unlocked at all, so a state-shape change that orphaned it would defeat its own purpose.
HOLO3. No version stamp, no format guard and no integrity check, stated as a deliberate choice on the ground that a snapshot is an operator's own run product (src/holo/receiver/snapshot.py:19-22). _decode rebuilds each dataclass by name from its field list (:143-145), so a renamed family raises KeyError and a changed field set raises TypeError; and engine.py:249 folds the result without checking the format row.
DIAL. Always, on both sides: it governs every load, and neither side offers a flag to weaken or strengthen it.
EVIDENCE. The deepest read on record is a four-run resume chain costing about 84 cpu-hours, and e034 records no tree sha for any of its four runs (experiments/034-sheet-i-full-readout.md:172-174, ARTEFACTS.md G-31). The precedent for a cheap write-side guard exists and is pinned: compression is a write-side choice only, so plain snapshots written before 2026-08-30 still resume (src/holo/receiver/tests/test_h3_snapshot.py:132-140).
YIELD. Not per tile: a wrong-tree snapshot folds decisions, and decisions are not certificates. But certificate COUNTS are this project's evidence, and an unstamped resume can assemble one from two trees without saying so.
OPTIONS. (a) Keep the absence. (b) Port a full migration table. (c) Port a stamp only — a schema integer, the family-name set, the format ref and the run's tree string in the npz meta, refusing loudly and naming what moved. (d) Accept and rely on the close sha.
RECOMMENDATION. Port now, in minimal form (c). Not a migration table — a stamp. It is a few lines in snapshot.write and snapshot.read plus one test, it changes no decode, and it converts two silent failures into one named refusal. It is put to Germano rather than just done because the tree's own comment says the absence is deliberate, and because it is the only 'port now' this family asks for.

**Ruling:**

### `fields-discovery-continuation-operator-21` — Policy detach — continuing an archived state under today's receiver policy

*Classification ABSENT · yield integrity · map: `HOLO1-MAP.md` → `fields-discovery-continuation-operator-21`*

WHAT IT DOES. Decides which parts of an archived state may still be trusted after the receiver's own numerics have changed.
V1. The receiver policy id is a readable five-component composition — survey, address, payload, scheduler, close — with an exact inverse parser, so a differing id names exactly which semantic components moved (src/holo/receive/runtime_model.py:350, :366-367). The detach then derives the continuation at load, writing nothing: certificates, captures and the close are kept always; regions and poses unless their segment moved; proposals, losers and parked dropped when addressing moved; evidence blocks dropped when payload moved; the previous id appended to a lineage, with one warning and one event carrying the census (src/holo/receive/_runtime_validation.py:373-496). A moved survey segment, or an id that is not a five-component composition, is still refused.
HOLO3. Absent — no policy identity in the data model, the snapshot or the engine.
DIAL. Always, on the v1 side; there is nothing to dial on holo3's.
EVIDENCE. Segment granularity was coarse in practice: a change to the arm SCHEDULE alone dropped 6,098 pending blocks (docs/RECEIVE-DESIGN.md:1804-1805). On the holo3 side the run dir's tree file is the only receiver-provenance record and the snapshot carries none of it (ARTEFACTS.md Appendix A).
YIELD. The detach itself buys nothing in holo3 — there is no policy identity and no stored evidence to drop. The provenance it carried does matter, and it merges into row 20.
OPTIONS. (a) Keep holo3's absence entirely. (b) Port the policy id and the detach. (c) Keep the absence for the detach, and carry provenance only — the tree string in row 20's stamp — deciding whether a mismatch warns or refuses. (d) Accept the loss outright.
RECOMMENDATION. (c). The detach is structurally unnecessary in holo3: there is no policy identity to refuse, holo3's snapshot holds no evidence blocks to drop, and v1's own measured drop shows how coarse the mechanism was. What is genuinely lost is the GATE — holo3 will fold placements, calibration outcomes, pooled heads and plane fields from any tree, silently. Carry the tree string in row 20's stamp and rule on warn-versus-refuse there; do not port the policy machinery.

**Ruling:**

### `fields-discovery-continuation-operator-30` — The provisional-placement warning contract

*Classification CARRIED-DIFFERENT · yield integrity · map: `HOLO1-MAP.md` → `fields-discovery-continuation-operator-30`*

*(Assembled from the reader's row: neither the refutation pass nor the second lens reached this row.)*

1. What it does: Whether an operator is told that the receiver's placement constants are unqualified stand-ins.
2. What v1 does: The production composition marks its pipeline PROVISIONAL and every run emits a ProvisionalReceiverWarning naming the outstanding duties verbatim (the physical/null false-accept gate, the channel-BER envelope, the support safety margin, and the unqualified real-capture full-domain yield).
