The notes live beside the constants they qualify, the enum refuses a PROVISIONAL status with no notes and a QUALIFIED one with notes, and the decode subcommand's own help and description repeat the caveat (`src/holo/receive/__init__.py:1-10`, `src/holo/receive/placement/defaults.py:1-8`, `src/holo/receive/placement/defaults.py:31-38`, `src/holo/receive/observer.py:20-60`, `src/holo/receive/discovery.py:483-490`, `src/holo/cli.py:886-898`).
3. What holo3 does: The word PROVISIONAL is appended to the diag format status row, which the CLI prints to stderr after every decode. There is no warning class, no notes list, no help text and no structural coupling to the constants: place.py carries the same historical bars (E022_SUPPORT_FLOOR_SITES 30, the three gates, BER_ENVELOPE 0.08, SUPPORT_MARGIN_SITES 0) as bare constants, and if _status_rows falls into its except branch the word is not printed at all (`src/holo/receiver/diag/readout.py:53-60`, `src/holo/receiver/cli.py:110-133`, `src/holo/receiver/plugins/place.py:16-22`; diag status row `format`).
4. Evidence (n/a): holo3: the status row's PROVISIONAL suffix is pinned at test_h3_diag.py:334 (`src/holo/receiver/tests/test_h3_diag.py:334`); v1: receiver status PROVISIONAL: historical E021-E023c stand-in constants; the physical/null false-accept gate, BER envelope and support margins are not frozen (`CLAUDE.md 'Receiver status: PROVISIONAL' bullet`)
5. Yield: integrity
6. Options: keep holo3's behaviour; port now; archive for later port; accept the loss; run a costed measurement.
7. Recommendation: port now, in minimal form
8. Notes from the reading: This is the row Phase 4's 'CLAUDE.md PROVISIONAL bullet re-adjudicated for holo3' needs decided first. Minimal port: print the qualification line from the CLI itself rather than from the diag readout (so the except path cannot swallow it), and carry the four outstanding-duty notes next to place.py's constants as house-style comments. That is a comment carry plus three lines of CLI, and it changes no decode. Germano should also rule whether the status is still PROVISIONAL for holo3 at all, given e029/e032/e034 — that is a qualification question, not a code question.

**Ruling:**

---


# holo2 entries

### placement-01 — Consult reach at the cheap tier — which already-placed windows may steer an unplaced one

**DIVERGED** · evidence **MEASURED** · yield **decides-certificates** · classification trail: reader DIVERGED → refuter upheld → second lens DIVERGED

The mechanism: which already-placed windows may hand an unplaced one a starting geometry, at the cheapest rung of the placement ladder.

holo2 offers the eight lattice neighbours on the window's own scan footing plus windows on strictly coarser footings whose area contains this one. Information flows coarse to fine only, so the coarsest lane can only ever ask its own eight neighbours.

holo3 offers every placed window of the same capture and region, at any lane and any distance, and keeps the nearest eight by the same distance measure. Same sort, same keep-eight, same price.

Evidence: the only head-to-head that exists (four decodes of one 1000x1000-cell crop of the e029 HP sheet, two synthetic platen angles, each scan handed unchanged to both receivers) puts holo3 ahead on every row - 112 against 100 and 82 against 56 certificates, 155 against 196 and 281 against 397 unresolved windows, coarse-lane failure 37.1% against 54.6% and 58.3% against 81.6%, wall 842 s against 1,370 s and 786 s against 1,206 s. Zero wrong certificates on either side, and at 0.80 degrees holo3's certificate set is a strict superset of holo2's.

Independently: between 28% and 80% of every coarse-lane chain pose in four datasets came from an anchor holo2's rule would never have offered.

The cost of the same reach: one window fired 18 times against holo2's worst of 7, and the cheap rung fires 2.7 times per chain pose on the seven-capture sheet H stack.

Options: keep holo3's reach; port holo2's directional filter (measured worse on every row of the only comparison); or keep the reach and cap the repetition separately, which is the residue report's own recommendation.

Recommendation: keep holo3's. Record the repetition as a separate cost line, to be null-proved on certificates before any change lands.

Caveat to state out loud: the head-to-head is one crop of one sheet at one worker with a synthetic rotation. It is evidence, not a population, and the reach percentages are upper bounds on the loss because a window denied an anchor at one instant may be placed later by another.

**Ruling:**

---

### placement-03 — Ordering WITHIN a price class — the offered-seed-distance leg of the queue key (ruling R-A)

**DIVERGED** · evidence **UNPROVEN** · yield **decides-order-only** · classification trail: reader DIVERGED → refuter upheld → second lens DIVERGED

The mechanism: which of several equally-priced ready windows is worked next.

holo2 sorts within a price class by the best available seed distance, so the frontier grows along the most confident edges first, with an arrival counter to stop starvation.

holo3 has no distance term: within a price class it is first-in-first-out by last push, so a window next to a pose that just landed is worked at the same priority as one nowhere near any anchor.

Evidence: none isolates the leg. holo2's own census says it never had to arbitrate - the nearest offer won all 56 accepted contests, accepted rank 0 every time. The head-to-head with the leg absent has holo3 ahead on certificates. The residue report ranks this last of its seven findings.

Why it is mostly an ordering question: both receivers run to exhaustion by default, the anchor set only grows, and a consult attempt spends nothing permanent - so on a run that drains, order changes the path, not the destination.

The one place it can cost a certificate: a window whose pose fit reads too few supported sites is retired permanently in holo3, and how much support a fit reads depends on the region lattice seed standing when it fires. Fire too early and the window is gone. The measured size of that door is small - 4 windows retired in one arm of the head-to-head and none in the other.

Options: keep holo3's; port a distance leg; or keep holo3's and revisit only if the retirement rule changes.

Recommendation: keep holo3's and write the reopening brief. The measurement that would settle it is cheap and needs no new paper: one archived K sheet decoded twice without the complete flag, with and without a distance leg, on holo3 - minutes of machine time, and the comparison is certificates at the moment of close.

**Ruling:**

---

### placement-06 — Window subdivision — WHEN four corner windows are added, and what happens to the refused parent (the straddle-audit refusal path)

**DIVERGED** · evidence **UNPROVEN** · yield **decides-certificates** · classification trail: reader DIVERGED → refuter upheld → second lens DIVERGED

The mechanism: when a refused rectangle is retried as four smaller ones.

holo2 quarters a window only on four named refusals, and only once its ladder is empty. On a refusal from the straddle audit it deliberately does not quarter: it re-arms the window and lets it climb to its next unspent word rung instead.

holo3 quarters immediately on a failed pose fit, quarters AND re-pushes on a straddle-audit refusal (the union of both predecessors' behaviours), and on ladder exhaustion quarters whatever the refusal word was. It never quarters a window that read too few supported sites.

The exhaustion difference is narrower than it looks: the correlation rungs are last on the ladder, so a window that truly runs out has usually just been refused by correlation, which is inside holo2's permitted set. The genuine gap is the window whose correlation rung was never offerable and which therefore exhausts under some other word.

Evidence: quartering is measured as actively harmful on the coarsest lane. At two pixels per cell a 100-cell child reads a true 0.40-degree rotation as 0.56 - a 40% error - against 0.389 at 200 cells, because halving the window halves the frequency resolution while the printed cell frequency stays pinned at the sampling limit. In one run 1,150 of 1,448 flagged windows were such children. Against that, holo2's quartering arm reproduces the v1 receiver exactly (88 = 88), and no artefact counts how often the wider trigger fires.

Also on record: holo3's own written specification says it quarters only after a correlation refusal, which its code does not do. Whatever is ruled, one of the two must move.

Options: keep holo3's; restore holo2's refusal-word gate (the constants are already spelled out in holo3 and currently unused, so the change is small and null-provable); or keep the gate and additionally never quarter on the coarsest lane, which the frequency-resolution measurement argues for directly.

Recommendation: keep holo3's straddle behaviour, which loses nothing either predecessor would have tried, and rule to narrow the exhaustion branch. This is a receiver-side change with a cheap null proof, so it is a port-now candidate rather than a loss to accept.

**Ruling:**

---

### placement-08 — The parent's afterlife — a window whose four corners all failed arms COLD on its full extent

**CARRIED-DIFFERENT** · evidence **UNPROVEN** · yield **decides-certificates** · classification trail: reader CARRIED-DIFFERENT → refuter upheld → second lens CARRIED-DIFFERENT

The mechanism: what happens to a window after all four of its quarters have failed - it gets one more attempt, an area-wide correlation over its whole rectangle, because correlation wants area.

holo2 polls downward: after every settle it walks its list of quartered parents, and a parent whose children have all reported anything other than success is armed for correlation. A child that has merely gone to sleep counts as failed on purpose, so that a parent is never blocked forever.

holo3 is pushed upward from a child's last event: the parent is armed only when every child has actually retired. A child that failed its own pose fit goes to sleep instead of retiring, and wakes again whenever a neighbour's pose moves - so the parent's second chance waits until each child has spent its entire ladder, correlation included.

So the difference is not lost work, it is late work: holo3 runs the parent's area-wide correlation after all four children have run theirs, which is the most expensive ordering of the same attempts. Both trees allow the parent's attempt exactly once.

Evidence: no artefact isolates this on either side. What is measured is the value of the thing it arms - across the three archived whole-sheet runs, correlation committed zero poses in 5,332 fires while taking between 13% and 65% of every placement second. On the two fragmentary crops, by contrast, correlation earned 34 of 377 and 52 of 327 poses. So the parent's second chance is worth nothing where the address chain reaches and may be worth something on fragment material - which is exactly the material nobody has closed yet.

Options: keep holo3's; port holo2's asleep-counts-as-failed clause (a two-line change) to arm the parent earlier; or keep holo3's and revisit when there is fragment evidence.

Recommendation: keep holo3's and record the deferral in plain words, because on every whole sheet in the corpus it costs nothing and saves the most expensive attempt for last. Flag it for re-examination alongside the fragment work, where correlation is the only thing that has ever paid.

One more honest limit: neither tree records how often a parent's second chance actually fires, so the size of the difference is unknown in both directions; what is known is the value of what it arms.

**Ruling:**

---

### placement-10 — Cold's tried-set dedup granularity — per page, or per (flavour, page)

**DIVERGED** · evidence **UNPROVEN** · yield **decides-certificates** · classification trail: reader DIVERGED → refuter upheld → second lens DIVERGED

The mechanism: the bookkeeping mark that stops a window sweeping the same page twice with the correlation search.

holo2 keys that mark by page alone, deliberately - one window has one rung, and a page swept at that rung was swept whoever paid for it. So a page first swept as part of the not-yet-reachable set and later promoted to the reachable set is never swept again.

holo3 keys it by flavour and page, so a page swept once as unreachable is swept again once a decoded address makes it reachable.

Why the pairing matters: holo3's two flavours now carry different support floors, and the reachable flavour's floor is the lower one. Keying by page alone would therefore throw away a strictly more permissive attempt on the grounds that a stricter one had already failed.

Evidence: none, on either side. holo3's design document states the change as a consequence of the per-flavour floor; holo2's code comment states its own key as sound under one shared floor. Neither has a yield or cost number. What the corpus does say is that correlation committed zero poses on all three archived whole sheets, so on that material the extra sweep would have bought nothing and cost real time; on the two fragmentary crops correlation did commit poses.

Options: keep holo3's; port holo2's flavourless key (which would only be correct if the floors were unified again).

Recommendation: keep holo3's. It cannot remove an attempt, only add one, and it must move together with the per-flavour floor - the two are one change. Record the cost honestly: at most one extra sweep per window and page that gets promoted, on a rung that is already the most expensive on the ladder.

The rule this satisfies is the standing one about evidence passes: a change that only adds attempts and can never subtract one does not need a measurement to be safe, only to be worth its time.

**Ruling:**

---

### placement-11 — The declared hypothesis count a cold sweep's support floor is derived from

**DIVERGED** · evidence **MEASURED** · yield **decides-certificates** · classification trail: reader DIVERGED → refuter upheld → second lens DIVERGED

The mechanism: how strict the correlation search's support floor is - the number of readable sites a rectangle must show before an origin may be scored at all.

holo2 computes one floor, once, before any pixel is read, priced on the whole declared page domain, and hands the same floor to both flavours. Every sweep is therefore held to the strictest number.

holo3 computes a floor per flavour at fire time, priced on that flavour's own share of the domain. The reachable flavour's share is smaller, so its floor is lower and it admits origins the other flavour excludes. The formula and its three constants are unchanged; only the page count that enters it moves.

The part that needs saying out loud: this floor is used twice. It is the eligibility mask before the search, and it is also the acceptance bar the straddle audit applies to anything the correlation route places. So the lower floor loosens a test that admits addresses, not only a test that decides what is worth scoring.

Evidence: the floor values are pinned by tests - at a 200-cell window, 43 for one page, 48 for ten, 54 for the full 133-page set on the largest tile matrix, and about 54 to 56 for the full set on every matrix. The whole page-domain change that carries this was qualified on a real archived capture against its own single-page control and closed byte-exact both ways, 1,356 certificates at the default domain against 1,360 for the control.

This is the one place where holo3 made a floor looser rather than stricter. The design argues it is lawful because a flavour's share is declared before the sweep rather than derived from the pixels currently in hand, and because the unreachable complement stays priced at the full rate.

Options: keep holo3's; port holo2's single whole-domain floor (stricter, fewer attempts admitted); or keep the per-flavour floor for eligibility but hold the acceptance audit at the whole-domain floor - a middle option nobody has costed.

Recommendation: keep holo3's, and put the two-consumer fact in front of Germano explicitly rather than as a footnote, because a loosened acceptance bar is the kind of change the receiver's false-accept record is supposed to guard.

**Ruling:**

---

### placement-17 — Park / dormancy and the wake channels — what re-arms a window that has nothing left to try

**DIVERGED** · evidence **MEASURED** · yield **decides-certificates** · classification trail: reader DIVERGED → refuter upheld → second lens DIVERGED

The mechanism: what wakes a window that has nothing left to try.

holo2 has exactly three wake channels, and pose revisions are deliberately not one of them - it measured that waking on a re-read produced three useless passes and turned the channel off.

holo3's wake set is a strict superset: the eight neighbouring rectangles at every scan resolution rather than only finer ones; a global signal whenever any page becomes reachable; and, crucially, pose revisions, because a revised pose changes the key the window subscribes to. On top of that, a revised pose mints a fresh candidate identity, so the window's own record of what it has tried no longer covers it and the cheap rung is offered again.

One boundary worth stating: holo3's wake set is narrower than its consult set. A distant pose does not wake a window; it only changes what that window sees when something else wakes it.

Consequence: holo3's supply of things to retry grows as the run proceeds, and the retry loop has no natural end short of exhaustion.

Evidence: the cost is one window fired 18 times against holo2's worst of 7, and the cheap rung firing 2.7 times per committed pose on the seven-capture stack. The yield is the same reach that places the coarse lane at all - between 28% and 80% of coarse-lane poses across four datasets. On identical scans the broader wake wins on certificates AND on wall clock: 112 against 100 and 82 against 56 certificates, 842 s against 1,370 s and 786 s against 1,206 s.

Options: keep holo3's; port holo2's three-channel discipline (which is measured worse in the only comparison); or keep the reach and bound the re-offer supply - for instance by not minting a fresh identity for a pose whose geometry moved less than the search box.

Recommendation: keep holo3's wake set, and rule separately on bounding the retry supply. That is a cost fix and must be null-proved on certificates before it lands. Rule this row and the consult-reach row together; they are two halves of one behaviour.

**Ruling:**

---

### placement-22 — What a calibration refusal costs — does the accepted pose survive it?

**DIVERGED** · evidence **UNPROVEN** · yield **decides-certificates** · classification trail: reader DIVERGED → refuter upheld → second lens DIVERGED

The mechanism: what a calibration refusal costs a window that has already been given a valid address.

holo2 writes the address and the calibration as one indivisible block, and fits the calibration first. If that fit refuses, the whole write is dropped - including the address that was already accepted. The window ends up with no address at all: its coverage is lost, and so is its value as an anchor for its neighbours. holo2's own comment records this as inherited behaviour that had never been written down, and says the fix is exactly the split holo3 has.

holo3 makes the address a fact in its own right and calibration a separate step that depends on it. A refused calibration is recorded as a refusal with its reason; the address stands, keeps steering neighbours, keeps contributing covered area, and stays open to later refinement. A separate low-priority row then re-tries the calibration for exactly those windows whenever new certificates land nearby.

So holo3 is strictly more permissive here: it can never lose an accepted address to a calibration refusal.

Evidence: neither tree counts how many windows this drops - holo2 has no census of it, and the one number near the retry row (593 fires producing no adopted poses) measures that row's total yield on a superseded receiver, not this clause.

Options: keep holo3's; port holo2's atomic block (which would reintroduce a loss its own author documented); measure the size of the loss on archived material.

Recommendation: keep holo3's. This is the cleanest case in the family of a divergence that is a repair rather than a risk, and it needs no measurement to rule - the losing side documented its own defect.

If a number is wanted anyway, it is cheap and needs no paper: count calibration refusals on an archived K decode and multiply by the tiles each such window covered - that is the upper bound on what holo2's rule was throwing away.

**Ruling:**

---

### placement-23 — Quarantine's placement-side consumer — a contradicted pose is never offered as a seed, and a placement whose own words contradict a standing pose emits nothing

**ABSENT** · evidence **MEASURED** · yield **integrity** · classification trail: reader ABSENT → refuter upheld

*(No second lens ran on this row. What follows is its reader's text with the refuter's corrections; there is no separate options list.)*

**What it does.** Which anchors may steer a window, and whether a mis-addressed placement can write evidence at all.

**holo2** — `emit_placement` runs a known-position word reduce over this fire's own pilot reads at its own (capture, footing) scope BEFORE any evidence is proposed. A contradiction condemns both sides: the decoded placement is committed as a pose BORN QUARANTINED (retained so the fact names a record, emitting nothing) and every standing pose asserting that position, plus all of its evidence, is marked. `consult` and `regional_seed` both skip a quarantined pose. Jurisdiction is UNCERTIFIED material only; `project_done` does not read the flag, so a condemnation moves zero done pixels. The window is retired.

### placement-24 — Declared-vs-voted printed pitch — what a survey does when the sheet disagrees with the format row

**DIVERGED** · evidence **UNPROVEN** · yield **decides-certificates** · classification trail: reader DIVERGED → refuter upheld → second lens DIVERGED

The mechanism: what happens when the scan's own measurement of the printed cell spacing disagrees with the format the operator named.

holo2 places on the measured value, silently. The addresses it then mints describe a lattice the declared format does not describe, and nothing in the run ever compares the two numbers.

holo3 refuses: it stops the run and names the capture, the measured value and the declared row. On an inconclusive measurement both behave the same - that capture contributes no windows and the rest of the run proceeds.

Evidence: no measurement on any of the three trees; this is a code-behaviour row. The nearest real-world data point is the earlier misdeclared-resolution incident, where a survey said one coding and the specification said another and a clean refusal was the right outcome.

The narrowing to record: a scan containing two sheets of different codings is refusable in holo3 and partially readable in holo2. No artefact says such a capture exists in the corpus.

The other narrowing: three trees give three answers in this area, and the earlier receiver's rule differed again - so whatever is ruled, only one behaviour survives.

Options: keep holo3's refusal; port holo2's place-on-the-measured-value behaviour; or a middle course - refuse by default, with an explicit operator flag to trust the measurement.

Recommendation: keep holo3's. A disagreement here means the operator named the wrong format, and every certificate minted afterwards would be recorded against a geometry nobody declared. A loud refusal costs one re-invocation; the alternative silently forks the run. If the mixed-sheet scan ever becomes real material, the flag is the answer, not silence.

**Ruling:**

### placement-25 — The default page domain — how many pages placement may address without an operator flag

**DIVERGED** · evidence **MEASURED** · yield **decides-certificates** · classification trail: reader DIVERGED → refuter upheld → second lens DIVERGED

The mechanism: how many pages a run may address when the operator says nothing.

holo2 assumes one. A run with no page count can only ever admit page zero, has no cross-page rival population, and needs the operator to know the answer in advance for anything larger.

holo3 assumes the whole set the format allows - 133 pages on the largest tile matrix, thousands on the smallest - with a flag to cap it, and the single-page setting is exactly holo2's behaviour. The set of pages already reached starts at page zero and grows whenever a decoded address opens a new one. Pages that have not been reached are swept last and priced highest.

Four things follow: an address on a page holo2 could never reach becomes admissible; the look-alike guard gets a real rival population instead of an empty one; the correlation floors become per-share; and small windows no longer place blindly into unreached pages - they wait for an address to open one.

Evidence, on a real archived capture and its own control: 1,357 certificates before the change, 1,360 with the change and the single-page cap, 1,356 at the full default - all three verified byte-exact to the same payload. The default costs one certificate inside run-to-run scatter and saves 35% of the processing time and 36% of the jobs. The only real fragment material went from 357 to 657 certificates across a run and a resume, but did not close either time.

Options: keep holo3's; revert to one page by default; keep the default and require the flag for fragment work.

Recommendation: keep holo3's. It is the newest merged work, qualified against its own null with a byte-exact close, and it is the only one of the three receivers that can address a multi-page or fragment volume without being told the answer first. Record that the fragment payoff is still open - the one fragment sheet has not closed under either setting.

Also worth one line in the ruling: holo3 builds each page's reference material the first time it is touched rather than all of it up front, which is what makes a domain of thousands of pages affordable at all.

**Ruling:**

---

### placement-26 — The dryness gate — an expensive placement tier may not start while anything cheaper is ready or in flight

**ABSENT** · evidence **MEASURED** · yield **decides-order-only** · classification trail: reader ABSENT → refuter upheld

**holo2** — Exactly three placement tiers carry `gated=True`: wide_consult, cold_live and cold_rest (resample at 200 is deliberately NOT gated). ### placement-28 — Placement takes no re-work / aging term

**CARRIED-IDENTICAL** · evidence **UNPROVEN** · yield **decides-order-only** · classification trail: reader CARRIED-IDENTICAL → refuter upheld

*(No second lens ran on this row. What follows is its reader's text with the refuter's corrections; there is no separate options list.)*

**What it does.** Whether a window that has been re-consulted many times gets more expensive, and so falls behind fresher work.

**holo2** — Structurally absent: the aging band `max(base, 90 + 5*(n-1))` is a function of a DESCRIPTOR's durable spend row, and a work SOURCE spends nothing at the wall, so the ordinal is undefined for placement. `Slot.price` reads no attempt count. Stated as structural rather than a carve-out.

*Where:* `holo2/src/holo2/engine/recipe.py:408-420`; `holo2/src/holo2/solve/place_tiers.py:65-69`; `holo2/src/holo2/solve/place_slot.py:197-218`

**holo3** — The engine hands `attempts` and `last_cost` to every cost function as data; `_place_cost` ignores both and returns `stage_price(stage, lane_depth, depth)`. So placement re-fires at a flat price forever, exactly as in holo2 — but here it is a choice the row makes, not a structural impossibility, since the escalation door is open to it.

*Where:* `src/holo/receiver/plugins/place_sched.py:259-262`; `src/holo/receiver/engine.py:303-305` · *scheduler row / doc:* SCHEDULE.md:24-34 (the escalation door: 'A row that wants its re-runs to grow more expensive prices them itself'); the `place` row carries no `*n` in the ladder

- (holo3) the flat re-fire price is what lets the re-fire loop run: one window fired 18 times, `ring` fires 2.7x per chain pose on sheet H — *artefact:* scratch/sheetI-20260831/v2v3-placement-residue.md:270-282, :443-449
- (holo2) the aging band's measured value on the families that DO carry it: attempts 1,248 -> 760 (-39%), worst key 11,820 -> 4,260 iterations (-64%), certified 160 -> 160 unchanged, wall 96.1 -> 49.1 s, three volumes byte-exact both sides — *artefact:* ARTEFACTS.md:724 (§2.9)

**Recommendation (reader).** keep holo3's behaviour and open the escalation door as a separate, gated line. This is the residue report's strongest and most yield-safe finding (R1): pricing a placement re-fire by its own ordinal cannot refuse anything — it only queues a much-repeated window behind fresher work — and holo2's aging measurement on other families shows the pattern preserves the certificate count exactly while cutting attempts by a third. It is a holo3-side improvement, not a port.

**Reader's notes.** The two trees agree today. The row is here because the CONSTRAINT differs: holo2 cannot price placement re-fires (no spend row), holo3 can and chooses not to. That makes it a candidate rather than a carry.

**Refuter (upheld).** *Corrections:* None.

*Reason:* holo3 `_place_cost` (place_sched.py:259-262) takes `attempts` and `last_cost` and ignores both; holo2's `Slot.price` (place_slot.py:197-218) has no attempt term. Behaviour is identical today, so CARRIED-IDENTICAL is the right word even though the constraint differs.

**Ruling:**

### placement-29 — Terminality short of success — which refusals remove a window from the frontier

**DIVERGED** · evidence **MEASURED** · yield **decides-certificates** · classification trail: reader DIVERGED → refuter upheld → second lens DIVERGED

The mechanism: which refusals remove a window from consideration for good, as opposed to putting it to sleep.

holo2 removes almost nothing. Five outcomes leave the set, and a window whose pose read too little support is not one of them - it sleeps, and a better lattice measurement can wake it and place it later.

holo3 has three exits, and too-little-support is one of them. That exit is final: a retired window is never woken and never re-staged.

The opposite-signed half of this row, which the map inherited from an earlier report, is wrong and should be struck: a quartered parent does not live forever. It survives exactly one extra wake cycle - the pass that creates its children returns early, and the next exhausted pass retires it and triggers its second chance.

So the real question is one rule: should a rectangle that shows fewer than thirty readable sites under its pose be gone, or asleep? holo3 says gone. The argument for gone is that support is a statement about how much readable paper is under the rectangle, and the earlier receiver did not retry such windows either. The argument for asleep is that how much support a fit reads depends on the region lattice measurement standing at that moment, and that measurement improves during a run.

Evidence: on identical scans holo3 retired 4 windows in one arm and none in the other, while holo2 retired none in either - so the door is real but narrow. holo3 nonetheless left fewer windows unresolved (155 against 196, 281 against 397) and spent a smaller share of its placement budget on them.

Options: keep holo3's; port holo2's sleep-instead-of-retire for this one outcome; or a middle course - retire only if the region's lattice measurement has not moved since the fit, which is a small, testable condition.

Recommendation: keep holo3's retirement, and strike the never-retiring-parent item from the map; there is no defect there to fix. If Germano wants the door closed more gently, the middle course is the cheap version and its bound is already measured at 4 windows on the only material we have.

**Ruling:**

---

## Evidence and ownership

### evidence-ownership-05 — Two generations of a window's evidence, and which one decodes

**CARRIED-DIFFERENT** · evidence **UNPROVEN** · yield **decides-certificates** · classification trail: reader CARRIED-DIFFERENT → refuter upheld

*(No second lens ran on this row. What follows is its reader's text with the refuter's corrections; there is no separate options list.)*

**What it does.** Whether a reading taken through a superseded pose and a reading taken through the revised pose can both own cells in one decoder input, and what happens to the older reading.

**holo2** — Both generations stay committed and stay in the attempt's READ-SET (a read-set may grow but never shrink), and the reduction is applied one layer in, on the DECODER INPUT: per (tile, window) keep only the pieces of the newest pose that HAS pieces — the live pose if it holds any, else all of them. The ctx pool harvests the same reduction so one physical label is never pooled twice at half weight. `index.live_pose` alone is explicitly refused: it would delete 4,392 / 4,117 pieces on the archived states.

*Where:* `holo2/src/holo2/solve/generation.py:118-152`; `holo2/src/holo2/solve/payload.py:597-649`; `holo2/src/holo2/solve/payload.py:291-304`; `holo2/src/holo2/solve/calibration.py:290-320`

**holo3** — There is exactly ONE `WindowEvidence` per window id. A pose revision re-fires `calibrate`, which takes the resample branch — same page cells, same reference values, new sampling through the revised pose — and the fold REPLACES the standing row (last write wins), bumping EVIDENCE_FIT and/or EVIDENCE_READ. There is no generation ledger, no superseded copy, and therefore no rule to state: a superseded read and a live read cannot meet in one freeze because the superseded read no longer exists.

*Where:* `src/holo/receiver/store.py:702-748`; `src/holo/receiver/plugins/calibrate.py:641-673`; `src/holo/receiver/plugins/calibrate.py:349-356` · *scheduler row / doc:* SCHEDULE.md 'How this matches v2' — one standing fact per subject; `calibrate` row (born by Placement, retire=never)

**What actually differs.** holo2 keeps both readings and reduces at read time; holo3 keeps one and overwrites. The safety property (no mixed generations in one freeze) is structural in holo3 and derived in holo2. The cost of holo3's choice is that a re-read is unconditionally authoritative: there is no quality gate on the replacement, and if a resample at a revised pose lands `accepted=False` the fold writes the refusal, `_update_cover` removes the window from every tile cover it fed, and the earlier accepted reading is gone. Because `resample_window` reuses the standing cells and labels, the label-driven refusals (`search_class_starved`, `heldout_starved`) cannot newly fire; the one reachable new refusal is `outside_raster` when the revised pose pushes the window's points off the raster edge.

**Evidence.**

- (holo2) MEASURED: 4,392 / 4,117 pieces hang on a non-live pose, so index.live_pose would delete them all — 'newest that HAS pieces' is the IDENTITY on every pre-F2a state — *artefact:* seeds/holo2-behaviour-ledger.md B39 -> holo2/src/holo2/solve/generation.py:1-52; ARTEFACTS.md:520 (re-read population 269/1,228 e029 and 272/1,282 xerox)
- (holo3) no artefact — no run record counts `outside_raster` calibrate refusals, and no artefact measures a lost accepted evidence row — *artefact:* no artefact

**Recommendation (reader).** keep holo3's, after a zero-cost check: the archived holo3 event streams already carry one `CalibrateAdopted` event per calibrate fire with its refusal reason (`scratch/holo3-overnight-events/*.jsonl.xz`, `scratch/sheetI-20260831/events-*.jsonl.xz`). Count the fires that refused `outside_raster` on a window that had previously been accepted. That is an `xz -dc | jq` pass over existing files — no decode, minutes of wall — and if the count is zero on every archived volume the row closes as MEASURED. If it is non-zero, the fix is a one-line quality gate in `_fold_evidence` (do not let a refusal replace an acceptance), not a generation ledger.

**Reader's notes.** holo2's whole `generation.py` + halo + parent-loss-fallback apparatus exists only because holo2 commits evidence per (pose, tile) rather than per window. None of it needs porting; what needs deciding is whether holo3's last-write-wins fold wants a floor.

**Refuter (upheld).** *Corrections:* Two additions that bound the exposure the row asks Germano to rule on. (1) The proposed event pass is feasible but keys differently than the row implies: `CalibrateAdopted` (src/holo/receiver/plugins/calibrate.py:618-624) carries capture id, the string 'level/region/patch-N', accepted and reason — NOT the window id or extent — so the jq pass must pair fires on (capture, level, region, patch_ordinal). (2) A refusal is not necessarily terminal: `_refused_outside` writes an empty `page_cells_yx` (src/holo/receiver/plugins/calibrate.py:331-337), so the NEXT calibrate fire on that window takes the `calibrate_window` branch rather than the resample branch (src/holo/receiver/plugins/calibrate.py:661-668) and can re-accept if the pose moves back. Say so — it turns the row from 'evidence can be lost forever' into 'evidence is lost until the pose moves again', which is a materially cheaper ruling. Keep ruling_needed=true; this is one of the two rows in the family whose behaviour genuinely differs in outcome.

*Reason:* holo2 keeps both generations and reduces at read time; holo3 has one WindowEvidence per window and the fold overwrites it, and the row's identification of `outside_raster` as the one newly reachable refusal is correct against the code.

**Ruling:**

### evidence-ownership-08 — Payload lane premium — what a decode over a given rung costs

**CARRIED-DIFFERENT** · evidence **UNPROVEN** · yield **decides-order-only** · classification trail: reader CARRIED-DIFFERENT → refuter upheld

*(No second lens ran on this row. What follows is its reader's text with the refuter's corrections; there is no separate options list.)*

**What it does.** The order in which a tile's readings are decoded: coarse rungs before fine ones, and the whole payload family before every placement rung.

**holo2** — `lane_premium` = LANE_COST_PER_RUNG (2) times the DEEPEST rung the attempt will read: a scan or retry has one footing, a union takes the max over the ppc's resident footings, on the stated principle that 'an attempt is not cheaper than its dearest component'. An empty footing set answers 0. COST_PAYLOAD 20 + 2*4 = 28 < 40 keeps payload under every placement rung.

*Where:* `holo2/src/holo2/solve/payload.py:317-336`; `holo2/src/holo2/solve/payload.py:940-969`

**holo3** — A scan pays 20 + 2 * its own level index. A union pays 20 + 2 * `union_rung_ordinal(ppc)`, which is the MINIMUM level index among the captures that hold that ppc. A retry inherits its origin's formula.

*Where:* `src/holo/receiver/plugins/tiles.py:749-751`; `src/holo/receiver/plugins/tiles.py:774-776`; `src/holo/receiver/plugins/tiles.py:811-815`; `src/holo/receiver/store.py:344-348` · *scheduler row / doc:* SCHEDULE.md price ladder — tiles.scan / tiles.union / tiles.retry at 20, same-price peers; 'Coarse rungs pay +2 per rung less than fine ones'

**What actually differs.** holo2 prices a union by its DEAREST component (max depth); holo3 prices it by its CHEAPEST (min level index across captures at that ppc). The two agree whenever every contributing capture has the same ladder — the ordinary single-dpi case — and diverge exactly on mixed-dpi stacks, where holo3 runs the union earlier than holo2's rule would. Both keep the whole payload family under the cheapest placement rung.

**Evidence.**

- (holo3) first cross-dpi union certificates on paper: 1,508 of the 4,307 — so the mixed-ladder case where the two rules differ does occur in production — *artefact:* ARTEFACTS.md:622 -> src/holo/receiver/docs/conversion_docs/MEASUREMENTS-2026-08-31.md:18-19
- (holo2) 'an attempt is not cheaper than its dearest component'; 20 + 2*4 == 28 < 40 keeps payload under every placement rung — *artefact:* seeds/holo2-behaviour-ledger.md B47 -> holo2/src/holo2/solve/payload.py:319-333

**Recommendation (reader).** keep holo3's. The two rules agree on every single-dpi stack and differ only on mixed-dpi unions, where holo3 runs the union earlier than holo2's 'not cheaper than its dearest component' rule would. Both run every attempt eventually, so nothing is forgone; if the ordering is ever wanted holo2's way it is a one-line change from min to max in store.union_rung_ordinal, visible in the regenerated ladder (python -m holo.receiver schedule). No run needed.

**Reader's notes.** Ordering only: both schemes run every attempt eventually, and neither gates. Settling it costs nothing beyond a note, because the ladder is regenerated from the plugin literals (`python -m holo.receiver schedule`) and any change would show there.

**Refuter (upheld).** *Corrections:* Two corrections. (1) `union_rung_ordinal` minimises over EVERY capture in the store that holds that ppc (src/holo/receiver/store.py:345-348), not over the captures covering this tile — so a capture that contributes nothing to the tile can still make its union cheap. That widens the divergence the row describes beyond mixed-dpi stacks to any store whose captures have different ladder lengths. (2) 'Both keep the whole payload family under the cheapest placement rung' is arithmetic, not a rule: payload is 20 + 2*level (src/holo/receiver/plugins/tiles.py:67,751) and the cheapest placement rung is 40 (src/holo/receiver/docs/SCHEDULE.md:66), so the property fails at level index >= 10; nothing in either tree enforces it. Also note `min(indices)` raises on an empty sequence — only the subject-minting guarantees it is non-empty.

*Reason:* min-vs-max is real: holo2 prices a union by max footing depth (holo2/src/holo2/solve/payload.py:334-336), holo3 by `store.union_rung_ordinal` = min level index (src/holo/receiver/store.py:344-348).

**Ruling:**

### evidence-ownership-10 — Same-capture cross-rung pooling — summing one scan's rungs on a below-floor tile

**ABSENT** · evidence **MEASURED** · yield **decides-certificates** · classification trail: reader ABSENT → refuter upheld

*(No second lens ran on this row. What follows is its reader's text with the refuter's corrections; there is no separate options list.)*

**What it does.** Whether a tile that no single rung of a single capture can support is ever attempted with all four rungs' readings summed.

**holo2** — Not built and refused by ruling J2-4 ('v1 has NO same-capture cross-rung sum — a lane IS a rung'). `union_tags` keys on distinct capture SHAs, so several rungs of one capture can never mint a union; `_decoder_input` partitions a union's pieces by footing and sums one owner per footing, but the footings only ever come from distinct captures.

*Where:* `holo2/src/holo2/solve/payload.py:251-270`; `holo2/src/holo2/solve/payload.py:651-695`

**holo3** — Absent, and by the same structure. `assemble_union` takes exactly one level per capture at the subject's ppc; `tiles.local`'s subject carries a ppc and its lane set is `cover_lanes(page, ty, tx, ppc)`, one lane per capture; `tiles.retry` inherits its origin's lane. Nothing anywhere sums two levels of one capture.

*Where:* `src/holo/receiver/plugins/tiles.py:291-301`; `src/holo/receiver/plugins/tiles.py:332-341`; `src/holo/receiver/plugins/local_ctx.py:413-462` · *scheduler row / doc:* SCHEDULE.md rows tiles.scan / tiles.union / tiles.local — every one scoped to a single ppc

**Evidence.**

- (holo2) REFUSED by ruling J2-4; the forgone yield is measured: summing the four corrected components certifies +2 e029 tiles no single footing closes ((42,9), (22,1)) and reaches the below_floor class — 16 of 78 gap tiles at 1.2-4.9% cell error refused for coverage — *artefact:* ARTEFACTS.md:504 -> holo2/docs/kparity/DIVERGENCE-LEDGER.md:67; ranked H3 in scratch/holo2-catchup/detect/DETECTION-GAP.md:113
- (holo3) the pooling amplitude window, measured at the codec: pooled closes from noise amplitude 1.2, winner-take-all union from 1.6, a single view from 2.4 — on synthetic independent noise, which is true across captures and false within one — *artefact:* ARTEFACTS.md:623 -> src/holo/receiver/docs/conversion_docs/MEASUREMENTS-2026-08-31.md:183-199
- (holo3) the informative-floor ceiling: below it no AttemptFact is emitted, so no rescue row is ever born — a rescue row can only help a tile the receiver still tries — *artefact:* ARTEFACTS.md:706 -> src/holo/receiver/docs/conversion_docs/MEASUREMENTS-2026-08-31.md:57-60

**Recommendation (reader).** archive for later port, as an ADDITIVE residue row rather than an ordinary route. The measured prize is small but real and is concentrated exactly where nothing else can reach: +2 e029 tiles that no single footing closes, and access to a below-floor class that today emits no AttemptFact at all and therefore arms no rescue row. The reason both trees refused it is sound and must be carried into any brief: the rungs of one capture are area averages of the SAME pixels, so their LLRs are not independent and their sum is not a lawful likelihood — the amplitude measurement above says so explicitly ('true across captures and false within one'). That argues against making it a peer of tiles.union and FOR making it a last-resort residue row in the toolbox pattern: transient, uncertified-tiles-only, priced above every refinement row, with the zero-wrong audit as its only bar. Cost of the port: one plugin of roughly local_ctx's shape, no format change, no state schema change; cost of qualifying it: the existing crop batteries plus a sim existence proof, no full-sheet decode.

**Reader's notes.** The below-floor door is the sharper half of the case and neither tree's record states it: today the coarse rungs' readings of a hard tile are discarded individually as below-floor, so the tile is never ATTEMPTED and no rescue row is born for it. A cross-rung sum is the only mechanism in either tree that could clear that floor from one capture.

**Refuter (upheld).** *Corrections:* Three corrections. (1) Say explicitly in the row that the mechanism is absent from BOTH trees so ABSENT is not read as a holo3 regression — the word is defensible only because holo2 NAMED and refused it. (2) The reason holo2 records is v1-parity, not independence: DIVERGENCE-LEDGER.md:67 reads 'v1 has NO cross-rung sum - a lane IS a rung'. The independence rationale is holo3's, and it is recorded — src/holo/receiver/docs/conversion_docs/MEASUREMENTS-2026-08-31.md:191-196 says the within-capture form 'was parked on exactly that physics (correlated fit noise, the regime where F1 o F6 measured equal-weight combining losing)'. Attribute each reason to the tree that holds it. (3) The port brief need not be designed from scratch: DIVERGENCE-LEDGER.md:67 already carries holo2's costed fix shape (a `ROUTE_POOL` capture-scoped route, equal weights, `_decoder_input`'s union branch verbatim, residue-only minting, priced COST_PAYLOAD + lane_premium over the deepest rung, k_eff floor unchanged). Cite it.

*Reason:* Absent from holo3 by structure (one level per capture in assemble_union / one lane per plan in local_ctx / retry inherits its origin), refused in holo2 by ruling J2-4, and the +2 e029 tiles and the 16-of-78 below-floor class are verbatim in holo2/docs/kparity/DIVERGENCE-LEDGER.md:67.

**Ruling:**

### evidence-ownership-15 — Quarantine's evidence-exclusion consumer — refusing to decode readings from a contradicted placement

**ABSENT** · evidence **MEASURED** · yield **integrity** · classification trail: reader ABSENT → refuter upheld

*(No second lens ran on this row. What follows is its reader's text with the refuter's corrections; there is no separate options list.)*

**What it does.** Whether a reading taken through a placement that an address-word contradiction condemned is still decoded and still allowed to own cells and weigh in a calibration fit.

**holo2** — One predicate, three readers. `route_evidence_keys` filters condemned pieces out of the attempt's READ-SET before either branch, so the exclusion is total: a condemned piece is not merely skipped by the decoder, it is not part of the attempt's identity. `pool_members` and the pool assembly ask the same predicate. The condemnation is a write on the condemned keys, so an in-flight decode over evidence condemned mid-flight dies at the wall's existing staleness check. Terminality on both sides, never rehabilitated.

*Where:* `holo2/src/holo2/solve/quarantine.py:1-56`; `holo2/src/holo2/solve/payload.py:306-307`; `holo2/src/holo2/solve/calibration.py:243`; `holo2/src/holo2/solve/calibration.py:263`

**holo3** — absent

*Where:* _(no path)_ · *scheduler row / doc:* no row; no key family; `plugins/words.py:299-327 word_rival_rows` is the within-window rival bar and is a different mechanism

**Evidence.**

- (v1) the zero-contradiction invariant is e022's: 70/70 regions, all 925 pilot tiles, ZERO contradictory reads across ~1,780 attempted known-position decodes — *artefact:* ARTEFACTS.md:750-752 -> docs/RECEIVE-DESIGN-EVIDENCE.md:492
- (holo2) holo2's own measurement is a wall cost, not a yield: W5 quarantine unit 1,915, oracles 314, 39 verdicts, wall 79.0 s — the wave costs ~1.7 s against a bypassed baseline, +2%. Yield: 'Zero contradictions on every healthy fixture' — *artefact:* ARTEFACTS.md:754-757 -> holo2/docs/MEASUREMENTS.md:1345, :1384-1386, :1390
- (holo3) zero wrong certificates across all five completed crop batteries (285 target addresses) and zero out-of-bbox certificates; every close that ran stayed byte-exact — *artefact:* ARTEFACTS.md:317-318, :601 -> src/holo/receiver/docs/conversion_docs/crop-batteries/*-results.json; PARITY.md:441

**Recommendation (reader).** accept the loss, and record it. There is no measured yield behind quarantine on any of the three sides — v1's ~1,780-read invariant says contradictions do not happen, and holo2's own number is a 2% wall cost. The integrity role it plays is already covered twice over in holo3: the per-tile codec gate is an LDPC syndrome plus a CRC over a codeword whose slot permutation and scramble are keyed by the tile ADDRESS, so a reading through a wrong-address pose cannot certify, and `printed_cells_for` raises `IntegrityError` if a certified re-encode disagrees with the format field at a known site. What genuinely leaves is the ability to NOTICE the contradiction and say so — a diagnostic, not a gate. If it is ever wanted back, the cheap form is an event, not a state family.

**Reader's notes.** Named by no seed row in this family (the seeds put quarantine under placement, where its consult half lives); the payload and calibration consumers are evidence-ownership and are equally absent. grok's hypothesis that quarantine is holo2's one unique yield lever has no measured yield behind it on either side (ARTEFACTS §2.11's own conclusion).

**Refuter (upheld).** *Corrections:* Citation only: the v1 invariant sits at ARTEFACTS.md:753, not :750-752 (a section and table header). The primary source does carry the row's fuller wording — docs/RECEIVE-DESIGN-EVIDENCE.md's SIX-SCAN SESSIONS bullet has '70/70 regions and all 925 pilot tiles' and 'ZERO contradictory reads across ~1,780 attempted known-position decodes' — so cite that line directly rather than through the shifted ARTEFACTS anchor.

*Reason:* One predicate with three readers in holo2 (quarantine.py:1-55, payload.py:306-307, calibration.py:243,263) and nothing whatsoever in holo3 — grep for 'quarantin' and 'condemn' over holo3 production code returns empty; `words.word_rival_rows` is a rival-hypothesis enumerator, a different mechanism as the row says.

**Ruling:**

### evidence-ownership-16 — Tile cover membership — which windows are offered to a tile's freeze at all

**CARRIED-DIFFERENT** · evidence **UNPROVEN** · yield **decides-certificates** · classification trail: reader CARRIED-DIFFERENT → refuter upheld → second lens CARRIED-DIFFERENT

What it does: decides which windows are even offered to a tile when its cells are frozen to owners.

holo2: every committed, non-quarantined piece of the scan. A window whose calibration refuses commits no evidence - and, importantly, loses its POSE as well, so its coverage and any refinement it could have fed are gone.

holo3: only accepted windows, and only where their payload cells actually reach the tile. A refused window keeps its placement, and a separate row re-offers it later.

Evidence: this is a row where holo3 matches v1 and holo2 is the odd one out. v1 keeps every window and its placement and filters only at the freeze; holo2's own comment says its drop-the-pose behaviour was carried from its earlier code, not from v1.

Correction to the number the first read used: the artefact says the re-offer row fired 593 times producing zero refined poses. That is a pose statistic. The re-offer's calibration branch produces no pose at all, so the count can neither credit nor debit it.

And the re-offer is inert by construction, which I verified rather than inferred: it re-samples the SAME placement using the window's stored cells and stored reference values, so a refusal caused by too few usable labels is reproduced bit for bit. The only inputs that can change the outcome are the pose and the region pitch, and both already re-fire calibration on their own. So the measured zero is expected, and the event-counting pass the first read proposed is not needed.

One asymmetry worth recording while the freeze is open: colour rows have no accepted concept in the fold, so a colour row always joins a tile's cover while a black-plane row joins only when accepted. It is safe today because every emitted colour row carries a real loss, but nothing states the invariant.

Options: keep holo3's and delete the inert re-offer branch; keep it as harmless scheduling noise; or state the colour invariant as a guard.

Recommendation: keep holo3's membership rule - it is v1's and it is the better side. Record the pose-retention difference as something holo3 already avoids rather than something to port, and treat the inert re-offer branch as wasted scheduling rather than lost yield, for whoever prices the rows next.

**Ruling:**

## Refinement

### refinement-02 — The window-local geometry restatement and the point the baseline is sampled at (local_segment / native_terms / sample_at)

**DIVERGED** · evidence **UNPROVEN** · yield **decides-certificates — it decides whether an adopted revision moves production sampling toward the middle of a cell or a quarter-cell away from it, which is the difference between refinement buying tiles and refinement costing them; on the archived corpus it is holo2's numbers that change, not holo3's** · classification trail: reader DIVERGED → refuter upheld → second lens DIVERGED

What the mechanism does: it decides where on the scan the refinement reads — both the baseline it is trying to beat and every candidate geometry it scores.

Both trees store a page geometry that names the CORNER of a cell, on purpose, and expect every sampler to add half a cell to reach the middle.

What holo3 does: adds the half cell in both places — the ordinary sampler and the refinement's own frame — so the refinement's baseline is, cell for cell, the read the standing evidence already carries.

What holo2 does: adds it in the ordinary sampler but NOT in the refinement path, and not in the re-read either. Its refinement therefore scores a baseline sampled half a cell up and left of where the receiver actually reads: the corner where four cells meet.

Evidence: closed arithmetic, no state and no run needed. The durable offset carries a -M*(0.5,0.5); holo2's refinement re-origins without adding it back and lands at o - M*(0.5,0.5); holo3 re-origins at corner+0.5 and lands exactly on o. Three pixels at a six-pixel pitch. holo3's side is pinned in the fast test lane; holo2's own comments claim the property its code does not deliver, and the old receiver had a guard for exactly this that holo2 dropped.

Consequence: a search box of a quarter cell cannot reach back to the middle from a corner, so almost every candidate looks better than the baseline and the winner is written back through the same displaced frame — moving production sampling a quarter cell AWAY from the middle. That fits holo2's record exactly: every revision accepted, 270 and 275 on the two sheets, and zero certificates bought, with done coverage going down.

Yield: decides certificates. Nothing to port — holo3 is the correct side. What changes is the map's evidence base: holo2's refinement adoption rate, its gate margins and its refinement yield are all measured through a displaced baseline and cannot be used to bound holo3's refinement.

Options: (a) keep holo3's behaviour and record the finding against every holo2 refinement number; (b) also write the twenty lines of arithmetic into a standalone check so the claim is executable, minutes, no run; (c) do nothing and leave holo2's 100%-adoption figure standing in the map.

Recommendation: (a) and (b). Strike holo2's refinement adoption and yield rows as confounded, in place, with this reason. Do not let (c) happen: the 100% figure reads as a success story and it is the signature of a bug.

One thing to drop from the row: the framing that this explains 'opposite adoption rates'. holo3's zero was a broken counter, and holo3 in fact adopts between a quarter and three quarters of its refinements on real volumes.

**Ruling:**

### refinement-03 — Refinement eligibility — the e024 two-sided reach gate

**CARRIED-DIFFERENT** · evidence **UNPROVEN** · yield **decides-certificates** · classification trail: reader CARRIED-DIFFERENT → refuter upheld

*(No second lens ran on this row. What follows is its reader's text with the refuter's corrections; there is no separate options list.)*

**What it does.** Which windows are offered a pose revision at all, and therefore where refinement spend and any refinement yield can land.

**holo2** — Reach is derived, never recorded: one pass over the evidence index groups pieces by the pose that produced them, and the tile map partitions the tiles they name into certified and open. A pose is eligible when it has BOTH open tiles (something to gain on, e024's law) and certified tiles (the label channel RF1 needs), and its extent carries no revision yet. Quarantined poses and quarantined pieces are dropped in the same sweep, and expansion mints no descriptor at all for an ineligible reach.

*Where:* `holo2/src/holo2/solve/refine.py:198-242`; `holo2/src/holo2/solve/refine.py:245-318`; `holo2/src/holo2/solve/refine.py:461-482`; `holo2/src/holo2/solve/refine.py:624-638`

**holo3** — The subject is the window, not a pose. reach_tiles derives the window's tile reach from its evidence page cells (store.evidence_reach) and reach_certified marks a tile certified when ANY stream of the row has a certificate for it. refine_eligible = 0 < |certified reach| < |reach| AND the certified reach strictly contains the adopted revision's basis; refine_exhausted (the retire predicate) fires when the whole reach is certified. Family.K only, and the standing evidence must be accepted.

*Where:* `src/holo/receiver/plugins/refine.py:517-548`; `src/holo/receiver/plugins/refine.py:551-558`; `src/holo/receiver/plugins/refine.py:567-580`; `src/holo/receiver/plugins/refine.py:636-656`; `src/holo/receiver/store.py:217-223` · *scheduler row / doc:* SCHEDULE.md:51, SCHEDULE.md:107-112 (the refine debt row), SCHEDULE.md:411-419 (the REFINE named problem)

**What actually differs.** Subject and derivation: holo2 offers one descriptor per eligible (window, pose) grouped off the evidence index; holo3 offers one per window against the standing placement. Certified test: holo2 partitions per (page, ty, tx, stream) work key, holo3 collapses to per-tile 'any stream certified' — exact for K rows, which carry one stream, and the only rows this descriptor serves. holo3 adds a fourth clause holo2 has no counterpart for (certified reach must strictly outgrow the basis, refinement-04) and expresses 'nothing left to gain' twice: once in eligible and once as a retire predicate that removes the subject permanently. holo3 drops holo2's quarantine filter entirely, because holo3 has no quarantine machinery.

**Evidence.**

- (holo3) post step 2 on e029: refine fires 106 -> 406 after the re-arm widening, revisit 0 -> 124, all crediting 0 certificates; the volume went 1940 -> 1938 (lost 2 at ty=42), wall 1.16x — *artefact:* ARTEFACTS.md:99
- (v1) on the xerox K sheet v1 won 18 of its 1,930 certificates by refinement and all 18 are in column 0; holo3 certified 13 of those 18 with no refinement at all; the 5 it did not get are 5 of holo3's 8 missing tiles — *artefact:* ARTEFACTS.md:123, ARTEFACTS.md:517-526
- (holo3) G-10: why holo3's refinement was not scheduled at the five column-0 rows v1 rescued is inference, not measurement — holo3's refinement writes no events — *artefact:* ARTEFACTS.md:814

**Recommendation (reader).** keep holo3's gate, and close G-10 first: add per-fire refinement events (subject, reach sizes, refusal reason) to holo3's refine and refine.color rows — an instrumentation change, not a mechanism change — then re-read the xerox K events already in hand (scratch/holo3-overnight-events/xerox-k-postfix-events.jsonl.xz) plus one re-decode of that volume. Until then no one can say whether the five column-0 tiles were never offered, offered and refused on support, or refused on the gate.

**Reader's notes.** The two sides' eligibility is the same idea (open residue AND certified labels) and I could not find a case where the per-stream/per-tile collapse changes a K verdict. What is genuinely different is that holo3's refinement never even reports which clause closed the door, where holo2 names all three by string (extent_already_revised / no_uncertified_residue / no_certified_labels, refine.py:479-482). The colour lane's eligibility has no 'something to gain on' clause in eligible at all — it lives only in the retire predicate (color_refine.py:361-368); see refinement-14.

**Refuter (upheld).** *Corrections:* Both sides read correctly at HEAD (holo2 Reach at refine.py:198-242 / reaches at :245-318 / _gather's three named refusals at :465-482; holo3 refine_eligible refine.py:536-542, reach_certified :551-558, _refine_eligible :636-646, _refine_retire :649-656, store.evidence_reach store.py:221-223). Two small precisions worth carrying: holo3's reach is derived from the EVIDENCE's page cells, so a tile the window only clips by a sliver counts as a full member of both `len(reach)` and the retire test — holo2's piece-derived partition has the same property (a piece exists per observed (tile,stream)), so this is a match, not a delta; and the K-row 'any stream certified' collapse is exact because store.row.streams has one entry for K. The G-10 recommendation (add per-fire refusal events) is the right first move and is instrumentation, not mechanism.

*Reason:* Every clause of both gates reproduces at HEAD; the stated delta is complete and correctly worded.

**Ruling:**

### refinement-04 — How many times one window may be revised — holo2's one-revision-per-extent bar vs holo3's basis-gated chain

**DIVERGED** · evidence **UNPROVEN** · yield **decides-certificates — the chain decides what geometry the payload is finally read through, and it is live on paper today: 162 windows across the archived runs have already taken more revisions than holo2's cap allows, 8 of them three** · classification trail: reader DIVERGED → refuter upheld → second lens DIVERGED

What the mechanism does: it decides how many times one window's geometry may be revised as more of the page certifies around it.

What holo2 does: at most one revision per window, ever, made structural — a revised window offers no subject at all, so total drift from the accepted placement is bounded by one search box. Its own text says lifting that bar is a measurement nobody made.

What holo3 does: no cap. A window becomes eligible again as soon as the certified tiles in reach strictly outgrow the set the last revision was fitted on, and each new fit starts from the already-revised geometry, so the steps compose.

Evidence, measured by me from the archived event streams (no decode, minutes): adoption is not rare and chains are not hypothetical. Across seven archived volumes the refinement rows adopted between a quarter and three quarters of their fires, and 162 windows took more than one adoption — 154 took two, 8 took three. Colour carries almost all of it (sheet I alone has 124 windows at two and 4 at three); the black-and-white runs show one twice-revised window each.

The map's 'holo3 has never adopted on a real volume' is wrong, and so is 'no chain has ever started'. Both trace to a counter that reads adoptions off an event the refinement path never writes.

Risk: three composed steps can move a window three quarters of a cell and nearly half a degree. That is enough to start reading a neighbour's cell. The failure mode is lost tiles, not wrong ones — a mis-addressed cell fails its check rather than lying — so this costs yield, never correctness.

Options: (a) keep the chain as it stands; (b) keep it and add holo2's one guard — refuse a geometry the window has already stood at — a few lines, no format change; (c) bound composed drift against the ORIGINAL accepted placement at one search box; (d) revert to holo2's cap and accept the loss; (e) measure first.

Costed measurement, no run: the accepted placements are in the archived streams (PoseCommitted carries the geometry) and the final standing geometry is in each run's state, so the composed-drift distribution for those 162 chained windows, and whether chained windows are the ones that lose tiles, is an afternoon of arithmetic on files already on disk.

Recommendation: (b) plus (e). Keep the chain — it is the more capable design and it is what the hard-column rescue case wants — add the cheap 'already stood here' guard, and run the drift measurement before anyone considers making the chain cheaper or longer.

**Ruling:**

---

### refinement-05 — The no-op guard — refusing a revision that did not move

**CARRIED-DIFFERENT** · evidence **UNPROVEN** · yield **integrity** · classification trail: reader CARRIED-DIFFERENT → refuter upheld

*(No second lens ran on this row. What follows is its reader's text with the refuter's corrections; there is no separate options list.)*

**What it does.** Whether a fit that lands back on a geometry already in play is committed, and whether an oscillating search is visible.

**holo2** — Two guards after the gate, reported separately on purpose. 'pose_unmoved': the canonical affine equals the standing LIVE pose — the fit moved away and came back, since the worker always fits from the SUPERSEDED subject pose. 'pose_already_stands': the affine equals an EARLIER pose of the same extent that the live pose has moved past, which a rising count exposes as a search oscillating between two answers. Before the gate there is a third, cheaper refusal, 'active_pose_selected', when the grid winner is the base pose itself.

*Where:* `holo2/src/holo2/solve/refine.py:419-457`; `holo2/src/holo2/solve/refine_fit.py:538-541`

**holo3** — Two refusals inside refine_window: 'active_pose_selected' when the grid winner equals the base pose, and 'unchanged_affine' after the gate when the recomposed native affine equals the standing placement's. A non-adopted Revision folds as a no-op (store.py:794-796 returns before touching the placement). There is no third check, because holo3 keeps no superseded poses to compare against.

*Where:* `src/holo/receiver/plugins/refine.py:486-490`; `src/holo/receiver/plugins/refine.py:504-507`; `src/holo/receiver/store.py:791-797` · *scheduler row / doc:* SCHEDULE.md:51

**What actually differs.** holo2's 'pose_unmoved' and holo3's 'unchanged_affine' are the same test against the standing geometry, but they mean opposite things because the two trees fit from different baselines: holo2 fits from the superseded parent (so returning to the live pose is an oscillation), holo3 fits from the standing placement (so returning to it is simply 'no movement'). holo2's second half, 'pose_already_stands', has no holo3 counterpart at all: holo3's store keeps exactly one Placement per window, so an earlier revision's affine is not knowable. holo3 also compares recomposed native terms for exact float equality where holo2 compares canonicalised pose keys (POSE_TERM_DECIMALS rounding), so a sub-quantum revision that holo2 would collapse to a no-op is committed by holo3 as a distinct affine.

**Evidence.**

- (holo2) 0 of 545 adopted revisions were no-ops at W2, with revisions unread — *artefact:* holo2/src/holo2/solve/refine.py:41-47 (module docstring; no artefact in ARTEFACTS.md carries this count)
- (holo3) no artefact — holo3 has adopted zero revisions on any real volume (ARTEFACTS.md:528-536), so neither refusal string has ever been exercised there — *artefact:* ARTEFACTS.md:528-536

**Recommendation (reader).** keep holo3's, and fold the missing half into refinement-04's guard rather than as a separate mechanism: a per-window set of affines already stood at is one small store field and restores 'pose_already_stands' for free, which is the only thing that makes a chained refinement's oscillation visible.

**Reader's notes.** The float-exact comparison at refine.py:505 is worth a second look at merge time: holo2 deliberately canonicalises before comparing because 'the canonical rounding is what can make a sub-quantum revision collapse onto the geometry it started from' (refine.py:429-434). holo3 has no canonical pose rounding at all — its placement identity is the window id, not the affine — so the trap holo2 named cannot be reproduced there, but the counter cannot be either.

**Refuter (upheld).** *Corrections:* (1) Say in one word that the second half is ABSENT, not merely different: `pose_already_stands` (holo2 refine.py:445-457) has no holo3 counterpart, because holo3 keeps one Placement per window; that half belongs on the RULINGS list as a port/accept decision, and the row's current wording buries it. (2) Line nit: the non-adopted no-op fold returns at src/holo/receiver/store.py:790-791, not :794-796. (3) The float-exact vs canonical comparison is confirmed: holo2 canonicalises to POSE_TERM_DECIMALS = 6 (holo2/src/holo2/solve/keys.py:39, :113) before the key compare; holo3 compares recomposed native terms with `==` (refine.py:504-505). (4) The '0 of 545 adopted revisions were no-ops' number is at holo2/src/holo2/solve/refine.py:45-46 as the row says — a docstring, no artefact.

*Reason:* Both guards reproduce at HEAD and the delta is stated correctly; only the ABSENT half needs to be named so the ruling list catches it.

**Ruling:**
