### refinement-06 — Live-pose derivation — which geometry of a window the rest of the tree reads

**CARRIED-DIFFERENT** · evidence **UNPROVEN** · yield **decides-certificates — adopting a revision overwrites the window's geometry AND replaces its standing evidence, with no way back; a revision that reads worse in truth costs that window's tiles and cannot be undone** · classification trail: reader CARRIED-DIFFERENT → refuter upheld → second lens CARRIED-DIFFERENT

What the mechanism does: it decides which geometry of a window the rest of the receiver reads after a revision — and whether the superseded one survives at all.

What holo2 does: keeps every pose it ever committed and derives the winner at read time as 'the latest committed pose for this window'. Nothing is retracted; superseded evidence stays on the shelf.

What holo3 does: keeps exactly one placement per window. An adopted revision is folded through the ordinary placement path and overwrites the old geometry, which is then gone; the window's page cells, tile reach and region pitch are all re-derived from it.

How holo3 chooses: a ranking — refinement route first, then the size of the certified set the fit was based on, then quality. Inside a chain the basis term always wins, so quality never gets a vote.

One property is true but for the wrong reason: a refined window is never re-placed by an ordinary placement, because the placement row retires as soon as any window is placed. The route term of the ranking is inert today, in both directions.

Yield: this decides certificates, in one direction. Adoption is irreversible — the previous evidence is replaced by the re-read, and the window's context calibration drops back to unadopted until the region's context row re-fires. If a revision reads worse in truth than the geometry it replaced, those tiles are simply lost and nothing walks it back.

Evidence on the other side: holo2's accumulate is not free either — thousands of evidence pieces end up hanging on poses that are no longer live, and unioning parent and revision over-claimed done coverage by 860 and 588 cells on the two sheets. holo3 has no equivalent measurement of its own.

Options: (a) keep holo3's single standing placement; (b) keep it and record the two undocumented properties (the third ranking term compares a placement mismatch rate against a refinement log-loss — different units — and adoption is one-way); (c) port a 'keep whichever evidence reads better' rule, which is real work and needs a score the two paths share; (d) measure the loss by re-running one archived volume with refinement off and comparing certificate sets.

Recommendation: (b). One standing placement is the simpler and safer answer and it deletes holo2's whole live-pose apparatus; what it owes is two sentences of documentation, not a port. Leave (d) parked behind the drift measurement in refinement-04, which would find the same thing more cheaply.

**Ruling:**

---

### refinement-07 — The re-read after a revision — when and at what price the revised geometry is actually sampled

**CARRIED-DIFFERENT** · evidence **UNPROVEN** · yield **decides-certificates — the re-read is the only path by which an adopted revision reaches a decoder at all; and I can now bound what it bought: at most 3 distinct tiles on each of the two archived black-and-white sheets, out of 795 tiles under refined windows that were already certified** · classification trail: reader CARRIED-DIFFERENT → refuter upheld → second lens CARRIED-DIFFERENT

What the mechanism does: the re-read. An adopted revision changes nothing until the window's cells are sampled again through the new geometry.

What holo2 does: a dedicated scheduler door with a four-clause test and its own seat priced at 200 — the same price the revision itself paid, deliberately, so the consequence cannot jump the queue the revision waited in.

What holo3 does: no separate row at all. The ordinary calibration worker lists the window's geometry among the facts it reads, so an adopted revision wakes it automatically at price 15, and it re-samples the standing evidence's own cell set at the new geometry. Same frozen cells, earlier arrival.

Evidence, holo2 side: its re-read reads through the SAME displaced sampler as its fit, so its re-read means sit half a cell from the ones they are compared against, while the code comment claims the opposite. Its census (every re-read 'resampled', no refusals) says the row ran, not that it read the right pixels. And its only measured yield is a combined figure for fit-plus-re-read of +27 and +32 tiles, on a run where the ownership-key change alone is worth 27 and 27 — so the re-read's own share is nearly nothing.

Evidence, holo3 side, measured by me from the archived event streams: the re-read is live on paper — every window that adopted a revision shows further calibration fires afterwards. And an upper bound on the whole lane's contribution: of the tiles lying under a window that adopted a revision, almost all were already certified before the adoption, and at most three distinct tiles per black-and-white sheet certified afterwards. That is a ceiling, not an attribution, and it is the first number holo3's refinement has.

One side effect worth knowing: the re-read resets that window's context calibration to unadopted until the region's context row re-fires, so for a short while the window is read with the weaker calibration.

Options: (a) keep holo3's; (b) keep it and record the measured ceiling; (c) key the re-read on 'the geometry changed because of a refinement' rather than any geometry change; (d) restore a separate priced row.

Recommendation: (a) plus (b). Folding the re-read into calibration deletes a scheduler door, a four-clause predicate and a memo, and reading the revised geometry sooner is strictly better for yield. Skip (c): a non-refinement re-placement cannot be scheduled at all, because the placement row retires the moment a window is placed — one sentence, not a guard.

**Ruling:**

---

### refinement-08 — Two generations of evidence, and which one decodes

**CARRIED-DIFFERENT** · evidence **UNPROVEN** · yield **decides-certificates** · classification trail: reader CARRIED-DIFFERENT → refuter upheld

*(No second lens ran on this row. What follows is its reader's text with the refuter's corrections; there is no separate options list.)*

**What it does.** Whether a superseded read and a live read can meet in one payload freeze, and whether a parent's reading survives a revision.

**holo2** — Both generations' pieces stay committed forever; live_generation reduces at the READ: per window, if the live pose holds at least one of the keys in hand, keep only its pieces, otherwise keep all of them. That is the identity on every state written before the re-read existed, which is why the rule is 'newest that HAS pieces' and not index.live_pose — reducing to the live pose alone would have deleted 4,392 / 4,117 pieces. The reduction is answerable from live_poses alone only because an extent carries at most two generations.

*Where:* `holo2/src/holo2/solve/generation.py:118-150`; `holo2/src/holo2/solve/generation.py:10-49`; `holo2/src/holo2/solve/payload.py:597-649`; `holo2/src/holo2/solve/resample.py:65-80`

**holo3** — There is one WindowEvidence per window and a re-read replaces it in place; the parent's scores, knots and losses are overwritten and not recoverable. The property holo2's rule exists to buy — a superseded read and a live read never meeting in one freeze — holds by construction, with no reduction step, no ordinal and no generation vocabulary anywhere in the tree.

*Where:* `src/holo/receiver/store.py:700-747`; `src/holo/receiver/plugins/calibrate.py:658-668`; `src/holo/receiver/plugins/tiles.py:206-245` · *scheduler row / doc:* SCHEDULE.md:90-91 (evidence_fit / evidence_read written by WindowEvidence)

**What actually differs.** holo2 keeps both readings on disk and adjudicates at read time; holo3 keeps one and adjudicates by overwriting. The invariant is the same and holo3's spelling is unconditional (it cannot be got wrong by a third generation), but holo3 loses the ability to fall back: if a revision's re-read is WORSE than the parent's, holo2 still has the parent's pieces committed (even if no reader currently prefers them) while holo3 has destroyed them. Certificates already minted are unaffected on both sides.

**Evidence.**

- (holo2) 4,392 / 4,117 evidence pieces hang on a non-live pose on the two archived states; 269/1,228 and 272/1,282 windows carry two committed poses — *artefact:* ARTEFACTS.md:555
- (holo2) the first holo2-only tiles (0,42,3) e029 and (0,5,21), (0,7,0), (0,27,36) xerox — v1 retains uncertified evidence on all four — *artefact:* ARTEFACTS.md:562
- (holo3) no artefact — the branch has never fired on a real volume — *artefact:* ARTEFACTS.md:528-536

**Recommendation (reader).** keep holo3's. The fallback holo2 retains is not actually used by any holo2 reader (live_generation prefers the live pose whenever it has pieces), so the loss is theoretical on holo2's own terms; v1's lane separation, where BOTH readings stay decodable and a certificate may still be minted from the base lane, is the real capability neither successor has, and that belongs to the W2 map.

**Reader's notes.** This row and refinement-04 are one design on holo2's side: 'newest that HAS pieces' is answerable without an ordinal ONLY because the revision cap holds. holo3 lifted the cap (refinement-04) and dissolved the problem (this row) at the same time, which is coherent — but it means the holo2 module that names the trap (generation.py:45-49, 'a THIRD generation would make this rule under-determined') has no holo3 anchor to point at, and the comment carry should not try to give it one.

**Refuter (upheld).** *Corrections:* Verified verbatim: live_generation's 'newest that HAS pieces' rule (holo2/src/holo2/solve/generation.py:116-148) and the module's own statement that a THIRD generation would make it under-determined (:45-49); holo3 has one WindowEvidence per window, replaced in place (store fold + calibrate.resample_window at calibrate.py:349-356). Nothing to change. One clarification worth a clause: holo3's re-read also destroys the parent's KNOTS and PROBS, not only its scores, which is what makes refinement-11's 'chained fits score through the re-fitted PAVA' unavoidable rather than a choice.

*Reason:* Both spellings reproduce at HEAD and the invariant claim is correct on both sides.

**Ruling:**

### refinement-09 — The region context fit must see the re-read's evidence

**CARRIED-DIFFERENT** · evidence **UNPROVEN** · yield **decides-certificates** · classification trail: reader CARRIED-DIFFERENT → refuter upheld

*(No second lens ran on this row. What follows is its reader's text with the refuter's corrections; there is no separate options list.)*

**What it does.** Whether the context correction that is applied at decode assembly was fitted over the geometry the cells were actually read at.

**holo2** — The 2026-08-23 fix: the ctx fit names region_evidence_collection_key in its read-set, so the pieces a re-read commits bump that key and the pool re-fits over the live generation at its own price. The omission was measured, not theoretical — 0 of 4 pools on either real sheet had a fit that had seen a re-read's pieces, which is what made the live pose publish no held-out loss on exactly the windows the re-read had made decode-authoritative.

*Where:* `holo2/src/holo2/solve/calibration.py:577-628`; `holo2/src/holo2/solve/resample.py:39-54`

**holo3** — The ctx descriptor's read-set is (evidence_fit, wid) for every window of the region, so a re-read's new evidence bumps evidence_fit and re-arms the region fit at 15 + 2/rung. The re-read also resets ctx_adopted to False on the fresh evidence (calibrate.py:328), so the corrected read is not carried over from the previous geometry.

*Where:* `src/holo/receiver/plugins/calibrate.py:738-742`; `src/holo/receiver/plugins/calibrate.py:768-781`; `src/holo/receiver/store.py:735-747`; `src/holo/receiver/plugins/calibrate.py:307-328` · *scheduler row / doc:* SCHEDULE.md:44 / :62 (ctx row at price 15) and SCHEDULE.md:90 (evidence_fit read by ctx)

**What actually differs.** Same intent, different plumbing: holo2 needed a bespoke collection key added at review time because its ctx fit subscribed to a pool rather than to the members; holo3's ctx already subscribes per member window, so the property is a consequence of the ordinary read-set rather than a fix. holo3 additionally clears the adopted-context flag on the fresh evidence, which holo2 has no counterpart for (its ctx correction lives outside the evidence record, applied at decode assembly).

**Evidence.**

- (holo2) before the fix: 0 of 4 pools on either real sheet had a fit that had seen a re-read's pieces; 0 of 255 xerox re-read poses had a published window loss — *artefact:* ARTEFACTS.md:502
- (holo2) CA2 context fit on real sheets: 4 fits each sheet, every one ADOPTED, zero refusals, held-out log-loss deltas -0.052 to -0.094 — *artefact:* ARTEFACTS.md:499
- (holo3) no artefact isolates a re-read-driven ctx refit — *artefact:* ARTEFACTS.md:528-536

**Recommendation (reader).** keep holo3's — the subscription is already the right shape and needs no port. Worth one line in the map: holo2 paid a review cycle to discover this and holo3 gets it for free from per-member reads, which is the strongest single argument in this family for the declared-reads model.

**Reader's notes.** The ctx row re-fits the WHOLE region on any member's re-read, at 15 + rung, so on a sheet where refinement actually adopts, holo3 will pay noticeably more ctx fits than holo2 did (holo2 measured 4 per sheet). No artefact bounds that cost on holo3, and it will not show until refinement adopts something on a real volume.

**Refuter (upheld).** *Corrections:* Verified on both sides (holo2 calibration.py:577-600 names region_evidence_collection_key with the measured 0-of-4-pools justification; holo3 _ctx_reads is per-member (KEY_EVIDENCE_FIT, wid) at calibrate.py:739-742, ctx price 15 + 2*level at :748-750, ctx_adopted cleared on the fresh evidence at :328). Add one consequence the row understates: `_corrected_row` (calibrate.py:507-517) also REPLACES heldout_logloss with the context-corrected loss, so a re-read does not merely un-adopt the context — it reverts the window's published loss to the marginal one until the region re-fits, which is precisely the window the ownership key (refinement-10) reads.

*Reason:* Same intent, different plumbing, correctly stated; the subscription really is per-member at HEAD.

**Ruling:**

### refinement-10 — The owner-loss fallback for a pose with no published loss

**CARRIED-DIFFERENT** · evidence **UNPROVEN** · yield **decides-certificates** · classification trail: reader CARRIED-DIFFERENT → refuter upheld

*(No second lens ran on this row. What follows is its reader's text with the refuter's corrections; there is no separate options list.)*

**What it does.** Which window owns a contested cell in a payload freeze when the standing geometry has not published a held-out loss.

**holo2** — The other half of the 2026-08-23 pair: a live pose with no published loss falls back to its extent's SIBLING generation's loss instead of infinity. Measured before the fix: 0 of 6 re-read windows had a loss for their live pose, while 6 of 6 had one for the parent, so without the fallback exactly the windows a re-read had made authoritative sorted to the bottom of the ownership key.

*Where:* `holo2/src/holo2/solve/ctx_apply.py:117-166`; `holo2/src/holo2/solve/payload.py:781-833`

**holo3** — The ownership key reads the standing evidence's heldout_logloss and falls back to infinity when it is None (tiles.py:212-213). The state holo2's fix exists for cannot arise: there is one evidence per window, a re-read re-fits and re-publishes the loss in the same fact, and there is no sibling generation to borrow from.

*Where:* `src/holo/receiver/plugins/tiles.py:206-245`; `src/holo/receiver/plugins/calibrate.py:292-328`; `src/holo/receiver/store.py:700-747` · *scheduler row / doc:* SCHEDULE.md:42 (calibrate publishes WindowEvidence carrying heldout_logloss)

**What actually differs.** holo2 needed a cross-generation fallback because its poses and pieces are separate records that can be out of step; holo3 publishes geometry-derived scores and their held-out loss in one WindowEvidence fact, so 'the standing geometry has no loss' means the fit was refused, and infinity is then the right answer rather than a bug. No port is possible or meaningful.

**Evidence.**

- (holo2) 0 of 6 re-read windows had a loss for their live pose while 6 of 6 had one for the parent; 0 of 255 xerox re-read poses had a published window loss — *artefact:* ARTEFACTS.md:502
- (holo2) the ownership key itself is worth 27 e029 + 27 xerox certificates against pose-id order — *artefact:* ARTEFACTS.md:496-497
- (holo3) no artefact — structural, verifiable by reading tiles.py:206-245 and calibrate.py:292-328 — *artefact:* ARTEFACTS.md:528-536

**Recommendation (reader).** keep holo3's; record the row as closed by construction rather than as a carry, so a future reader does not go looking for the fallback and conclude it was dropped.

**Reader's notes.** The ownership key itself (B40) is the evidence-and-ownership family's row, not mine; what is mine is only the re-read's interaction with it. I could not find any holo3 path that leaves a standing accepted evidence without a heldout_logloss, but I did not exhaustively check the plane_k and colour producers of WindowEvidence (plugins/plane_k.py:412-421 does gate on it being non-None).

**Refuter (upheld).** *Corrections:* The row's open caveat can be closed and should be: holo3 has exactly ONE `WindowEvidence(` construction (calibrate.py:310) and exactly one accepted CalibrationFit (calibrate.py:288-290), which always carries a float loss; plane_k reaches evidence through the same calibrate.window_evidence (plane_k.py:397); the only `replace` on an evidence row is _corrected_row (calibrate.py:516-517), which sets the loss rather than clearing it. So 'an accepted standing evidence always publishes a heldout_logloss' is verified structurally, and the inf branch at tiles.py:212-213 really is unreachable for accepted rows. Keep the classification and the 'closed by construction' recommendation.

*Reason:* holo2's cross-generation fallback answers a state holo3 cannot reach, and I verified holo3 cannot reach it.

### refinement-11 — Which calibration the refinement scores through

**CARRIED-DIFFERENT** · evidence **UNPROVEN** · yield **decides-certificates** · classification trail: reader CARRIED-DIFFERENT → refuter upheld

*(No second lens ran on this row. What follows is its reader's text with the refuter's corrections; there is no separate options list.)*

**What it does.** The probability map applied to every candidate's sampled means, and hence the log-loss the adoption gate compares.

**holo2** — Generation-zero replay: the pose's evidence cites a frozen CalibrationRecord by key, and refine reads its PAVA knots and probabilities back out of the params blob. detect.Calibration also wants n_search, which the blob does not carry, so it is reconstructed as the smallest integer admitting the stored probabilities — sound rather than approximate, since llr reads only knots and probabilities. A window whose calibration cannot be replayed refuses 'no_generation_zero_calibration' before a pixel is read.

*Where:* `holo2/src/holo2/solve/refine.py:122-131`; `holo2/src/holo2/solve/refine.py:328-353`; `holo2/src/holo2/solve/refine.py:495-500`

**holo3** — refine reads knots and probs straight off the STANDING WindowEvidence and calls calibration_llr with them; there is no n_search to reconstruct because holo3's llr does not take one. A window with no accepted evidence is refused in prepare_refine (refine.py:572-573) rather than inside the fit.

*Where:* `src/holo/receiver/plugins/refine.py:436-441`; `src/holo/receiver/plugins/refine.py:571-573`; `src/holo/receiver/plugins/calibrate.py:292-328`; `src/holo/receiver/lib/pava.py` · *scheduler row / doc:* SCHEDULE.md:51 (refine reads evidence_fit, evidence_read)

**What actually differs.** holo2 replays the FROZEN generation-zero fit (the calibration the pose's pieces were decoded under) and reconstructs a constructor argument to do it; holo3 uses whatever calibration the standing evidence currently carries. For a first refinement those are the same fit. For a chained refinement (refinement-04) they are not: holo3's second fit scores through the PAVA re-fitted at the first revision's geometry, which is arguably the right baseline but is a different quantity from holo2's, and holo2 could not reach the case at all because it capped the chain at one.

**Evidence.**

- (holo2) no artefact — the replay is described only at holo2/src/holo2/solve/refine.py:122-131 — *artefact:* holo2/src/holo2/solve/refine.py:122-131
- (holo3) no artefact — holo3 has adopted zero revisions on a real volume, so no chained fit has ever been scored — *artefact:* ARTEFACTS.md:528-536

**Recommendation (reader).** keep holo3's — 'score through the calibration the current evidence was fitted under' is the only convention that stays coherent once chaining exists, and it removes a reconstruction step. Record the difference so nobody reads the chained fit as a generation-zero replay.

**Reader's notes.** holo3's arrangement also gives it the property holo2's docstring claims for itself but arguably does not have (refinement-02): because refine crops and filters with calibrate's own primitives over calibrate's own points, refine's baseline logits ARE the LLRs the standing evidence carries. I verified the crop bounds, aperture and interpolation match (calibrate.py:201-209 vs :229-244, both via _crop_bounds and _aperture_px); the only difference is the out-of-crop fill value (median of the raw crop for window_crop, 0.0 for sampled_window_means), which touches only points outside the crop.

**Refuter (upheld).** *Corrections:* Two precisions. (1) The row (and its notes) claim holo3's refine baseline logits ARE the standing evidence's LLRs. Measured: the two point sets agree to 4.5e-13 px but are NOT bit-identical (216 of 500 points exactly equal in a synthetic check) because the two paths compose the affine in a different order (calibrate.scan_points_yx does (cells+0.5)@M.T+t; refine goes through the re-origined local pose). The sampling primitives themselves ARE bit-identical: sampled_window_means and window_crop+sample_points returned max-abs-diff 0.0 on both of _lattice_indices' branches. Say 'to within float rounding', so nobody later writes a bit-exact pin that fails. (2) A mechanism the row omits: v1's refine applies the ADOPTED CONTEXT OFFSETS when the generation-zero detector is the context profile (src/holo/receive/certified_refine.py:703-715), holo2 deliberately does not (refine.py:135-140), and holo3 does not either — it scores through the marginal PAVA off evidence.knots/probs (refine.py:436-441) while the standing evidence's published loss, on a ctx-adopted window, is the CORRECTED one (calibrate.py:513-517). So holo3 sides with holo2 against v1 here, and the refinement gate optimises a different detector from the one the tiles decode through. That belongs in the row's delta.

*Reason:* The 'score through the current evidence's calibration' rule is stated correctly; the equality claim is float-approximate and the context arm is missing.

**Ruling:**

### refinement-12 — Re-arm and spend identity — what makes refinement ask again

**CARRIED-DIFFERENT** · evidence **UNPROVEN** · yield **decides-order-only** · classification trail: reader CARRIED-DIFFERENT → refuter upheld

*(No second lens ran on this row. What follows is its reader's text with the refuter's corrections; there is no separate options list.)*

**What it does.** How often a refinement fire is attempted as the certificate frontier grows; it orders and prices work rather than deciding a verdict, but it decides whether a late first chance is ever taken.

**holo2** — The worker's read-set is the pose key plus the GLOBAL certified collection, so any new certificate anywhere re-arms every eligible refinement descriptor: one spend per (pose, certificate-frontier version), k = 1, no 'at least k new certificates' tightening. The coarseness is deliberate and argued — a reach-scoped certified membership would be a new durable mechanism, and the cost of the coarse wake is descriptor arming, not spends, since an unchanged reach re-derives the same refusal.

*Where:* `holo2/src/holo2/solve/refine.py:380-403`; `holo2/src/holo2/solve/refine.py:41-47`

**holo3** — Reach-scoped: the read-set is (evidence_fit, evidence_read, pose_affine, pose_route) for the window plus one tile key per (tile in the window's pose-derived reach, stream). Certificates outside the reach do not wake it. The gate that stops a re-fire from repeating work is the basis-superset clause, not a spend memo.

*Where:* `src/holo/receiver/plugins/refine.py:622-633`; `src/holo/receiver/plugins/refine.py:537-542`; `src/holo/receiver/store.py:217-223` · *scheduler row / doc:* SCHEDULE.md:107-112 — 'it subscribes to the certified tiles in its reach, both halves of its own evidence and its own pose, so any change to those re-fires it — including a first chance arriving late'

**What actually differs.** holo2 wakes on the whole certificate frontier and relies on the fire's own refusal to be cheap; holo3 wakes only on the tiles in reach, plus its own evidence and pose. holo3's is strictly narrower and cannot miss a wake that matters, because a certificate outside the reach cannot change the label set or the residue. One asymmetry to note: holo3's reads use the POSE-derived window_reach while eligible, retire and prepare use the EVIDENCE-derived evidence_reach (refine.py:627 vs :641/:653/:576); the two differ after a pose moves and before the re-read lands, which is a deliberate over-declaration (extra wakes, never wrong results, per SCHEDULE.md:383-386).

**Evidence.**

- (holo3) post step 2 on e029: the widened re-arm took refine fires 106 -> 406 and revisit 0 -> 124, all crediting 0 certificates, at 1.16x wall — *artefact:* ARTEFACTS.md:99
- (holo2) RF1 churn: +1,784 commits on e029 (+15.5%), +1,618 on xerox (+13.2%), for zero certificates; 274 expansions x 270 = 73,980 mints vs a dial-off control's 2 x 0 — *artefact:* ARTEFACTS.md:558

**Recommendation (reader).** keep holo3's. The e029 post-step-2 number is the honest cost of the widening (4x the fires for 1.16x wall and no yield); it is cheap and it is what makes a late first chance reachable, which is the whole point of the REFINE named problem (SCHEDULE.md:414-419).

**Reader's notes.** holo2's churn number is the closest thing either side has to a bound on this mechanism's cost, and it is not transferable: holo2's fires ADOPTED (and each adoption commits), holo3's refuse. The comparison that would matter — cost per fire on a sheet where refinement adopts — does not exist on either side.

**Refuter (upheld).** *Corrections:* Verified: holo2's read-set is (COLLECTION_CERTIFIED, pose) with the module arguing for the global collection (refine.py:380-403); holo3's is per-window evidence/pose plus one key per (tile in window_reach, stream) (refine.py:622-633). Strengthen the row's 'cannot miss a wake' with the mechanism that makes it true: the engine RE-DERIVES a worker's reads whenever a KEY_POSE_AFFINE for its own subject moves (src/holo/receiver/engine.py:415-421 in `_touch`, via `_rederive_reads` :341-358), and `_births` adds fresh keys to a standing worker (:394-401) — so the pose-derived reach subscription is refreshed exactly when the reach changes, which is why the window_reach/evidence_reach split is safe rather than merely over-declared.

*Reason:* Both re-arm identities are as described, and the narrower holo3 subscription is provably wake-complete at HEAD.

**Ruling:**

### refinement-13 — The price of refinement and its rung premium

**CARRIED-DIFFERENT** · evidence **UNPROVEN** · yield **decides-order-only — but with teeth: I measured that in an ordinary run that closes, holo3's refinement never fires at all, so this price is what makes the whole family a harvest-only lane today** · classification trail: reader CARRIED-DIFFERENT → refuter upheld → second lens CARRIED-DIFFERENT

What the mechanism does: the price refinement pays, which is what decides when — and whether — it runs.

What holo2 does: 200 plus 2 per level, above every placement tier, so refinement is emergently last. Cold search is cheaper but carries 20 per level, so cold overtakes refinement from level three up; holo2 recorded that crossover as the open half of the price and wanted to raise refinement's price so 'last' would be unconditional.

What holo3 does: 200 flat, no level premium, with cold repriced to 250/260 and still 20 per level. Refinement now runs before cold at every depth. That is not a fix of holo2's question — it is the opposite answer: cold becomes the desperate work at the top instead.

Yield: ordering only. Nothing here changes what a fit computes; it changes what is still pending when the payload closes and the run stops.

Measured, on files already on disk: the same scan of the black-and-white HP sheet, decoded on the current tree without the full-harvest flag, closes verified at 1,357 certificates and the refinement row never fires once. The archived full-harvest decode of that identical scan fires it 126 times, adopts 31 and ends at 1,938 certificates. At 200 the seat comes up only after the close has already happened.

So the honest statement of this row is not 'refinement runs late' but 'in an ordinary run that closes, refinement does not run at all'. Every refinement number holo3 has comes from a full harvest or from a volume that failed to close — which is, to be fair, exactly the case the lane exists for: damaged sheets, fragments, residues.

Also gone: there is no ageing band on this row, so a refinement that keeps refusing never gets dearer, and coarse and fine levels tie at 200 where holo2 drained coarse first.

Options: (a) keep the ladder as it is and record the harvest-only fact; (b) reprice refinement below the payload tail so it can earn a seat before the close; (c) restore a level premium so coarse levels drain first; (d) measure (b) before doing it — re-run one archived volume with refinement priced under the payload rows and compare certificates and wall.

Recommendation: (a) now, with the justification restated honestly as a deliberate reordering rather than a fix, and (d) parked as the next cheap experiment. Do not do (b) on judgement: the ceiling measured in refinement-07 says the lane's whole contribution on a healthy black-and-white sheet is at most a few tiles, so buying it a seat ahead of the close has to be justified on the hard volumes, not the easy ones.

**Ruling:**

---

## Done projection and the done gate

### done-gate-01 — The done projection: certified tiles mapped back onto a capture's own cells

**CARRIED-DIFFERENT** · evidence **UNPROVEN** · yield **decides-certificates** · classification trail: reader CARRIED-DIFFERENT → refuter upheld

*(No second lens ran on this row. What follows is its reader's text with the refuter's corrections; there is no separate options list.)*

**What it does.** Which cells of a capture are already spent — the fact every pose-free done test is asked against, so it decides which unplaced windows are ever attempted.

**holo2** — For one region, walk one pose per posed window; for each, take the region's cell-centre grid over the window's extent, push it backwards through the pose (inverse of the committed native affine), floor to page cells, ask which 64-cell tile that is, and OR in the tiles that are certified in every stream, intersected with the survey support mask. The result is a boolean cell array per region, derived on demand and never persisted. The cell centres come from a CellLattice object (origin + pitch*(index+0.5)); tile_of_cell is integer floor-division by 64.

*Where:* `holo2/src/holo2/solve/tilemap.py:750-823`; `holo2/src/holo2/solve/tilemap.py:704-721`; `holo2/src/holo2/solve/geometry.py:95-102`; `holo2/src/holo2/solve/geometry.py:300-303`; `holo2/src/holo2/solve/plane_views.py:275-291`

**holo3** — The same arithmetic, line for line: inverse affine over origin+pitch*(arange+0.5) centres, floor, tile_of_cell, mask by the certified-in-every-stream tile grid and by support, OR-accumulate. The inputs are plain tuples — (page, extent_cells, native_affine) triples plus a dict of per-page done grids and the region frame's origin/pitch/shape/support — instead of a store index and a lattice object. store.done_cells assembles the triples from the standing placements of the capture+region across every level and caches the result per (capture, level, region).

*Where:* `src/holo/receiver/plugins/tiles.py:583-610`; `src/holo/receiver/plugins/tiles.py:567-580`; `src/holo/receiver/store.py:400-435`; `src/holo/receiver/lib/geometry.py:103-105` · *scheduler row / doc:* SCHEDULE.md:562-582 (the done gate); docs/DONE-GEOMETRY.md §2/§4

**What actually differs.** Algorithm and constants identical (same inverse-affine, same floor, same //64, same support AND, same OR accumulation; holo3's arange carries an explicit float64 dtype, holo2's promotes from int64 — same values). What differs is plumbing: holo2 reads poses through a StoreIndex and a CellLattice and caches one DoneProjection dataclass per region per generation; holo3 passes raw tuples and caches a bare numpy array stamped with maintained counters. Both pool every rung of a capture+region into one native-pixel projection, which is sound on both sides because the survey that defines a region is per capture, so all rungs share one cell lattice (holo2: window_plans_for over the same regions; holo3: store._fold_survey builds one frame per level from the same Region).

**Evidence.**

- (holo2) affine family bit-exact between the trees: affine_matrix/affine_terms/invert_affine/apply_affine/shift_affine and rescale/compose all compared over random terms — *artefact:* src/holo/receiver/tests/test_h3_drift_kernels.py:474-514 (slow lane) — the underlying geometry primitives ARE pinned; the projection assembly is not
- (v1) 719 of 1,479 committed placements (48.6%) pixel-gateable; gated windows minted zero certificates, provably — *artefact:* ARTEFACTS.md:94 and :575 -> docs/DONE-GEOMETRY.md:257-273

**Recommendation (reader).** keep holo3's, and consider a costed pin before Phase 3: a cross-tree drift oracle comparing holo2.solve.tilemap.project_done against holo.receiver.plugins.tiles.project_done over random poses/grids/support masks would take seconds in the slow lane and no receiver run, but it can only be written while holo2 exists. Without it the equivalence is a re-derivation by reading, not a pin.

**Reader's notes.** I re-derived equality by reading both bodies; they are textually equivalent modulo argument shape. holo3 caches done_cells per (capture, LEVEL, region) although the array is level-independent (the frame's native origin/pitch/shape/support are identical across levels), so a five-rung capture recomputes the same projection up to five times — cost only, no behaviour.

**Refuter (upheld).** *Corrections:* Test citation is off and under-stated: the affine pin is src/holo/receiver/tests/test_h3_drift_kernels.py:472-494 (test_geometry_affine_family_matches_holo2 — affine_matrix/affine_terms/invert_affine/apply_affine/shift_affine) plus :497-512 (test_geometry_rescale_and_compose_match_holo2_place_shared), not :474-514. Add the expiry: that file reaches holo2 through pytest.importorskip at test_h3_drift_kernels.py:26-27 and is slow-lane-only (pytestmark at :32-35), so at Phase 3 the pin does not fail, it SILENTLY SKIPS (ARTEFACTS.md:803, G-02) — the 'primitives are pinned, only the assembly is not' half of this row has the same expiry date as the assembly, which strengthens the recommendation rather than weakening it. Two verifications the row asserts and I confirmed: (a) the level-independence that makes holo3's cross-level pooling sound — place.region_frame at src/holo/receiver/plugins/place.py:164-193 derives origin_native_yx/pitch_native_px/shape_cells/support_cells from pitch_native = dpi*pitch_in only; scale/origin_level_yx/pitch_level_px are the only level-carrying fields, and done_cells (store.py:431-433) reads none of them, so the per-level cache key is redundant and the note 'cost only' stands; (b) holo2 pools across footings identically (plane_views.py:281-291 projects one region with view.lattice, and windows of every footing share window.region_id — tilemap.py:595-607). One unstated but harmless difference: holo2 short-circuits a non-schedulable region to a 1x1 false array (plane.py:560-566, plane_views.py:280-283) where holo3 returns None from done_cells (store.py:415-423); same answer at every caller.

*Reason:* Arithmetic equality re-derived line for line at HEAD; only the pin's citation and its post-deletion fate need fixing.

**Ruling:**

### done-gate-02 — One pose per window — the latest, never the union of an extent's poses

**CARRIED-DIFFERENT** · evidence **UNPROVEN** · yield **nothing observable on the archived corpus (the mechanism class is certificate-deciding, but the disagreement case is unreachable in holo3 today)** · classification trail: reader CARRIED-DIFFERENT → refuter upheld → second lens CARRIED-DIFFERENT

One pose per window, and which one stands.

The mechanism: a window can be re-fitted, so more than one pose may have been committed for it. Everything downstream - the sampler, the

evidence fit, and the done projection that decides which windows are already spent - has to be told which single pose counts.

holo2 keeps every committed pose as a durable record and hands the projection the most recent one. It reached that rule the hard way: an

earlier version unioned a window's poses, which claimed 860 extra cells on the hp sheet and 588 on the xerox sheet as already spent for

re-fits that had read no pixels at all. Under the corrected rule the done coverage fell back by 3,996 and 1,449 cells.

holo3 keeps exactly one pose per window and overwrites it only when a new one wins a fixed order: a re-fitted pose beats an original, a

longer certified basis beats a shorter one, and a tie goes to the lower cost number. A union is not expressible, because the superseded

pose is not kept at all, and the answer does not depend on which worker committed first.

Evidence: holo2 has the measured hazard and the correction (+860/+588 union, then -3,996/-1,449); 269 of 1,228 hp windows and 272 of 1,282

xerox windows carry two poses, so the population is not rare. holo3 has no matching measurement.

Options: keep holo3's behaviour; port holo2's record-keeping so a superseded pose survives (buys nothing today and adds a fact); archive.

Recommendation: keep holo3's. The hazard the rule exists to close is structurally impossible here, and the only way the two rules could

disagree - a later pose with a shorter basis or a worse fit - cannot be produced: re-fitting requires a strictly larger basis, and the

engine never runs two placement jobs on one window. Carry holo2's docstring, which is the fullest written statement of the rule.

**Ruling:**

---

### done-gate-04 — What 'done' means for a PLACED window — exact tile reach vs the projected extent

**CARRIED-DIFFERENT** · evidence **UNPROVEN** · yield **decides-certificates** · classification trail: reader CARRIED-DIFFERENT → refuter upheld → second lens CARRIED-DIFFERENT

What 'already spent' means for a window that already carries a pose.

The mechanism: before any row does work on a window, the receiver asks whether every tile that window could still contribute to is already

certified in every colour stream. If yes, nothing it does can mint anything, so no row of it is posed, sampled, re-fitted or revisited.

holo2 asks one question for every window, placed or not: project the region's committed poses onto cells, then check every supported cell of this

window's rectangle is covered. A placed window is judged by the same projection as an unplaced one, inheriting its boundary error.

holo3 splits it in two behind one name. A placed window is judged on the exact tiles it actually audited - the cells its own pass kept, plus

the cells its evidence fit used - and every one must be certified. An unplaced window falls through to holo2's projection test.

Evidence: on holo3's simulations the certificate set and payload are byte-identical with the gate on and off, no row fired more jobs, and the

containment is pinned by a test (the tiles a window's evidence covers lie inside the reach it is gated on). No real-sheet comparison exists on

either side, and no holo3 gating rate on paper at all - that is recorded as a gap.

Options: keep holo3's; port holo2's projected-extent test for placed windows too (slower and less exact); archive; or spend a costed

measurement - the existing census tool over an archived sheet-I resume can report both verdicts per placed window for a few minutes of CPU.

Recommendation: keep holo3's, and run the two-verdict census when the gate is next touched. The exact-reach test is better founded than the

projection it replaces, and the only residual is that a done window is never re-calibrated, so a better fit that would have nudged the region

context model never happens. Also fix two stale descriptions: an unused second copy of the check, and an API page describing a missing consult.

**Ruling:**

---

### done-gate-08 — Done is inviolable under quarantine — a condemnation moves zero done pixels

**ABSENT** · evidence **n/a** · yield **integrity** · classification trail: reader ABSENT → refuter upheld

*(No second lens ran on this row. What follows is its reader's text with the refuter's corrections; there is no separate options list.)*

**What it does.** Whether a word contradiction can un-spend geometry, which would both reopen retired windows and invalidate the projection's cache key.

**holo2** — project_done does not read the quarantined flag and does not import the quarantine module, so condemning a pose moves no done pixel and the cache key (committed poses x certified keys) stands unamended. Quarantine's whole jurisdiction is uncertified material — contradiction_writes skips a certified tile outright, on the ground that the certificate vindicates the geometry that read it. live_pose likewise does not filter condemned poses, deliberately: the readers that must refuse one ask is_quarantined themselves. The property is pinned by projecting a region before and after a condemnation and comparing the arrays.

*Where:* `holo2/src/holo2/solve/tilemap.py:750-823`; `holo2/src/holo2/solve/quarantine.py:22-35`; `holo2/src/holo2/solve/tilemap.py:586-591`; `holo2/src/holo2/solve/DESIGN.md:54`

**holo3** — absent — holo3 has no quarantine mechanism at all, so there is nothing that could move done pixels and no invariant to hold. The done projection is a pure function of standing placements and certificates, and certificates are terminal (store._fold_certificate raises on a conflict and never removes one).

*Where:* _(no path)_ · *scheduler row / doc:* src/holo/receiver/docs/API.md:757-758 — 'No quarantine: word trust is one-way (W5 rival bar at admission), so known_position_words and its consumer are not built'

**Evidence.**

- (holo2) 'Zero contradictions on every healthy fixture'; done coverage untouched, adversarially pinned; the W5 block is 236 lines — *artefact:* ARTEFACTS.md:756 -> holo2/docs/MEASUREMENTS.md:1384-1386, :1390
- (holo2) the invariant quarantine is built against is e022's, not holo2's: ~1,780 known-position reads across the six-scan sessions, zero contradictory — *artefact:* ARTEFACTS.md:753 -> docs/RECEIVE-DESIGN-EVIDENCE.md:492
- (holo2) holo2's own quarantine measurement is a wall cost, not a yield: ~1.7 s, +2% — *artefact:* ARTEFACTS.md:755 -> holo2/docs/MEASUREMENTS.md:1345

**Recommendation (reader).** accept the loss for this row alone — there is nothing to port here, because the invariant is a property of a mechanism holo3 does not have. The ruling that matters is the quarantine row itself, which belongs to the placement/evidence family; if quarantine is ever ported, this invariant (project_done must not read the marking) and its before/after array pin must be ported with it, or the projection's monotonicity and its cache key both break.

**Reader's notes.** holo3's monotonicity has a different foundation: certificates are terminal by fold, pinned by test_done_ness_is_monotone_through_further_folds. That covers the certificate half of holo2's cache key; the pose half is covered by row done-gate-10, not by this one.

**Refuter (upheld).** *Corrections:* holo3's API.md quote is at :758-759, not :757-758 (:753-757 is the no-journal bullet). A grep of the whole holo3 tree for 'quarantin' returns exactly that one line plus retirement_docs prose — the absence claim is total. Optionally add holo2/src/holo2/solve/records.py:356-364 to holo2's `where`: the PoseRecord docstring restates ruling 1 ('tilemap.project_done never asks: done pixels are certificate-backed') alongside quarantine.py:24-35 and DESIGN.md:54. Classification note: this row's own content changes no holo3 work (it is an invariant of a mechanism holo3 does not have), so it is a rider on the quarantine row rather than an independent ABSENT — which is exactly what the row's recommendation says. Keep it as written; the value is the pin recipe (project a region before and after a condemnation, compare arrays) surviving as a port precondition.

*Reason:* The invariant, the ruling and the total absence all verify; only the API.md anchor slips one line.

**Ruling:**

### done-gate-10 — Permanence — a done retirement against a projection that can recede

**CARRIED-DIFFERENT** · evidence **UNPROVEN** · yield **decides-certificates (in holo3's favour, and unmeasured on both sides)** · classification trail: reader CARRIED-DIFFERENT → refuter upheld → second lens CARRIED-DIFFERENT

A permanent retirement against a picture that can move backwards.

The mechanism: a window is retired because the certified area covers it. For an unplaced window that area comes from projecting neighbouring poses,

and a neighbour's re-fit moves it by a sliver either way. If the retirement is permanent and the picture recedes, the window is silently lost.

holo2 makes the retirement durable: it is written into the window record and a resumed run reads it back. Its own comment states the exposure

openly - a re-fit's covered area is neither a subset nor a superset of its parent's, the sliver is about three cells, and it is unmeasured.

holo3 is the same inside a run: a retired bookmark stays retired, the only exception being the subdivision afterlife. Across runs it is not durable

at all - a resume re-derives the ladder from the facts, so a window whose picture receded is asked again. It also removes the exposure entirely for

placed windows, judging them on their exact audited tiles instead of a projection.

Evidence: holo2's own words, no number on either side. The one narrow way holo3 can still un-do a done window is a calibration fit from work

already in flight landing after the window retired.

Options: keep holo3's and record the residual as a known unmeasured exposure; port holo2's durable record (strictly worse); or spend a costed

measurement needing no scanner - the census tool folds an archived sheet-I snapshot and re-derives the verdict per window, so a variant asking it

before and after each pose revision would bound the recession in minutes of CPU on state already on disk.

Recommendation: keep holo3's and run that measurement when the gate is next touched. holo3 is strictly safer than holo2 here, since a wrongly

suppressed window returns on the next run, and the sheet-I readout was a resume chain - so this difference could have paid on the real corpus.

**Ruling:**

---

### done-gate-11 — Done as a subdivision verdict — whether a done child counts as having served the area

**DIVERGED** · evidence **UNPROVEN** · yield **decides-certificates (holo3 does strictly more work than holo2 and may gain from it; the population is measured zero in holo2's recorded census on both real sheets, and that census is a lower bound)** · classification trail: reader CARRIED-DIFFERENT → refuter reclassified to DIVERGED → second lens DIVERGED

When a window is cut into corners, does a corner that was already covered count as having served the area?

The mechanism: a window that exhausts its placement ladder is quartered into four corners. When they report back, the parent either rests or gets

one last expensive full-extent correlation pass; what counts as a corner having served its area decides which.

holo2 counts three exits as served - placed, area already covered, or settled placed - and if any corner served, the parent rests for good.

holo3 rests the parent only if a corner was actually PLACED. A corner that left because its area was already covered is settled but not placed, so

the parent is woken, armed for the cold pass and pushed back. At its next pop it is retired only if the parent's own area is covered - which one

covered corner does not imply, and neither do all four when the parent is not square, since the corners then leave an uncovered band. In those

cases holo3 runs a full-extent pass holo2 would never have run.

Evidence: holo2's recorded census found no subdivision corner ever gated as covered on either real sheet - 0 of 57 on the hp sheet, 0 of 15 on the

xerox sheet at the coarsest rung, none at finer rungs - so the clause never fired there. That census is explicitly a lower bound, and there is no

recorded holo3 gating rate on a real sheet at all.

Options: keep holo3's behaviour (it does more work and can only gain certificates by it); port holo2's rule now; archive for a later port; or spend

a costed measurement with no new scanning - counting subdivided parents with a covered child on an archived sheet-I resume state, minutes of CPU.

Recommendation: keep holo3's and run that count before ruling - its version is the safe direction (it spends CPU where holo2 rested and only ever

adds a placement chance), so the only real question is cost, and the archived corpus can answer that without paper.

**Ruling:**

---

### done-gate-12 — The colour gap — window-granular done-ness cannot suppress a per-lane colour row; the cell-level emission mask is the colour done gate

**HOLO3-ONLY** · evidence **UNPROVEN** · yield **decides-order-only** · classification trail: reader HOLO3-ONLY → refuter upheld

*(No second lens ran on this row. What follows is its reader's text with the refuter's corrections; there is no separate options list.)*

**What it does.** Whether the colour endgame stops. Nothing retires the colour evidence row, so the only done-ness colour gets is that certified tiles' cells are excluded from what the lane assembles and emits.

**holo2** — absent — holo2 has no colour path at all; its FORMATS can name colour rows but the codecs fail fatally, and no colour number of any kind exists for it.

*Where:* _(no path)_

**holo3** — The colour lane subject is (capture, level, page) and its retire predicate is a constant False, so the row never retires. Done-ness enters two levels down: suppressed_tile_codes / emission_mask drop the cells of fully-certified tiles from every window's contribution to the region assembly and to the head fit, and color_refine refuses a window whose every (tile, stream) pair is certified and refuses to prepare when no cargo cell survives the emission mask. Window-granular suppression exists for colour only through the shared place / calibrate / revisit rows.

*Where:* `src/holo/receiver/plugins/color_region.py:695-701`; `src/holo/receiver/plugins/color_sites.py:46-70`; `src/holo/receiver/plugins/color_region.py:363-372`; `src/holo/receiver/plugins/color_region.py:515-527`; `src/holo/receiver/plugins/color_refine.py:351-368`; `src/holo/receiver/plugins/color_refine.py:381-389`; `src/holo/receiver/store.py:396-398` · *scheduler row / doc:* SCHEDULE.md:590-593 ('Colour's evidence row is per-LANE, so window-granular done-ness cannot suppress it — that is the gate's honest colour gap')

**Evidence.**

- (holo3) the colour sim is unmoved job for job when the gate lands — every window there still touches one of its 21 open tiles — *artefact:* src/holo/receiver/docs/SCHEDULE.md:589-592
- (holo3) 'colour evidence is per LANE, so window-granular done-ness cannot suppress the colour lane's work — that is where colour endgame cost actually sits' — *artefact:* src/holo/receiver/docs/conversion_docs/OPEN-2026-08-31.md:128-131
- (holo2) holo2 has no colour number of any kind, by construction — *artefact:* ARTEFACTS.md:817 (gap G-17)

**Recommendation (reader).** keep holo3's for this campaign, and record the gap as a scheduled line rather than a retirement blocker. It is not a holo2 port question — there is nothing on the holo2 side to port — but it is the one place in this family where a mechanism is known to be missing and its cost is named ('where colour endgame cost actually sits') with no number attached. The cheap first measurement is a per-lane cost census on an existing colour run's events; no decode.

**Reader's notes.** Two facts about the emission mask worth carrying: it is the ONLY done-ness colour assembly sees, and store.suppressed_codes (off the cached done_grid) and color_sites.suppressed_tile_codes (rebuilt from the certificate mapping) are two spellings of the same set, used by color_refine and color_region respectively. They agree today; nothing pins that they must.

The mask reaches further than the row says: emission masks are consumed not only by color_region.py:363-372 and :515-527 but by color_region_head.py:330, :407, :427 (the head fit) AND color_arms.py:104-109, :233-238 (the colour-uplift arms), so done-ness gates the uplift arms too. The 'two spellings' note is right and worth keeping; the one asymmetry is that color_sites.suppressed_tile_codes (color_sites.py:46-62) applies no on-grid clip where tiles.done_tile_grid (tiles.py:567-580) does — they agree while every certificate is on-grid, and nothing pins that.

*Reason:* Colour gap is real and correctly stated; the mask's consumer list is incomplete and one gap id is misnumbered.

## Close

### close-10 — The default-mode exhaustion consult — holo3 forces one unconditional full close at the end of every non-verified run; holo2 does not

**DIVERGED** · evidence **MEASURED** · yield **nothing observable** · classification trail: reader CARRIED-DIFFERENT → refuter reclassified to DIVERGED → second lens DIVERGED

Mechanism: what happens at the very end of a run that never verified. holo3 always pays one full solve there; holo2 usually pays none.

holo2: the end-of-run look reuses the same gate the mid-run looks used, so it is refused whenever nothing has landed since the last one - which at exhaustion is the normal case - and even when it runs it still honours the readiness and new-answer gates. A default open run ends with zero extra solves.

holo3: the end-of-run look is unconditional whenever at least one certificate exists, and it bypasses the readiness gate and the new-answer gate (src/holo/receiver/engine.py:598-601). This is deliberate and pinned by a test named for it (src/holo/receiver/tests/test_h3_engine_contract.py:865).

It can never change an outcome. Readiness is exactly necessary for a solve - a block's rank can never exceed the distinct symbols it holds - so the forced attempt can only return the same open verdict the counts already implied.

It buys one real thing: an open run always ends with a genuine per-block rank report rather than a counts-only one, which is what an operator reads to decide whether another capture is worth making.

Evidence, previously called unproven, is now measured from artefacts already on disk. The largest colour volume in the record - sheet H, seven captures, 4,237 certificates, 121,967.7 CPU-seconds - ended open with a real solve having run, and the entire consult budget for that run was 0.881 CPU-seconds. Sheet I: 0.589 to 1.311 s. e029: 0.192 to 0.333 s.

Options: (a) keep holo3's and record the reason; (b) restore holo2's skip by adding readiness to the end-of-run condition, one clause - but this would cost the rank report and buys under a second; (c) leave it and re-check if a much larger volume ever appears.

Recommendation: keep holo3's and close the question. Record the proposed one-clause fix as unnecessary rather than as a standing option, and note that the cost objection that opened this row is now answered at under one second on the largest volume ever decoded.

**Ruling:**

---

### close-12 — What happens when the close finds an integrity failure — a durable terminal record versus an aborting exception

**DIVERGED** · evidence **UNPROVEN** · yield **nothing observable** · classification trail: reader DIVERGED → refuter upheld → second lens DIVERGED

Mechanism: what the receiver does when the certified evidence contradicts itself, or the recovered payload fails its digest.

holo2: makes it a delivered, permanent fact. The failure is written through the ordinary commit path as a closing record, the diagnostics are published, the ending summary explains, and the run directory is frozen - a rerun reports the same verdict and cannot re-attempt.

holo3: makes it an abort. The error unwinds out of the engine before the finishing step runs, so the run's entire certificate harvest, its event log and its resumable snapshot are never written; the command line prints one line and exits 1. Only the launch line and the tree description survive, written at start.

holo3 does catch contradictions earlier - at the moment a certificate is folded in, not at the close (src/holo/receiver/store.py:903, :914) - which narrows the window but does not change the disposition.

The two trees err in opposite directions. holo2 over-freezes: a digest mismatch permanently condemns a page that more captures might have fixed. holo3 under-preserves: a contradiction found after eight hours of colour decoding throws that work away.

Evidence: none either way. No integrity failure has ever been observed on paper, on any receiver; the corpus holds five verified closes and two opens, and every close that ran was byte-exact. Both dispositions are untested by reality.

Options: (a) keep holo3's; (b) port the narrow half - write the run directory products before the error escapes, so an abort leaves a diagnosable event log and, where the set is still consistent, a resumable snapshot; (c) port holo2's durable record and session freeze as well; (d) accept the loss.

Recommendation: port only the narrow half. Wrap the run loop so the finishing step still writes its products, then re-raise - roughly a dozen lines plus a test, no decode needed. Do not port the freeze: condemning a page on a digest mismatch is the more dangerous half, and holo3's resume model makes re-deriving the close from the certificate set free. State plainly in the entry that a contradiction caught at fold leaves a continuable snapshot while one caught during the close does not; the second case is diagnosable only.

Worth putting to Germano as a doctrine question: holo1 and holo2 treat a failed digest as terminal for the page; on holo3 the same event is transient. Neither has ever fired.

**Ruling:**

---

### close-13 — The durable close record, the terminal-session freeze, and the close-detach property

**ABSENT** · evidence **MEASURED** · yield **decides-certificates** · classification trail: reader ABSENT → refuter upheld

*(No second lens ran on this row. What follows is its reader's text with the refuter's corrections; there is no separate options list.)*

**What it does.** On a resume: whether a run re-derives the close or reads a stored answer, and whether an already-closed state may keep certifying.

**holo2** — A verifying close stages the payload as a blob and writes a `CloseRecord` carrying its ref; a terminal record freezes the session, and a later run reads the status off the STORE and re-reads the payload through the blob path with ref verification, reporting `resumed_terminal`. `--no-close` is the documented escape: it does not read the record and supersedes it on the first commit of the new run.

*Where:* `holo2/src/holo2/solve/close.py:503-514`; `holo2/src/holo2/engine/orchestrator.py:924-948`; `holo2/src/holo2/format/fountain_close.py:862-897`; `holo2/src/holo2/cli.py:585-599`

**holo3** — Absent by design. holo3 persists FACTS (`facts.npz` at RunDone) and nothing else; a `--resume` folds them back through `_ingest` and the close is recomputed from scratch on the first consult. A verified run writes the payload to `--out` and the snapshot; a resumed run over the same facts recomputes the same close.

*Where:* `src/holo/receiver/engine.py:639-662`; `src/holo/receiver/engine.py:248-249`; `src/holo/receiver/engine.py:251-266` · *scheduler row / doc:* 05-fountain.md:267-271 (explicit DROP: "Resume/journal/replay: close_record_from_result, CloseRecord/BlobRef/DurableCloseStatus, certificate_frontier_sha256, terminal-record immutability"); SCHEDULE.md:382-385 (holo3's resume is one fact snapshot folded back through ordinary ingest)

**Evidence.**

- (holo1) close detach is lossless — a close is a pure derivation from the certificate set and a later closing run recomputes it byte-exactly — *artefact:* src/holo/receiver/docs/retirement_docs-2026-09/ARTEFACTS.md:592 (cites src/holo/receive/session.py:662)
- (holo3) the sheet-I four-run resume chain re-derived the close on every closing run: VERIFIED 2445b95db5b49bf3, 1,798,761 B, three times, over 5,543 -> 5,866 -> 5,866 -> 5,880 certificates — *artefact:* src/holo/receiver/docs/retirement_docs-2026-09/ARTEFACTS.md:235 and :225
- (holo1) the e028 verified-close trap — the failure mode a retained close record creates — *artefact:* src/holo/receiver/docs/retirement_docs-2026-09/ARTEFACTS.md:591

**Recommendation (reader).** accept the loss — and note it is a gain, not a loss, for yield. Because the close is a pure derivation from the certificate set (holo1's own finding, ARTEFACTS.md:592), a stored close record buys nothing but the freeze, and the freeze is what `--no-close` existed to escape. holo3's resume chain proves the derivation on the deepest read on record. The one thing the record bought that holo3 has not replaced is the operator warning that an existing terminal answer is being set aside — meaningless without a stored answer.

**Reader's notes.** Mostly out-of-filter (journal/replay), included because the freeze does change whether a state may keep certifying. holo3's snapshot IS a durable resume surface, so 05-fountain.md:191-192's aside that detach semantics are "meaningless without resumable state" is stale of the tree that shipped.

**Refuter (upheld).** *Corrections:* No change. Verified: holo2's durable record and blob path (solve/close.py:503-514, format/fountain_close.py:862-895, orchestrator.py:924-948, cli.py:585-599) and holo3's facts-only persistence (engine.py:248-249, :251-266, :639-662; the explicit DROP list at src/holo/receiver/docs/conversion_docs/05-fountain.md:267-271). The 'stale aside' observation is right: 05-fountain.md:191-192 does say detach-to-open is 'meaningless without resumable state', and holo3 shipped facts.npz + --resume.

*Reason:* Absence confirmed in code and in the drop list; the resume evidence reproduces.

**Ruling:**

## The dryness gate

### dryness-01 — The dryness gate: an admission hold that stops expensive work from starting while cheaper work is still running

**ABSENT** · evidence **n/a** · yield **decides-certificates, but only on fragment material and only through placement floors; nothing observable on any archived whole-sheet K run** · classification trail: reader ABSENT → refuter REJECTED an argument (classification word stands) → second lens ABSENT

The mechanism: an admission hold. An idle worker may not start an expensive job while a cheaper job is still running, even though its slot then sits empty.

holo2 has one. Three placement rows are marked held, and a held job waits while anything cheaper is ready or in flight, on the stated grounds that a cheap payload fire may certify the very window the expensive pass was going to search.

holo3 has none. One heap, cheapest ready job wins, no comparison with what is running. A hold was built on 2026-09-01 at 22:42 and deleted at 23:16 the same evening, and the correlation search was repriced to last instead. The contract now says so explicitly.

The evidence is thin on both sides and must be stated as such. Neither tree has a single number for the hold: holo2 never instrumented it, and every head-to-head in the record was run at one worker, where holo2's hold provably cannot fire at all. So the family has zero observations of the only clause that does anything.

What we do know: on all three current-tree runs of the healthy black-and-white sheet, no job in the held price band ever fired - the dearest placement fire was 60 against a band starting at 120 - so on that material a hold would have had nothing to hold. I verified this in the three event streams.

One argument in circulation must not go to the user: that a hold is harmless because it only defers. Both trees delete work at the moment a worker is picked up, so deferring can remove a fire outright. It is still harmless, but for a better reason - the work that gets deleted is exactly the work that could no longer mint anything, because the test is 'this window is already fully certified'.

The real exposure is elsewhere and neither the ordering nor the hold controls it: a page-correlation pass is one-shot per window, and both the pages it sweeps and the support it demands are decided at the instant it fires. Moving that instant changes the search. The page-domain design already accepts this - certificate counts may differ between worker counts - so no ordering change in this family is provably yield-neutral.

Options: keep holo3's behaviour and record the reason; port the in-flight clause now (about ten lines and one test); archive it as a brief for the first multi-worker fragment run; or accept the loss silently.

There is no cheap settling measurement. Running the old receiver on this material would be a fresh decode of an archived sheet and it would still not isolate the hold, because the two trees differ in a dozen other prices.

Recommendation: keep holo3's behaviour, and archive the ten-line clause as a brief rather than porting it blind. Record honestly that this is a design ruling with no measurement behind it on either side, and that the reason it is safe is the done-window retirement, not any claim that deferral is free.

*The first refuter rejected an argument in the reader's row; the second lens adjudicated it. Its corrections are in the map under this row's Notes.*

**Ruling:**

---

### dryness-02 — Which work the gate holds back: three named passes versus a whole price band

**ABSENT** · evidence **n/a** · yield **decides-order-only** · classification trail: reader ABSENT → refuter upheld

*(No second lens ran on this row. What follows is its reader's text with the refuter's corrections; there is no separate options list.)*

**What it does.** The reach of the hold - whether it applies only to the widened consult and the two page-correlation sweeps, or to every recovery pass above a price line.

**holo2** — The hold is DATA on the price table, not a threshold. Exactly three rows carry gated=True: wide_consult (120), cold_live (140) and cold_rest (150). The re-read pass (resample, 200) is deliberately not gated - place_tiers.py:189-197 argues that its price alone does the ordering, since it is the second half of a refinement that already waited behind everything. The scheduler copies the flag straight off the table onto every job it offers (place_sched.py:597), so the gate's reach is exactly whatever the table says and nothing else.

*Where:* `holo2/src/holo2/solve/place_tiers.py:153-177`; `holo2/src/holo2/solve/place_tiers.py:193-200`; `holo2/src/holo2/solve/place_sched.py:580-597`; `holo2/src/holo2/core/work.py:398`

**holo3** — absent. While it existed (9b15b48 to d399ba3) holo3's version was a PRICE BAND, not a per-row flag: cost >= 120 held. On holo3's ladder that band is far wider than holo2's three rows - it covers wide (120), the colour lane refit (160), pooled colour context (170), plane fields (180), refine and refine.color and revisit (200), both supplemental colour arms (210/220), local context (220), plane-K (230), the swim (240) and both colds (140/150 at the time). So holo3's gate would have held nine recovery rows that holo2 never gated, including every certificate-minting rescue row. Today none of them is held.

*Where:* `src/holo/receiver/engine.py:433-475` · *scheduler row / doc:* the deleted engine.DRYNESS_COST = 120 (git show 9b15b48, git show d399ba3)

**Evidence.**

- (holo3) tiles.local minted 213 certificates at 73% of the run's CPU on sheet I (machine 2); the four-run chain's later runs read 26 then 2, at about 3,000 cpu-s each — *artefact:* ARTEFACTS.md:703 and :735, citing src/holo/receiver/docs/conversion_docs/MEASUREMENTS-2026-08-31.md:30-31 and experiments/034-sheet-i-full-readout.md:89-93
- (holo3) run 2 of the sheet-I resume chain added 323 certificates - tiles.planek 281, tiles.local 26, tiles.retry 10, tiles.swim 6 - and produced the first VERIFIED close; run 4 added 14 more (planek 9, local 2, swim 1, retry 1, union 1) — *artefact:* ARTEFACTS.md:235, scratch/sheetI-20260831/report-resume*.json
- (holo3) without tiles.local the K sim volume is LOST (45 certificates); with it, 48 certificates and the volume comes back byte-exact, a strict superset, zero wrong — *artefact:* ARTEFACTS.md:701, citing src/holo/receiver/docs/SCHEDULE.md:436-489

**Recommendation (reader).** Keep holo3's (no gate). If the in-flight clause is ever ported per DRY-01, port holo2's SHAPE and not holo3's deleted one: a per-row boolean on the price declaration, set only on the two cold stages. A 120-price band would hold back the rows that actually mint holo3's late certificates - tiles.planek alone brought 281 of the 323 certificates that turned sheet I's first verified close, and tiles.local is the difference between a lost and a recovered sim volume. Holding those behind a cheap payload fire buys nothing and risks the endgame, which is precisely why holo2 kept its own re-read pass out of the gated set.

**Reader's notes.** holo2's three gated rows and holo3's nine-plus band were never compared on any run: holo3's gate lived 34 minutes and no decode was recorded on it. This row is a design-shape observation, not a measured one.

**Refuter (upheld).** *Corrections:* Verified: exactly three gated=True rows in holo2 (place_tiers.py:163 wide 120, :167 cold_live 140, :172 cold_rest 150), resample gated=False at :193-197, the flag copied straight onto the offered job at place_sched.py:597, and the deleted DRYNESS_COST=120 band (git show 9b15b48). All three evidence numbers resolve exactly (ARTEFACTS.md:701, :703, :235). Three corrections. (a) 'nine recovery rows' undercounts: by the current ladder (SCHEDULE.md:66-81) the >=120 band covers color.color_refit 160, color.pooled_ctx 170, fields 180, refine 200, refine.color 200, revisit 200, tiles.uplift 210/220, tiles.local 220, tiles.planek 230, tiles.swim 240 - ten plugin rows / eleven stages, plus wide and both colds. (b) TWO OF THEM WOULD NOT HAVE BEEN HELD ON THEIR FIRST FIRE: fields.py:804-806 (`FIELD_COST * attempts + RUNG_COST*subject[1]`) and color_pooled.py:188-190 (`POOLED_CTX_COST * attempts`) have no `if attempts else base` guard, and the engine passes attempts=0 on the first push (engine.py:306, incremented only at fire, :470), so their first fire prices at 0-4, below survey (10). Measured, not inferred: scratch/J-run.54028/events.jsonl contains JobDone plugin=fields cost=0 twice (and cost 2, 4), then 180/182/184, 360/362/364, ... (c) the argument that the re-read is deliberately ungated sits at place_tiers.py:178-192, not :189-197.

*Reason:* The shape claim (per-row flag vs price band) is correct and well-evidenced; only the row count and the two unguarded first fires need fixing.

**Ruling:**
