### dryness-04 — Where the page-correlation search sits in the price order relative to refinement and the residue rescue rows

**DIVERGED** · evidence **UNPROVEN** · yield **decides-certificates** · classification trail: reader DIVERGED → refuter upheld → second lens DIVERGED

The mechanism: where the page-correlation search sits in the price order, relative to refinement and to the rows that rescue individual tiles.

holo2 puts the search below refinement on the coarse lanes, and its own source comment records the resulting inversion as an open question for the user. holo2 has no tile-rescue rows at all, so its expensive end is just widened consult, correlation, refinement.

holo3 moves the search to the very top: every rescue row - refinement, revisit, the two supplemental colour arms, local context, the yellow-as-black rescue, the swim - now runs first, and the search is last in the tree. The move was made in the same commit that deleted the admission hold, with the reasoning that ordering by price should need no extra policy on top.

The evidence points the same way. Across the three whole-sheet runs in the record the correlation search committed zero poses in 5,332 fires while taking between an eighth and two thirds of every placement second; the rows now ahead of it minted 281, 213, 26 and 6 certificates on real paper.

But it has never been exercised. The only runs on the new prices are the three runs of the healthy black-and-white sheet, and none of them fired a single job in that part of the ladder - I checked all three event streams, the dearest fire was cost 60 against a band starting at 120. Everything said about the new order on colour or fragments is extrapolation.

It matters more than an ordering usually would, because the correlation search runs once per window and decides what to search from the state of the page set at the moment it fires. Running it after the rescue rows means it sees more open pages and a different support floor. This is a yield mechanism, not just a schedule.

One correction for the record: the claim that a second expensive rescue fire falls behind the search is off by one - the first two fires cost the same and only the third crosses. Measured in the fragment run's event stream (711 fires at the base price, 185 at double).

Options: keep holo3's order; revert to the old order; archive the question; or measure it.

The measurement is affordable and uses paper we already have: re-run the fragment sheet's resume on the current tree and compare the certificate count and the search's pose count against the archived run. About one wall-hour at six workers and roughly 22 GB of memory - one decode, alone on the machine.

Recommendation: keep holo3's order, record it as reasoned but unexercised, and schedule that one fragment re-run as the first thing done on the new prices. It is the only material in the record where the search earns poses at all.

**Ruling:**

---

### dryness-05 — The escalation door: making repeated expensive work drain last without refusing it

**DIVERGED** · evidence **UNPROVEN** · yield **decides-order-only** · classification trail: reader CARRIED-DIFFERENT → refuter reclassified to DIVERGED → second lens DIVERGED

The mechanism: making repeated expensive work drain last without ever refusing it - a job that has failed several times sinks behind fresher work.

holo2 does this in one place: a single price function reads how much a piece of work has already spent and lifts its price for every re-attempt, unbounded. It is deliberately blind to which family the work belongs to, so it ages repeated payload decodes and repeated calibration and pose work.

holo3 does it per row: each row prices its own repeats, the engine just hands over the count. Six colour and rescue rows multiply their price by the repeat count. Repeated payload decodes are NOT aged - they cost the same forever - and neither are placement re-fires.

Two facts to fix before this goes further. The steepening starts one fire later than described: the first two fires of an escalating row cost the same and only the third doubles (measured in the fragment run's stream: 711 fires at the base price, 185 at double). And two rows - the plane-field fit and the pooled colour context - forgot the guard entirely, so their FIRST fire prices at zero and jumps ahead of the entire tree. That is measured, not inferred: an 8.4-second field fit ran at cost 0 in the fragment run.

The largest difference is one the original reading buried: holo2 ages repeated payload decodes and holo3 does not, and payload decoding is the row that mints most certificates - 60.8% of the whole-sheet run's processor time.

It does not follow that we lost yield. holo2's own measurement of its aging is the answer: attempts fell 39%, wall halved, and certificates were unchanged at 160 with the volume byte-exact on both arms. Aging is a cost mechanism, measured as such by the tree that has it.

And holo3 restrains repeats differently rather than not at all: a retry cannot re-run until the evidence it depends on has moved, and it retires the moment its tile certifies.

Options: keep holo3's behaviour; port the aging term to repeated payload decodes as a cost saving; archive it; or fix only the two rows that enter free.

The free first fire should be fixed regardless. It is a one-line guard per row, the other four rows already have it, and it is the one place in the tree where the most expensive work jumps ahead of the cheapest.

Recommendation: keep holo3's prices, fix the two unguarded rows, and log the missing payload aging as a cost-only improvement with holo2's measurement attached, not as a yield debt. Also note that neither tree ages placement re-fires - the outstanding proposal from the earlier residue report - and that this belongs to a separate line of work, not to retirement.

**Ruling:**

---

### dryness-07 — How big the expensive band is: the page set a cold search sweeps

**DIVERGED** · evidence **MEASURED** · yield **decides-certificates** · classification trail: reader DIVERGED → refuter upheld → second lens DIVERGED

The mechanism: how wide the expensive search casts. One flavour sweeps pages the run has already placed something on; the other sweeps everything else.

holo2 was always run with a single-page domain, so its second flavour existed but never swept anything. holo3 makes the page set dynamic and lets the second flavour sweep the whole addressable domain - 133 pages on the sheet format, thousands on small matrices - which is two to three orders of magnitude more work per fire.

This is measured, and it is the largest single cost in the record: on the fragment sheet the wide sweep fired 696 times for 9,640 processor-seconds and produced zero poses - half the entire run - at 13.9 seconds a fire against 1.1 for the narrow flavour. The narrow flavour, by contrast, earned 15 and 39 poses on the two runs and is not in question.

It is not only cost. The size of the declared page set raises the support the search demands, so the same window with the same evidence can be accepted at a small domain and refused at a large one. The design doc says this plainly - the narrower route's lower floor admits origins the wider route excludes.

So the honest correction to the earlier reading: the price of a search fire does not scale with how many pages it sweeps. The one row on the ladder whose cost varies by twelve times is the one row whose price is flat. That decoupling, not the missing admission hold, is what makes a large domain expensive.

One more fact from the same run: poses landed on 28 different pages, and the wide sweep committed none of them. Every page was opened by decoded address words. On the only fragment material we have, the wide sweep has never opened a page.

Neither an admission hold nor the repricing would have saved a second of that 9,640, because the run was told to drain completely and both are only deferrals.

Options: keep it and bound the domain per run with the existing flag; make the wide sweep opt-in when a run is told to drain; delete it; or measure it.

Recommendation: keep it - it is the only route that can reach a page nothing else has opened, and capping an evidence pass is exactly what the standing rule forbids - but write the fragment measurement into the design section as the worked instance, so the next operator sizes the page bound before starting a drain-to-exhaustion run.

If a number is wanted, the settling run is cheap: re-run the fragment resume on the current tree without the drain flag. About one wall-hour at six workers, one decode alone on the machine. The earlier estimate of 5.4 wall-hours read processor-hours as wall-hours; the archived runs took 34 and 56 minutes.

**Ruling:**

---

### dryness-08 — Nothing bounds how many expensive page searches run at once, and each one accumulates memory in proportion to the pages it sweeps

**DIVERGED** · evidence **UNPROVEN** · yield **decides-certificates (a run killed for memory certifies nothing further; no other channel)** · classification trail: reader DIVERGED → refuter upheld → second lens DIVERGED

The mechanism: nothing limits how many expensive page searches run at once, and each one holds a transform per page it sweeps until the fire ends.

Neither tree ever had such a limit. What changed is the size of what the missing limit guards: holo3's wide sweep covers a whole page domain where holo2's covered one page. Six workers each sweeping 133 pages hold, in the worst case, one or two transforms per page apiece.

The published numbers: 45.8 MiB per page measured, 5.95 GiB derived for a full domain. Recorded peaks on real runs are 48.8 GiB for the big colour sheet, with two later steps breaching at 60.5 and 60.2 GiB - and those two were 0.27 GiB apart at eight and six workers, so worker count is not the driver of the growth we have measured.

This is the one row in the family whose failure is total. Every other difference here moves work around; this one can kill a run, and a killed run certifies nothing more. The machine has 64 GB and the standing rule is one production decode at a time.

Against that: the one measured instance survived. The fragment run swept a 100-page domain at six workers to exhaustion and produced its report. The accumulation is a reading of the code, not a measured allocation, and only the first wide fire on a window pays the full domain, since later fires sweep only untried pages.

holo3 does bound one thing - two workers cannot be in the same row on the same window at once - but that only spreads the concurrent sweeps across different windows, which is the normal case.

One asymmetry to record rather than paper over: holo2 built the accounting for a per-fire residency budget and deliberately left the policy unwritten pending measurement; holo3 dropped the concept by ruling.

Options: keep it and record the exposure; build a concurrency limit; make the page bound memory-derived; or measure the peak on a wide-domain run.

The free measurement suggested earlier does not exist - the fragment run's report carries no memory figure, I checked. Getting one means re-running that decode with the memory ledger attached: about one wall-hour at six workers.

Recommendation: keep holo3's behaviour, add one sentence to the design's memory section saying the per-fire cache scales with the page domain and that memory, not price, is what bounds the page setting on a drain-to-exhaustion run, and take the peak figure opportunistically on the next fragment run. Do not invent a concurrency cap: a ceiling that reduces evidence is the one thing the standing rule forbids, and the only instance we have survived.

**Ruling:**

---

## Pricing

### pricing-02 — The two cold prices — 140/150 gated behind global dryness vs 250/260 ordered last by price

**DIVERGED** · evidence **MEASURED** · yield **decides-order-only** · classification trail: reader DIVERGED → refuter upheld → second lens DIVERGED

MECHANISM. A cold pass is the whole-page correlation sweep - the dearest thing the receiver does, and the only route that can place a window with no neighbour to lean on. Its price decides how much of the sheet's cheap work happens before that sweep starts.

HOLO2. Two flavours at 140 (pages already posed this session) and 150 (the rest), +20 per finer footing, both behind an admission gate: no cold pass may start while anything cheaper is ready or running.

HOLO2. Because 140/150 sit under the re-read price of 200, a shallow cold pass could still be admitted ahead of a waiting re-read; that inversion closes only at footing depth 3 for the rest flavour and 4 for the live one.

HOLO3. 250 and 260, +20 per footing, no gate anywhere. Both flavours now sit above every other row in the tree, so 'everything cheaper first' holds at every footing instead of only the deep ones - the price alone produces what the gate used to enforce.

EVIDENCE. The one head-to-head says the gate saves nothing on a run that drains, and that holo3's plain ladder reproduces the ordering at one worker (first cold fire at 53%/44% of the placement stream against holo2's 44%/36%).

EVIDENCE. That comparison is stronger than it was read to be: holo2's gate is inert at one worker, so those percentages measure the PRICE, not the gate.

EVIDENCE. Nothing in the corpus can show a certificate difference. The runs that fire cold (sheet J: 1,330 and 847 live, 696 rest) drain to an empty queue; the runs that stop early (the three e029 arms, ~2,900 jobs unfired at a verified close) never fire cold at all.

EVIDENCE. The 1,356-versus-1,360 pair often quoted here is a page-domain result, not a cold-price result - no cold pass fired in either arm. It licenses 'ordering changes certificates at a preempting close' generally, and nothing about 250/260.

OPTION. Keep 250/260 ungated.

OPTION. Restore 140/150 and the gate.

OPTION. Keep the prices, restore only the gate.

OPTION. Measure: sheet J at eight workers, one arm per setting, about 3 CPU-hours each from stored captures.

RECOMMENDATION. Keep holo3's. The only head-to-head we have says the gate buys nothing on a draining run, and 250/260 makes 'everything cheaper first' unconditional where holo2's inverted at shallow footings.

RECOMMENDATION. Correct two records with the map: src/holo/receiver/docs/conversion_docs/13-engine-diag-cli.md:113-118 still declares 140/150 and the deleted rework constants 'verbatim', and ARTEFACTS.md:104 and :768 write the null arm's flag as --pages 1 where the recorded argv says --max-pages 1.

**Ruling:**

---

### pricing-04 — COST_INGEST = 10 — a declared scan converted to its resolution ladder as priced work vs holo3's up-front intake

**CARRIED-DIFFERENT** · evidence **UNPROVEN** · yield **decides-order-only** · classification trail: reader CARRIED-DIFFERENT → refuter upheld

*(No second lens ran on this row. What follows is its reader's text with the refuter's corrections; there is no separate options list.)*

**What it does.** Whether a second or third capture's conversion overlaps decoding of the first, or blocks it; and how much raster is resident before any job runs.

**holo2** — Each declared scan is minted as one ingest descriptor priced at 10, below every other class, because it is the work that creates all other work. The provider's derivation chain admits scan N+1 only once scan N's record stands, so exactly one survey is in flight whatever the pool width and the raster transient is bounded without a gate. A pooled run therefore overlaps the next capture's conversion with placement on the capture that just landed.

*Where:* `holo2/src/holo2/engine/recipe.py:168-177`; `holo2/src/holo2/engine/recipe.py:580`

**holo3** — Intake is not a scheduled row at all: `_start` calls `_intake_run` once and converts every declared scan to its full ladder of per-plane rasters before the frontier exists, then folds the Captures. The cheapest priced row is `survey` at a flat 10, one fire per capture. So the 10 survives as a price; the streaming property does not.

*Where:* `src/holo/receiver/engine.py:102-109`; `src/holo/receiver/engine.py:229-247`; `src/holo/receiver/plugins/intake.py (ingest_captures)`; `src/holo/receiver/plugins/survey.py:30-31`; `src/holo/receiver/plugins/survey.py:807-809` · *scheduler row / doc:* SCHEDULE.md:59 (survey, price 10, the cheapest row); SCHEDULE.md:339-341 'Ingest converts each scan into the resolution ladder and the survey finds the printed regions'

**What actually differs.** holo2 mints one priced descriptor per scan and lets the pool interleave conversion with decoding, one capture in flight at a time. holo3 does all conversion serially in `_start`, before the first job, and prices only the survey. Inputs differ (holo2: a declared scan is a work subject; holo3: captures are constructor input), when it fires differs (holo2: any time the queue reaches 10; holo3: before the loop), and the memory shape differs (holo3 holds every capture's ladder for the whole run; holo2's transient is one capture wide).

**Evidence.**

- (holo3) survey 1 job, 8.51 cpu-s of 1,639.8 cpu-s total on the one-capture e029 default-domain run (the intake conversion itself is not accounted in any plugin row) — *artefact:* scratch/page-domain-qual/merged-default.log, licensed at ARTEFACTS.md:104 and ARTEFACTS.md:734
- (holo2) no ingest wall or memory number of its own; the nearest record is the prepared-planes doctrine (ingest ~19.5 GB resident; workers-8 and workers-4 both SIGKILLed on a 64 GB machine while serial survived) — *artefact:* ARTEFACTS.md:668-672

**Recommendation (reader).** keep holo3's, and record the gap. What would settle it: a multi-capture holo3 run already writes per-plugin cpu in report.json but attributes nothing to intake, so the discriminating number is a wall split on an existing multi-capture invocation (e.g. the sheet-H four-capture argv at ARTEFACTS.md:256) — a receiver run, therefore out of scope for this campaign; the cheap alternative is one instrumented line in `_start` on the next real decode.

**Reader's notes.** Relevant to memory, not only order: holo3's largest colour runs already sit near the machine's kill line (step 4 breached at both workers 8 and workers 6, ARTEFACTS.md:737), and up-front intake of every capture's ladder is one contributor the artefacts cannot separate (G-25). This row does NOT claim a yield effect; on a single-capture run the two designs are indistinguishable.

**Refuter (upheld).** *Corrections:* None load-bearing. Verified: src/holo/receiver/engine.py:229 calls `_intake_run` inside `_start` before the frontier exists, src/holo/receiver/engine.py:102-109 delegates to plugins/intake.ingest_captures, and src/holo/receiver/engine.py:230-232 keeps every capture's rasters resident for the run (`self._rasters`, `_planes_by_lane`); survey is a flat 10 with no rung term (src/holo/receiver/plugins/survey.py:29-31, :807-809). holo2's ingest entry is RecipeEntry(TASK_INGEST, …, COST_INGEST) at holo2/src/holo2/engine/recipe.py:580 with the streaming rationale at :168-177. Optional precision: cite survey.py:29-31 rather than :30-31 (STAGE_SURVEY is on :29).

*Reason:* The streaming-vs-up-front delta and the UNPROVEN status are both correct at HEAD.

**Ruling:**

### pricing-09 — The rework/aging band max(base, 90 + 5*(n-1)) + premium — a re-attempt's price grows with its own spend history

**ABSENT** · evidence **MEASURED** · yield **decides-certificates** · classification trail: reader ABSENT → refuter upheld → second lens ABSENT

MECHANISM. A job that has already fired and failed can be woken again. What its second, tenth or fiftieth attempt costs decides how much new evidence has accumulated before it is retried, and it is the only thing that damps an endless re-arm loop.

HOLO2. One uniform engine rule over every scheduled family: a re-attempt is lifted into a band starting at 90 and rising 5 per prior attempt, read from that job's own durable spend row. The first re-attempt lands between the word routes; the band passes wide at the 7th, cold at the 11th, the re-read at the 23rd. Deliberately uncapped.

HOLO3. No engine-side aging at all - a re-fired worker pushes at exactly its declared price. Eleven of seventeen rows ignore the attempt count entirely, including every payload row, so a payload retry costs 20 on its first fire and on its fiftieth. Six colour rows price their own re-runs instead.

EVIDENCE. holo2 measured aging as a large saving at zero yield cost on its corpus: attempts down 39%, wall 96.1 to 49.1 s, certified 160 to 160 unchanged, three volumes byte-exact both ways.

EVIDENCE. holo3 measured the deletion as certificate-identical on both simulations, 49/49 and 279/279.

EVIDENCE. Both null-proofs were taken on runs that drain - holo3's at one worker under --complete - which is exactly the regime where a price cannot move a certificate. They show nothing else broke; they say nothing about yield.

EVIDENCE. The stored real sheet does not drain. The three e029 arms verify with about 2,900 jobs still queued, and re-fires are most of that queue: the two payload rows are 19,841 of 23,855 jobs and 1,315 of 1,639.8 CPU-seconds at 6.9 and 6.1 fires per subject, every one priced at 20.

EVIDENCE. The corpus already shows what an ordering change is worth there: a scheduling change that fired no cold pass at all still moved the count 1,360 to 1,356 on the same capture, payload byte-exact.

OPTION. Keep holo3's behaviour.

OPTION. Port the band back into the engine - this reopens the 2026-08-29 ruling that the engine may not determine cost.

OPTION. Put a rework term inside the two payload rows' own price functions, which that ruling does allow.

OPTION. Measure first: add such a term on a scratch copy and re-run the default e029 arm against the stored capture - about 1,600 CPU-seconds per arm, roughly ten minutes of wall each at eight workers, no paper and no new scan.

RECOMMENDATION. Keep holo3's behaviour and record the cost honestly rather than porting: the user ruled the engine may not age cost, and holo2's own numbers say aging bought CPU and no yield.

RECOMMENDATION. Do not carry holo3's null-proof as if it settled yield; it was taken in the only regime that cannot show the effect. If the re-fire population is ever worth damping, the rows' own price functions are where it belongs, and the measurement above is cheap enough to run before deciding.

RECOMMENDATION. Correct two records: 13-engine-diag-cli.md:118 and 00-contract.md:319 both still declare the deleted rework band as a build pin, while a test forbids the engine from owning any such name.

**Ruling:**

---

### pricing-11 — COST_REFINE = 200 — pose re-work priced at the top, 'after the ladder' as an emergent property

**CARRIED-DIFFERENT** · evidence **UNPROVEN** · yield **decides-order-only** · classification trail: reader CARRIED-DIFFERENT → refuter upheld

*(No second lens ran on this row. What follows is its reader's text with the refuter's corrections; there is no separate options list.)*

**What it does.** When a certificate-labelled pose revision (and, in holo3, the mop-up and colour refinement rows that share its price) is allowed to fire relative to placement, payload and cold work.

**holo2** — The refine entry declares 200, above cold_rest (150), the dearest tier any other family declares, and is NOT gated — the price alone makes 'after the ladder' emergent instead of a phase. A refine descriptor carries the ordinary lane term (200 + 2d), and the entry's own comment prints the crossover table: refine is overtaken by cold_rest from rung 3 and cold_live from rung 4, both reachable at 4800 dpi, and the aging band lifts other families past 200 from their 24th re-fire.

*Where:* `holo2/src/holo2/engine/recipe.py:293-376`; `holo2/src/holo2/engine/recipe.py:597`; `holo2/src/holo2/solve/refine.py:637`

**holo3** — REFINE_COST 200, flat: no lane term, no ordinal, and three rows share it as declared round-robin peers (K refinement, colour refinement, the mop-up revisit row). Because cold now costs 250/260, refine precedes every cold pass at every rung unconditionally — the property holo2 could only claim through rung 2.

*Where:* `src/holo/receiver/plugins/refine.py:68-69`; `src/holo/receiver/plugins/refine.py:663-665`; `src/holo/receiver/plugins/color_refine.py:59`; `src/holo/receiver/plugins/color_refine.py:463-465`; `src/holo/receiver/plugins/revisit.py:131-133` · *scheduler row / doc:* SCHEDULE.md:73-75 (refine / refine.color / revisit, three same-price peers at 200); SCHEDULE.md:391-407 'How this keeps the spirit of v1'

**What actually differs.** Same constant, three differences. (1) holo2 adds the lane term (200 + 2d) so refine subjects on coarse rungs go first; holo3 is flat, so refine subjects fire FIFO within the price. (2) holo2's refine re-attempts age past 200 from the 24th re-fire; holo3's never move. (3) The crossover with cold disappears in holo3 because cold was repriced to 250/260 — holo2's own pin records that inversion as OPEN for the user, and holo3's reprice closes it in the direction the ruling wanted.

**Evidence.**

- (holo2) refine outranks every declared tier cost and every ordinary pass at every depth; cold_rest overtakes it from rung 3 and cold_live from rung 4 (MAX_LANE_DEPTH = 4, reachable at 4800 dpi) — *artefact:* holo2/tests/engine/test_cost_vocabulary.py:404-470
- (holo3) (200, 'refine', 'refine'), (200, 'refine.color', 'refine'), (200, 'revisit', 'refine') — marked shared-price peers — *artefact:* src/holo/receiver/tests/test_h3_schedule.py:33-35
- (holo3) across the five completed post-change volumes `refine` fired 717 times, `refine.color` 3,899 and `revisit` 593, producing 0 route=refine poses — zero, everywhere — *artefact:* ARTEFACTS.md:528-531

**Recommendation (reader).** keep holo3's. The flat price makes the ruled property unconditional and no artefact suggests the lane term ever mattered for refinement. What would settle the (1)/(2) deltas: nothing cheap — refinement adopted nothing at all on five volumes (ARTEFACTS.md:528-531), so its ordering is currently unobservable, and holo3's refinement writes no events (G-10). Record as UNPROVEN-because-unexercised rather than proposing a run.

**Reader's notes.** The zero-adoption finding is adjudicated as honest refusals rather than a stuck gate (POST-FORENSICS §A.1, ARTEFACTS.md:531-535). Yield-relevance is recorded as order-only for the PRICE; refinement's own yield is the refinement family's row.

Ladder golden lines :33-35 are exact. The zero-adoption evidence resolves at ARTEFACTS.md:528-532 verbatim (717 / 3,899 / 593, zero route=refine poses).

### pricing-12 — The payload lane premium of a UNION attempt — dearest component vs shallowest rung ordinal

**CARRIED-DIFFERENT** · evidence **UNPROVEN** · yield **decides-order-only** · classification trail: reader CARRIED-DIFFERENT → refuter upheld

*(No second lens ran on this row. What follows is its reader's text with the refuter's corrections; there is no separate options list.)*

**What it does.** Where a cross-capture union attempt sits inside the 20..28 payload band, i.e. whether it is decoded before or after the single-capture attempts on the same tile.

**holo2** — `lane_premium` takes the MAXIMUM depth over the footings the attempt will read, on the stated principle that 'an attempt is not cheaper than its dearest component'. A scan or a retry is scoped to one footing and takes that footing's depth; a union takes the max over the ppc's resident footings; an empty set answers 0.

*Where:* `holo2/src/holo2/solve/payload.py:317-336`; `holo2/src/holo2/solve/payload.py:933-970`

**holo3** — `_union_cost` is PAYLOAD_COST + 2 * `store.union_rung_ordinal(ppc)`, and that helper returns the MINIMUM level index among the captures that reach this ppc. Scan and single-capture retry take the level index directly, exactly as holo2 does; only the union (and the union-origin retry, tiles.local, tiles.planek and tiles.swim, which reuse the same helper) take the min.

*Where:* `src/holo/receiver/plugins/tiles.py:774-776`; `src/holo/receiver/store.py:344-348` · *scheduler row / doc:* SCHEDULE.md:65 (tiles.union at 20, the payload band)

**What actually differs.** max over component depths (holo2) vs min over the captures' level indices for that ppc (holo3). On the ordinary case — every capture declared at one dpi — the two are identical, because one ppc sits at the same index on every ladder. They differ only on a MIXED-dpi stack, where the same ppc is a coarse rung of the finer capture and a fine rung of the coarser one: holo2 charges the dearest reading, holo3 the cheapest, so holo3 schedules cross-dpi unions earlier by up to 8 cost points inside a 20..28 band.

**Evidence.**

- (holo3) first cross-dpi union certificates on paper: 1,508 of the 4,307 four-capture (2x1200 + 2x2400) sheet-H harvest — *artefact:* ARTEFACTS.md:622 (src/holo/receiver/docs/conversion_docs/MEASUREMENTS-2026-08-31.md:18-19)
- (holo2) cross-DPI union (1200 + 2400): 39 / 39 -> 41/64; joint = exact union of the two singles' sets — *artefact:* ARTEFACTS.md:620

**Recommendation (reader).** keep holo3's, and record the delta. The whole payload band is 20..28 and sits under every placement rung on both sides, so the reordering is confined to payload work on mixed-dpi stacks; no artefact shows it mattering. If a ruling wants the stated principle back it is a one-line change (max instead of min) with a golden-price consequence for tiles.union/local/planek/swim.

**Reader's notes.** Mixed-dpi stacks are not hypothetical — the sheet-H four-capture and seven-capture runs are exactly this shape (ARTEFACTS.md:251-257). I could not find any comment in holo3 stating the intent of `union_rung_ordinal`; the name reads as 'the canonical rung of this ppc' rather than as a deliberate reversal of holo2's rule, so this may be an unnoticed drift rather than a decision.

**Refuter (upheld).** *Corrections:* max-vs-min verified exactly (holo2/src/holo2/solve/payload.py:334-336 `max(depths) if depths else 0`; src/holo/receiver/store.py:344-348 `min(indices)`). TWO THINGS THE ROW UNDERSTATES, both from store.py:344-348. (1) `union_rung_ordinal(ppc)` iterates EVERY capture in the store, not the captures that reach THIS tile — holo2's max is over the footings the attempt will actually read. So on a mixed-dpi stack holo3's price can be set by a capture that is not a component of the attempt at all; the delta is wider than 'min instead of max over the same set'. (2) holo2 answers 0 for an empty footing set by an explicit and reasoned clause; holo3's `min([])` would raise ValueError. Neither changes the recommendation, but the delta paragraph should say both. Cross-dpi evidence resolves at ARTEFACTS.md:620 and :622, and the sheet-H four-capture shape (2x1200 + 2x2400) at ARTEFACTS.md:256.

*Reason:* Direction of the reversal confirmed; the scope of the reversal is larger than the row states.

**Ruling:**

### pricing-14 — The fire-count tripwire — max_fires_per_worker = 256, charged at arm time

**ABSENT** · evidence **UNPROVEN** · yield **integrity** · classification trail: reader ABSENT → refuter upheld

*(No second lens ran on this row. What follows is its reader's text with the refuter's corrections; there is no separate options list.)*

**What it does.** Whether a non-converging wake graph stops the run loudly or simply runs forever at cost.

**holo2** — Every fire is counted per rendered worker id and charged after the idle test and before dispatch, so a pool cannot outrun it; exceeding 256 raises FireCountTripwire and kills the run. It is declared as an observability tripwire, not a semantic bound: set well above the expected wake-graph depth so a non-converging fixpoint is loud instead of slow. Both admission sources are charged the same way.

*Where:* `holo2/src/holo2/engine/recipe.py:423-431`; `holo2/src/holo2/engine/orchestrator.py:505-537`

**holo3** — Absent. `worker.attempts` is incremented and bucketed into a histogram (1 / 2 / 3-5 / 6-10 / >10) for the diag EngineStat, and the run summary prints cpu-s per fire and fires per subject, but nothing raises and nothing is bounded. A runaway wake loop would show as a growing '>10' bucket and be visible only to whoever is watching.

*Where:* `src/holo/receiver/engine.py:46-54`; `src/holo/receiver/engine.py:268-276`; `src/holo/receiver/engine.py:278-296`; `src/holo/receiver/engine.py:470-471` · *scheduler row / doc:* no SCHEDULE.md paragraph; the observable is the diag `attempts_hist` and the run log's churn line (fires per subject)

**Evidence.**

- (holo2) no artefact records the tripwire ever firing; its own comment calls it an observability tripwire rather than a semantic bound — *artefact:* holo2/src/holo2/engine/recipe.py:427-431
- (holo3) e029 default-domain run: ctx 46.0 fires per subject, calibrate 9.1, tiles.scan 6.9, tiles.retry 6.1 (base arm: 42.5 / 14.3 / 11.2 / 10.0) — *artefact:* scratch/page-domain-qual/{merged-default,base-e029}.log, licensed at ARTEFACTS.md:104/:734

**Recommendation (reader).** accept the loss. The measured fire counts on a real holo3 sheet peak around 46 per subject, an order of magnitude under holo2's 256, and holo3 already publishes the per-subject fire count in both the diag pane and the run summary. Porting it back would mean re-introducing an engine-side abort, which the same 2026-08-29 ruling that deleted the aging term pushed out of the engine.

**Reader's notes.** By the vocabulary this could be filed OUT-OF-FILTER as a safeguard; it is written as a full row because the brief names B61 and because the abort does change what a run produces (it ends it). Nothing in holo3 would notice a wake graph that fails to converge except the operator.

**Refuter (upheld).** *Corrections:* None. Verified: holo2/src/holo2/engine/recipe.py:423-431 PriceTable.max_fires_per_worker = 256 with the observability-tripwire comment; charged in orchestrator.py:519-538 `_charge`, after `_bind` and before dispatch, keyed on the rendered worker id, raising FireCountTripwire. holo3 has attempt_bucket at engine.py:44-52 and `_note_attempt` at :268-276 feeding the diag histogram only; nothing raises. The base-arm fire counts (42.5 / 14.3 / 11.2 / 10.0) are correctly attributed here — unlike pricing-09.

*Reason:* Absence confirmed in code; the 'accept the loss' recommendation rests on measured fire counts an order of magnitude under 256.

### pricing-15 — The tried-set floor — identical inputs never re-run — and whether that memory is durable

**CARRIED-DIFFERENT** · evidence **MEASURED** · yield **decides-certificates** · classification trail: reader CARRIED-DIFFERENT → refuter upheld

*(No second lens ran on this row. What follows is its reader's text with the refuter's corrections; there is no separate options list.)*

**What it does.** Whether a woken job actually fires. Within a run it is the only thing that stops a wake from re-running work on unchanged inputs; across a resume it decides whether a second pass re-does everything.

**holo2** — An attempt's identity is its whole read-set bound to the version of every key in it; the durable spend ledger refuses a repeat at the same identity and `fold_refusal` requires at least one read key to have ADVANCED (and none to have regressed) before a re-fire is lawful. Because the ledger is journalled, a resumed session does not re-run what a previous session spent.

*Where:* `holo2/src/holo2/core/identity.py:70-130`; `holo2/src/holo2/engine/orchestrator.py:539-559`

**holo3** — One integer per bookmark: any touched subscription bumps `worker.version`, and a pop whose `tried_version == version` is skipped as stale. There is no durable spend memory at all — `--resume` folds a fact snapshot through the ordinary `_ingest` path, which mints fresh bookmarks with fresh versions, so a resumed run re-offers every job and is bounded only by the done-geometry, certified-tile and decision-cell refusals.

*Where:* `src/holo/receiver/descriptor.py:52-65`; `src/holo/receiver/engine.py:410-424`; `src/holo/receiver/engine.py:452-455`; `src/holo/receiver/engine.py:251-266`; `src/holo/receiver/snapshot.py:1-58` · *scheduler row / doc:* SCHEDULE.md:374-389 ('Kept, now declarative: … the tried-set floor (identical inputs never re-run)'); API.md §9a, the lean resume

**What actually differs.** Per-read-key versions with a monotone advance test and a durable ledger (holo2) vs one per-worker counter and no durable memory (holo3). Within a run the floors are equivalent in intent, but holo3's is coarser: any subscribed key moving re-arms the whole worker, where holo2 checks each key's version and refuses a re-fire in which nothing advanced. Across a resume they are opposite: holo2 would decline the spent work, holo3 deliberately re-offers it — which is what the e034 four-run chain is built on.

**Evidence.**

- (holo3) the e034 resume chain: 5543 -> 5866 -> 5866 -> 5880; run 3 is the control, +0 for 1,039 jobs / 4,193 cpu-s, still VERIFIED; run 4 adds the 4800 capture for +14 — *artefact:* ARTEFACTS.md:235
- (holo2) no artefact measures a holo2 resume that adds a capture; 'union on paper via an add-a-scan resume is unmeasured in BOTH trees' — *artefact:* ARTEFACTS.md:842 (G-32)

**Recommendation (reader).** keep holo3's. The re-offer-everything resume is the mechanism the deepest read on record used (+323 on run 2, +14 on run 4 after a fourth capture), and its cost is measured and modest (run 3's control paid 4,193 cpu-s for +0). Record the trade rather than porting the ledger: a durable spend memory would have made the e034 chain cheaper and the sheet-J resumes too, but it is the thing that would have to be built, not carried, since holo3 has no journal.

**Reader's notes.** SCHEDULE.md calls this 'the tried-set floor' in the v2-comparison section; the seed ledger uses the same words for a different mechanism (B15, the rival gate's 49 x (tried + offered) support floor), which belongs to the placement family. Both readings are legitimate; this row is the scheduling one.

**Refuter (upheld).** *Corrections:* Both sides verified: holo2/src/holo2/core/identity.py:73-79 (attempt_identity) + :108-129 (fold_refusal: no key may regress and at least one must advance) + orchestrator.py:552-556 (`ledger.is_spent` refusal); src/holo/receiver/descriptor.py:53-65 (one `version`/`tried_version` pair per bookmark), engine.py:410-424 (`_touch` bumps version on any touched key), :452-455 (the stale skip), :251-266 (`_replay` folds the snapshot through the ordinary `_ingest`). ONE ADDITION worth carrying: holo3's `attempts` is equally non-durable, so on every resume each escalating row's ordinal restarts at 0 and the six dear rows re-fire at their base price — the e034 four-run chain (ARTEFACTS.md:235) is four fresh escalation ladders, not one. That is part of the same trade the row recommends keeping, and it is not stated anywhere in the map.

*Reason:* Within-run and across-resume deltas both reproduced; the e034 numbers resolve exactly.

**Ruling:**

### pricing-19 — The escalation door — plugin-owned multiplicative re-pricing on six rows, and the colour lane's own fit ordinal

**HOLO3-ONLY** · evidence **MEASURED** · yield **decides-order-only** · classification trail: reader HOLO3-ONLY → refuter reclassified to HOLO3-ONLY → second lens HOLO3-ONLY

MECHANISM. Six of holo3's dearest rows price their own re-runs. Each time such a row is woken again on the same subject it charges a multiple of its base price, so a row that a growing certificate frontier keeps waking drifts behind cheaper work instead of being served over and over.

HOLO2. No analogue. holo2 has one uniform engine-side aging rule and no per-family pricing at all; a provider there cannot price its own re-runs.

HOLO3. The engine hands each row its fire count and its last price as data and interprets neither. Four rows guard the first fire so it charges the base price.

HOLO3. Two do not. The plane-field fit and the pooled colour context are dispatched at price 0 the first time - below survey's 10, the cheapest work in the receiver - instead of the 180 and 170 the ladder, the design note and the golden all advertise.

HOLO3. The escalation also bites one fire later than first reported: fires one and two both charge the base, and the third is the one that passes cold.

EVIDENCE. The zero price is in the archive, not only in the source. The churn line reports the mean price at which a row's jobs were dispatched, and those means match the unguarded sequence exactly.

EVIDENCE. Sheet I's first run: plane fields 6.0 fires per subject at mean price 452, the average of 0/180/360/540/720/900 plus the footing term (guarded it would read 482); pooled context 8.0 fires at mean 595, the average of 0 to 1190 in steps of 170.

EVIDENCE. Sheet I's second resume: '160 cpu-s/fire at price 2, 1.0 fires/subject' - a single 160-CPU-second fit dispatched at price 2. In the same J run the guarded swim row reads 530, its own sequence exactly, so guarded and unguarded rows are distinguishable inside one stored file.

EVIDENCE. The test that should have caught it is named 'what the row charges on its first fire' but passes an attempt count of 1, which is the second push.

EVIDENCE. Harm is bounded: a plane field is adopted only if it beats the standing one on certificate count, so a premature thin fit is always superseded and cannot poison later evidence - and every colour run we hold drains to an empty queue.

OPTION. Rule the zero deliberate and document it.

OPTION. Fix it - one line in each of two files, copying the guard the four sibling rows carry - and change the test to pass an attempt count of 0 so it tests what its name says.

OPTION. Fix and confirm on the colour simulation used for the wave-0 baseline: minutes of runtime, no real sheet needed.

RECOMMENDATION. Fix and confirm on the simulation. This is an omission, not a design: the row declares 180 and 170, prints them in the ladder, and charges 0.

RECOMMENDATION. Record two further facts the map does not carry: the fire count lives only in memory, so every resume restarts each escalating row at its base price (the four-run sheet-I chain is four fresh ladders), and the last-price argument is passed to every row and read by none.

**Ruling:**

---

### pricing-20 — What the re-read after a pose revision costs

**CARRIED-DIFFERENT** · evidence **UNPROVEN** · yield **decides-order-only** · classification trail: reader CARRIED-DIFFERENT → refuter upheld

*(No second lens ran on this row. What follows is its reader's text with the refuter's corrections; there is no separate options list.)*

**What it does.** When the re-sample of a window whose pose was just revised actually happens relative to everything else.

**holo2** — The re-read is its own priced class, TIER_RESAMPLE, deliberately outside the placement ladder and priced at 200 — RF1's own number, because it is RF1's other half. Charging it less would let it preempt the ladder the revision itself waited behind; charging it more would split one mechanism across two places in the order. It carries the ordinary lane term and is not gated.

*Where:* `holo2/src/holo2/solve/place_tiers.py:90-98`; `holo2/src/holo2/solve/place_tiers.py:179-197`

**holo3** — There is no re-read row and no re-read price. An adopted revision folds a Placement carrying the new affine, which bumps `pose_affine`; that wakes the window's `calibrate` worker, which re-samples at the ordinary calibration price of 15 + rung. The revised geometry is therefore read back near the front of the queue, not at the back.

*Where:* `src/holo/receiver/plugins/refine.py:512-514`; `src/holo/receiver/plugins/calibrate.py:730-732` · *scheduler row / doc:* SCHEDULE.md:107-124 (the refine debt rows: 'An adopted revision folds as a Placement with route refine, which re-fires the whole colour lane … through the ordinary declared reads — no bespoke wiring')

**What actually differs.** A dedicated 200-priced re-read tier (holo2) vs re-use of the ordinary calibrate row at 15 + rung (holo3). The delta is 185 cost points and a reversal of intent: holo2 deliberately keeps both halves of the refinement mechanism at the same place in the order, holo3 treats a revised pose as ordinary new geometry and re-reads it before almost everything else.

**Evidence.**

- (holo2) re-read census: 239/239 and 255/255 `resampled`, no refusals; the re-read population is 269/1,228 e029 and 272/1,282 xerox windows carrying two committed poses — *artefact:* ARTEFACTS.md:563 and :555
- (holo3) no artefact: refine adopted zero revisions on all five post-change volumes, so the re-read has never fired on a measured holo3 run — *artefact:* ARTEFACTS.md:528-531

**Recommendation (reader).** keep holo3's. Re-reading revised geometry early is defensible on its own terms (a corrected pose makes every tile it covers cheaper to decode), and the mechanism is currently unexercised, so there is nothing to measure. Record it so that if refinement ever starts adopting on paper, the price of its second half is a known, deliberate choice rather than an accident of re-use.

**Reader's notes.** The refinement family reader owns the mechanism (holo2's separate resample fire vs holo3's placement fold); this row covers only the price and is written to be merged with theirs.

**Refuter (upheld).** *Corrections:* None. Verified: holo2/src/holo2/solve/place_tiers.py:179-197 RESAMPLE_TIER at 200 with the 'RF1's own number' rationale, registered in TIER_PRICE at :199-201 and deliberately gated=False; holo3 has no re-read row — src/holo/receiver/plugins/refine.py:512-514 `revised_placement` folds a Placement with route=refine, which bumps pose_affine and wakes the window's calibrate worker at 15 + rung (src/holo/receiver/plugins/calibrate.py:730-732; SCHEDULE.md:117-124). The 185-point delta and the reversal of intent are stated correctly, and ARTEFACTS.md:555 and :563 resolve verbatim.

*Reason:* The dedicated 200-priced tier really is gone and the ordinary calibrate row really is what re-reads; UNPROVEN is honest given zero adoptions.

**Ruling:**

### extra-03 — Attaching a capture to a resumed run BY RECORD — its surveyed regions and footings without its pixels

**ABSENT** · evidence **UNPROVEN** · yield **decides-certificates** · classification trail: reader ABSENT → refuter upheld

*(No second lens ran on this row. What follows is its reader's text with the refuter's corrections; there is no separate options list.)*

**What it does.** Whether a resumed run can keep using the geometry of a capture whose image file is not handed to this invocation, and therefore whether the evidence and poses that cite that capture stay addressable.

**holo2** — A region record durably carries the survey's support grid (origin, step, extent) beside its mask blob, so the survey's own SupportMask, bbox and area can be rebuilt exactly from the store. On resume every capture the store knows and this invocation was NOT handed is rebuilt from records alone, with no file opened and nothing committed. A capture whose pixels ARE present is built by ingest instead and its fresh survey wins. A region whose parent capture record is missing is refused, not skipped, because that is a corrupt state.

*Where:* `holo2/src/holo2/capture/catalog.py:50-70`; `holo2/src/holo2/capture/catalog.py:73-90`; `holo2/src/holo2/capture/catalog.py:93-145`; `holo2/src/holo2/capture/catalog.py:18-22`

**holo3** — absent. holo3's resume folds a facts.npz whose first family is CaptureSurvey, and the survey fold looks its capture up in a store built from THIS invocation's declared scans. Dropping a capture from the declaration on a resume raises a bare KeyError, and the file says that was ruled knowingly on 2026-08-30: the operator re-declares every capture on every run, so no guard is built.

*Where:* `src/holo/receiver/snapshot.py:27-32`; `src/holo/receiver/snapshot.py:65-111`; `src/holo/receiver/engine.py:229-249` · *scheduler row / doc:* SCHEDULE.md:337-373 ('the life of a decode'); snapshot.py's own header

**Evidence.**

- (holo3) the e034 four-run resume chain re-declared its captures on every run and added the 4800 capture only at run 4 (+14 certificates); no run in the record resumed with fewer captures than it had — *artefact:* ARTEFACTS.md:235
- (holo1) a `--complete` resume of sheet I crashed at LOAD ('stream M is not declared'), state undamaged — the nearest thing in the record to a resume that could not re-open its own inputs — *artefact:* ARTEFACTS.md:224

**Recommendation (reader).** keep holo3's — but record the consequence. The two designs need different things: holo2 stores evidence durably and therefore must be able to name a capture without its pixels; holo3 stores only decisions and regenerates evidence from the rasters, so it needs every capture file on every resume by construction. The loss is real but narrow: a holo3 resume cannot proceed at all if one of its multi-gigabyte TIFFs has moved, where holo2 would carry on with the rest. Accept it, and say so in the resume documentation.

**Reader's notes.** Not a yield loss on any archived volume — every recorded resume had all its captures. It becomes a loss the first time an archive outlives its scans, which is exactly the deep-time case docs/BOOTSTRAP-DESIGN.md exists for.

**Refuter (upheld).** *Corrections:* Add the code that makes the KeyError claim checkable rather than quoting the docstring for it: src/holo/receiver/store.py:543 `capture = self._captures[fact.capture_id]` inside _fold_survey is the raise; snapshot.py:27-32 only records that it was ruled. Principle 1 says the docstring ruling is context, not the verdict — the verdict is that line. holo2 side verified in full at catalog.py:50-70 (region_from_record), :73-90, :93-145 (the orphan-region refusal at :130-134) and the fresh-survey-wins rule at :18-22.

*Reason:* ABSENT is correct and both sides' citations are exact; only the evidence for the crash should come from code, not from the ruling comment.

**Ruling:**

### extra-05 — Capture identity, the refusals at ingest, and re-authenticating the source file before anything durable is written

**CARRIED-DIFFERENT** · evidence **UNPROVEN** · yield **integrity** · classification trail: reader CARRIED-DIFFERENT → refuter upheld

*(No second lens ran on this row. What follows is its reader's text with the refuter's corrections; there is no separate options list.)*

**What it does.** Which acquisitions count as one capture (and therefore what a cross-capture sum is entitled to sum), and whether a source file that changed mid-run can poison committed evidence.

**holo2** — The SHA-256 of the encoded source bytes IS the capture identity, and the docstring states the consequence: re-encoded, resized or cropped copies have different byte identities but are correlated evidence and must not be submitted as independent acquisitions. Ingest refuses a multi-page TIFF, a 4-channel image, a non-real dtype, non-finite pixels, an unreadable PIL mode, and a file whose stat tuple moves while it is being opened. Before any durable retention the raster re-authenticates its path: a matching (dev, ino, size, mtime_ns) memo skips, any difference escalates to a full byte re-stream and SHA compare.

*Where:* `holo2/src/holo2/capture/scan.py:352-396`; `holo2/src/holo2/capture/scan.py:36-71`; `holo2/src/holo2/capture/scan.py:221-264`; `holo2/src/holo2/capture/scan.py:286-313`

**holo3** — The capture id is the same SHA-256 of the file bytes. holo3 adds the Q2 refusal — the same source declared at two DPIs in one run is refused by name, because a corrected DPI renames every lane the evidence cites. It drops the rest: no dtype, channel-count, finiteness or multi-page checks, no open-time stat comparison, and no re-authentication before writing, because holo3 copies every plane into its own run-dir memmaps at intake and never reads the operator's file again.

*Where:* `src/holo/receiver/plugins/intake.py:35-39`; `src/holo/receiver/plugins/intake.py:42-62`; `src/holo/receiver/plugins/intake.py:172-207`; `src/holo/receiver/plugins/intake.py:213-241` · *scheduler row / doc:* SCHEDULE.md:339-341; intake is not a scheduled row

**What actually differs.** Same identity function, different guard set and a different reason for the guards. holo2 keeps reading the operator's file for the whole run and therefore has to re-authenticate it; holo3 copies the pixels once into uint8 memmaps under the run directory and reads only those, which makes the source-changed hazard structurally unreachable after intake. What holo3 genuinely drops is the input-validation set: a 16-bit or 4-channel or multi-page TIFF that holo2 refuses by name is silently cast into uint8 planes by `target[y0:y1] = source`.

**Evidence.**

- (v1) the multi-scan law: never double-count the same physical capture (raw plus its decimation) in one state; one ordinary state freezes one DPI — *artefact:* CLAUDE.md channel-facts list; e023b physical-evidence invariant
- (holo3) no artefact — no run in the record used a 16-bit, 4-channel or multi-page capture. The three archived TIFFs whose headers I read directly are uint8 (a header read, not a decode, and not an ARTEFACTS.md row) — *artefact:* no artefact

**Recommendation (reader).** port now, cheaply: a handful of refusals at holo3's `load_native` (dtype real-numeric, channels in {1,3}, one TIFF page, and a named refusal rather than a silent cast for anything wider than uint8). It is a few lines, it costs nothing at run time, and the failure it prevents is silent — a 16-bit scan currently becomes garbage pixels rather than an error, and the receiver would report it as a hard sheet.

**Reader's notes.** The settling run is not a decode: open one 16-bit TIFF through holo3's intake and compare a plane against the source (seconds). No such capture exists in the corpus today, so this is a latent capability loss, not a measured one.

**Refuter (upheld).** *Corrections:* Strengthen one clause: a multi-page TIFF is not merely unrefused, it is silently READ AS PAGE 0 — src/holo/receiver/plugins/intake.py:43-47 `tiff.pages[0].asarray(...)` — so the failure is a wrong capture, not a cast. Add that a 4-channel array survives load_native (only a singleton third axis is squeezed at :61-62), is labelled channels=3 at :174, and has its fourth channel silently dropped by native_plane_names(3). All four holo3 citations verified, including the Q2 refusal at :227-233 and the absence of any dtype/finiteness/stat guard; holo2's refusals verified at scan.py:88-111 and the re-authentication at :221-264.

*Reason:* Every claim reproduces at HEAD; the recommendation to port a handful of refusals stands and the silent-failure argument is if anything understated.

**Ruling:**

### extra-16 — The address-word contradiction as a durable fact — a legally decoded word that names a tile the geometry says is somewhere else

**ABSENT** · evidence **MEASURED** · yield **integrity** · classification trail: reader ABSENT → refuter upheld

*(No second lens ran on this row. What follows is its reader's text with the refuter's corrections; there is no separate options list.)*

**What it does.** Whether the receiver has any way to notice that two placements disagree about what is printed at one position, and what the scope of that disagreement is.

**holo2** — When a word decodes legally — inside the BCH radius with five clean zero pads — but names a counter different from what the standing placements at that position claim, the receiver mints a fact keyed on the contradicted position at one footing. It carries BOTH sides' full contributor sets: the placements whose reads produced the decode, and the placements already asserting that position. Both sides are condemned identically and neither is voted on. The fact is complete at mint and frozen: a later write that revised it would be out-voting the containment after the fact.

*Where:* `holo2/src/holo2/solve/contradiction.py:33-49`; `holo2/src/holo2/solve/contradiction.py:52-126`; `holo2/src/holo2/solve/contradiction.py:128-163`; `holo2/src/holo2/solve/quarantine.py:171-194`

**holo3** — absent. holo3 has integrity checks for two disagreeing CERTIFICATES and for opposite metadata bits, but nothing that compares a decoded address word against the addresses standing placements already assert, and no fact to record such a disagreement.

*Where:* _(no path)_ · *scheduler row / doc:* no descriptor row; the term does not occur in holo3's production code

**Evidence.**

- (holo1) the expected silence: 70/70 regions, all 925 pilot tiles, ZERO contradictory reads across ~1,780 attempted known-position decodes — *artefact:* ARTEFACTS.md:444
- (holo2) quarantine cost about +2% of the verdicts wall (~1.7 s); yield zero contradictions on every healthy fixture — *artefact:* the seed ledger's holo2-only machinery table, row 'Quarantine (PL7)'
- (both) zero wrong certificates on either side at either platen angle in the holo2-vs-holo3 head-to-head, and zero wrong across 285 targets in five crop batteries — *artefact:* ARTEFACTS.md:430-432, :316-317

**Recommendation (reader).** accept the loss, and record the reason. The mechanism has never fired on healthy material in either tree's whole measured record, it cost holo2 ~2% of its wall, and the certificate layer is proof-carrying — a wrong pose fails CRC rather than producing a wrong certificate, which is what the zero-wrong audits actually test. What is genuinely lost is the diagnostic: on a sheet where addressing goes wrong (the hp c2-cmy7 refusal, 47 word matches on a 60.3% footprint) holo2 would have a named fact and holo3 has only counters. If a cheap version is ever wanted, PAGE-DOMAIN.md:194-197 already parks the adjacent idea (a CRC refuse on a certified-looking placement retrying the search's runner-up) and says it reopens with quarantine tooling.

**Reader's notes.** Three consumer-side rows already exist (placement-23, evidence-ownership-15, done-gate-08, all ABSENT). This row is the PRODUCER: without it none of those consumers has anything to consume, so the four should be ruled together as one line rather than separately.

**Refuter (upheld).** *Corrections:* Fill the empty holo3 `where` with the tree's own admission: src/holo/receiver/docs/DIAG-DESIGN.md:30-31 and :153 reserve deep red for contradiction and say it is 'never painted without a contradiction producer' — holo3 stating in its own living doc that the producer is absent, which is better evidence than a grep. Second, source the cost number properly: '+2% of the verdicts wall (~1.7 s)' is at holo2/docs/MEASUREMENTS.md:1345 ('W5 quarantine ... 39, wall 79.0 s (the wave costs ~1.7 s against a bypassed baseline, +2%)'), not 'the seed ledger'. Verified as stated: holo2/src/holo2/solve/contradiction.py:33-49 keys the fact on the contradicted position at one footing, :52-126 carries both sides' contributor sets, :128-141 freezes it, :144-155 declares both sides as references; grep over holo3's production tree finds no contradiction producer. ARTEFACTS.md:444 reproduces the 70/70 regions, 925 tiles, ~1,780 reads exactly.

*Reason:* ABSENT is correct on current code, the recommendation is proportionate, and the join with placement-23 / evidence-ownership-15 / done-gate-08 is the right handling.

**Ruling:**

### extra-17 — Whether a resume is allowed to reuse durable facts written under a different policy, price table, provider set, format row or page domain

**ABSENT** · evidence **UNPROVEN** · yield **integrity** · classification trail: reader ABSENT → refuter upheld

*(No second lens ran on this row. What follows is its reader's text with the refuter's corrections; there is no separate options list.)*

**What it does.** Whether stale facts from an earlier build silently join a new run's evidence, or the run refuses to open.

**holo2** — The session identity is the envelope header: format row id, receiver policy id, the recipe's entries AS DATA (task classes, footing selectors, gates, prices, arms), the ordered provider ids, and the declared page domain. The store compares the whole value on resume, so a moved gate, a re-ordered provider binding or a changed price is a refused open — loud and un-migrated, never a quiet reinterpretation. The policy id itself was forked once, on purpose, when the placement family changed, which made every state directory under the old name refuse to open.

*Where:* `holo2/src/holo2/engine/records.py:1-25`; `holo2/src/holo2/engine/records.py:29-45`; `holo2/src/holo2/engine/session.py:41-56`; `holo2/src/holo2/engine/session.py:203-248`

**holo3** — absent, and stated as such: 'there is no version stamp, no format guard and no integrity check — a snapshot is an operator's own run product, and the operator is trusted with it.' `_replay` folds every fact through the ordinary `_ingest` with no comparison against the run's declared row, price table or bound.

*Where:* `src/holo/receiver/snapshot.py:19-25`; `src/holo/receiver/snapshot.py:166-179`; `src/holo/receiver/engine.py:248-266` · *scheduler row / doc:* conversion_docs/ARMS-2026-08-31.md:162-173 and RULINGS:41-48 (the lean resume); API §9a

**Evidence.**

- (holo3) the e034 four-run resume chain: 5,543 -> 5,866 -> 5,866 -> 5,880, close VERIFIED byte-exact three times; run 3 was a +0 control — *artefact:* ARTEFACTS.md:235
- (holo3) PAGE-DOMAIN.md's own supersession table records that the page bound is 'a per-run admission filter — no resume guard, no stored policy', deliberately — *artefact:* src/holo/receiver/docs/PAGE-DOMAIN.md:36-37, :112
- (holo1) the adjacent failure the record does hold: an off-catalog colour resume died at 'stream M is not declared' because a row id only meant something if a table in that build still held the row — *artefact:* ARTEFACTS.md:224

**Recommendation (reader).** port a minimal form now: stamp the format row id and the tree describe string into facts.npz and refuse (by name) a resume whose declared row differs. That is two fields and one comparison; it costs nothing and it closes the one failure mode that would be invisible — folding another sheet's certificates into this run and closing on them. Do NOT port the full holo2 identity (recipe entries and provider ids by value): holo3's arms wave moved eight rows in 24 hours, and a by-value policy comparison would have invalidated every archived facts.npz each time, which is precisely the cost the lean-resume ruling was avoiding.

**Reader's notes.** The risk is asymmetric: a mismatched resume cannot produce a WRONG certificate (certificates are proof-carrying and the close verifies a SHA), but it can waste a long run, and a facts.npz from a different tile matrix would fold certificates at coordinates this row does not have. No artefact records such an incident on holo3.

**Refuter (upheld).** *Corrections:* One citation fix: the supersession row that records 'no resume guard' is src/holo/receiver/docs/PAGE-DOMAIN.md:111 ('C-8 | no resume | untouched; the bound is per-run'); :112 is C-2 about memmaps. The :36-37 quote is exact. Verified: snapshot.py:19-25 states the no-guard position, :166-179 is a bare npz write/read with no stamp of any kind, and engine.py:251-266 folds every fact through the ordinary _ingest with no comparison against the declared row. The recommendation (stamp the row id + tree describe, refuse by name, do NOT port the by-value recipe comparison) is well-aimed: the by-value half is exactly what holo2's session identity does at engine/records.py:29-45 and it is what would have invalidated every archived facts.npz during the arms wave.

*Reason:* ABSENT is correct on current code and the minimal-port recommendation is the cheapest thing that closes the one invisible failure.

**Ruling:**

### extra-18 — Not re-reading a capture a lineage has already read — the ingest attempt as a spendable, durable fact

**CARRIED-DIFFERENT** · evidence **UNPROVEN** · yield **decides-order-only** · classification trail: reader CARRIED-DIFFERENT → refuter upheld

*(No second lens ran on this row. What follows is its reader's text with the refuter's corrections; there is no separate options list.)*

**What it does.** Whether a resumed run re-opens, re-hashes, re-surveys and re-converts every declared capture before it can do any new work.

**holo2** — One spendable attempt per declared scan, whose identity is the resolved path plus the operator's DPI, so the ledger's spend row is the durable statement 'this lineage has already read these bytes at this DPI'. The frontier mints only the FIRST declared scan whose capture record does not stand, so scan N+1 becomes expandable only when scan N has landed — one survey in flight whatever the pool width — and a resume finds nothing to mint at all because the records already stand.

*Where:* `holo2/src/holo2/solve/ingest.py:54-99`; `holo2/src/holo2/solve/ingest.py:101-128`; `holo2/src/holo2/solve/ingest.py:1-42`

**holo3** — Every declared capture is re-hashed, re-decoded and re-written as a fresh ladder of per-plane uint8 memmaps in `_start`, before the frontier exists and before the snapshot is folded — on every run, including every resume. There is no memo, no record of a previous conversion and no way to say 'this one is already prepared'.

*Where:* `src/holo/receiver/engine.py:102-109`; `src/holo/receiver/engine.py:229-247`; `src/holo/receiver/plugins/intake.py:161-241` · *scheduler row / doc:* SCHEDULE.md:339-341; intake is not a descriptor row

**What actually differs.** holo2 makes ingest durable work with an identity and a spend row, so a resume skips it entirely; holo3 makes it an unconditional constructor step. What that costs is a full SHA-256 pass plus a decode plus the whole decimation ladder per capture per run — v1 measured one SHA-256 pass over a 6.8 GB TIFF at about 19.1 s, and holo3 additionally writes every level of every plane. On the e034 chain that conversion ran four times over the same three (then four) captures.

**Evidence.**

- (holo1) the phased-schedule residual traced to re-authentication: the whole +169 s residual is eight extra source re-authentications, one SHA-256 pass over a 6.8 GB TIFF costing about 19.1 s — *artefact:* ARTEFACTS.md:715
- (holo3) no artefact — intake is not a plugin row, so its cost appears in no report.json plugin table; the four-run sheet-I chain's per-run walls are recorded but not decomposed — *artefact:* ARTEFACTS.md:235

**Recommendation (reader).** keep holo3's, and measure once if resume latency ever bites. The costed measurement is cheap and is not a receiver run: time `intake.ingest_captures` alone on the sheet-I three-capture set (one process, minutes) and compare against the recorded resume walls. Note that holo3's design makes the skip harder than holo2's — holo3's derived planes live in the run directory, so a skip needs the plane files to persist and be re-validated, which is a real feature, not a flag.

**Reader's notes.** pricing-04 already covers the OVERLAP consequence of the same difference (holo2 interleaves conversion with decoding, holo3 does not). This row is the RESUME consequence, which pricing-04 does not state: holo2 pays the conversion once per lineage, holo3 once per invocation.

**Refuter (upheld).** *Corrections:* None material. holo2's one-at-a-time frontier verified verbatim at solve/ingest.py:77-99 ('The FIRST declared scan whose capture record does not stand'), and the spend identity at :101-128. holo3 side verified: _start calls _intake_run BEFORE the frontier exists and before _replay (engine.py:229-249), ingest_captures re-hashes every declared path (intake.py:224-225) and ingest_capture rewrites every plane with open_memmap mode='w+' (:134-137) — no existence test, no memo, and the run directory differs per run so the files cannot be reused. The row correctly declines to promise a number where none exists.

*Reason:* Both sides reproduce exactly; the recommendation to keep holo3's and measure only if latency bites is right, and the measurement named is genuinely not a receiver run.

**Ruling:**

### extra-20 — Whether a window that has been retired can ever come back

**DIVERGED** · evidence **MEASURED** · yield **decides-order-only** · classification trail: reader DIVERGED → refuter upheld

*(No second lens ran on this row. What follows is its reader's text with the refuter's corrections; there is no separate options list.)*

**What it does.** Whether a window the scheduler gave up on can be re-armed later by new evidence.

**holo2** — A window record whose status is RETIRED is terminal at the commit wall: any write that changes it raises StateRegression, naming the retirement reason. Retirement is therefore a durable, irreversible statement about that window, and the only way back into the frontier is a different window.

*Where:* `holo2/src/holo2/solve/families.py:84-107`; `holo2/src/holo2/solve/records.py:228-244`

**holo3** — Retirement is a scheduling state, not a record, and it is reversible: `_afterlife` un-retires a parent window when all four of its children have settled without placing, restoring its subscriptions and re-pushing it with cold armed. The retire predicate is re-asked at every pop, so a window whose retire cause stops holding simply stops retiring.

*Where:* `src/holo/receiver/engine.py:360-385`; `src/holo/receiver/engine.py:535-562`; `src/holo/receiver/engine.py:564-576` · *scheduler row / doc:* SCHEDULE.md:9-10 (retire is asked at each pop); PAGE-DOMAIN.md:143-145 (small-support windows wait for a word to open a page and re-arm via the live-pages wake)

**What actually differs.** holo2 makes retirement a durable terminal fact (an appeal is unrepresentable); holo3 makes it a revocable scheduler bit and revokes it in exactly one place, the parent's cold afterlife. The direction is towards holo3 doing MORE work, not less, so this is not a yield loss; what changes is that holo3 has no durable record of a window having been given up on, so a census of retirement causes exists only in the event stream of the run that produced it.

**Evidence.**

- (holo3) retire causes are tallied per cause and emitted as WindowRetired events with the cause named; the counter appears in EngineStat — *artefact:* src/holo/receiver/engine.py:360-377, :278-296
- (holo2 vs holo3) windows retired in the head-to-head: holo2 0 / holo3 4 at 0.00 degrees, 0 / 0 at 0.80; unresolved windows LEFT 196/155 and 397/281 — *artefact:* ARTEFACTS.md:415-421

**Recommendation (reader).** keep holo3's

**Reader's notes.** placement-29 covers which refusals remove a window from the frontier and is already DIVERGED; this row adds only the durability question (a record versus a bit) and the one un-retirement path, which placement-08 covers on the cold side.

**Refuter (upheld).** *Corrections:* SCHEMA: a DIVERGED row with ruling_needed:false is not a state claude-plan.md §7 allows — every DIVERGED row gets a RULINGS.md entry. Set ruling_needed true and carry the existing recommendation ('keep holo3's') into it; the classification word itself is right. Mechanism verified exactly: src/holo/receiver/engine.py:535-562 un-retires the parent — afterlife_used, cold_armed, dormant_since=None, retired=False, and the bookmark's own retired flag cleared with _retired_count DECREMENTED at :558-561 and its subscriptions re-registered — and _place_repush at :564-575 is the one caller. holo2's terminal law verified at families.py:84-107 (StateRegression naming the retirement reason). ARTEFACTS.md:415-422 gives 0/4 retired and 196/155, 397/281 unresolved exactly as quoted.

*Reason:* Behaviour difference is real and correctly described; only the ruling flag contradicts the row's own classification.

**Ruling:**

## Not raised as rulings, but UNPROVEN

`claude-plan.md` §7 gives a ruling entry to every DIVERGED, ABSENT or UNPROVEN row. These rows are CARRIED-DIFFERENT with UNPROVEN equivalence evidence, and their own reader judged that no ruling was needed. They are listed rather than written out, so the omission is visible and any of them can be pulled into the set above on request.

| row | mechanism | refuter | reader's recommendation |
|---|---|---|---|
| `placement-15` | R5 lattice-seed reduction — one reciprocal lattice seed per (capture, level, region), from the best-supported source | upheld | keep holo3's — the fold-time single writer removes the failure mode the key binding existed to prevent, and the order is the same total order. |
| `placement-16` | The seed wake — a pass that superseded the lattice seed wakes the region's dormant windows | upheld | keep holo3's — same coverage, declared rather than pushed. |
| `placement-18` | The claim/settle flag protocol — commit-then-ping, test-and-clear-then-consult, single ownership | upheld | _(none given)_ |
| `close-04` | The count gate — the cheap necessary condition that licenses a full close attempt | reclassified; UNPROVEN is the merge's correction of the reader's PINNED | keep holo3's. |
| `extra-02` | What happens to work a producer offers but cannot do yet — a permanent refusal, a refusal that sleeps on a named wake key, or no vocabulary at all | upheld | _(none given)_ |
| `extra-06` | The structure-tensor score that finds where the printed field is on a platen (E021 region survey, the scoring leaf) | reclassified; UNPROVEN is the merge's correction of the reader's PINNED | _(none given)_ |
| `extra-07` | Measuring the printed cell pitch from a crop — the gradient-comb spectrum, the probe ladder, and the largest-harmonic-consistent selection rule | reclassified; UNPROVEN is the merge's correction of the reader's PINNED | _(none given)_ |
| `extra-15` | How close two fitted geometries have to be before they count as the same pose | rejected | _(none given)_ |

Three of these carry a deadline rather than a ruling, and the deadline is Phase 3. `extra-06` and `extra-07` are the survey and pitch leaves: their refuter found that the pin the reader named is a shared-CONSTANT gate that would pass unchanged if the arithmetic were rewritten, and that the oracle it cited pins holo2 against `holo.receive`, not against holo3. Its words: one array oracle comparing the two leaves on a fixture crop is seconds to run and "MUST be written before Phase 3 deletes holo2, or this carry becomes permanently unverifiable". That belongs in the Phase-2 suite-preparation package (`claude-plan.md` §8 step 2.3), not in a ruling. `extra-15` is the third: its refuter REJECTED the row's holo3 column outright (holo3 keeps one standing placement per window and supersedes it; it does not accumulate two), so the row must be rewritten before anyone rules on it.
